# Rust Fidelity Audit

Status: in progress.  This document is the gate for production fidelity
changes. `vendor/pokecrystal` is the only behavior authority; TypeScript and
existing Rust tests are implementation evidence only.

## Method

- Scope: production Rust under `rust/crates`; test-only references are listed
  only when they demonstrate a production path or a test fixture can escape.
- Search inventory: `TODO|FIXME|HACK|stub|dummy|placeholder|fake|synthetic|
  approximate|simplified|fallback|legacy|default|noop|unsupported|temporary|
  unwrap_or|unwrap_or_default` plus hard-coded gameplay identifiers and Bevy
  input handlers.
- ASM locations below refer to the clean local checkout at `vendor/pokecrystal`.
- “No source evidence” is a blocking classification, not permission to retain
  the behavior.

## Initial production findings

- All 20 exact `QueueScript` targets in `engine/events/overworld.asm` are now exported as canonical global script roots instead of depending on test injection or incidental reachability. Definition-only CPU closures for Fly and Headbutt presentation stay out of the script cursor catalog and are source-fingerprint certified. The queued Surf path materializes its live-facing `wMovementBuffer`, Waterfall samples live `wPlayerTileCollision`, and `TreeMonEncounter` runs through an exact source-certified, DIV-traced mutation that writes `wTempWildMonSpecies`, `wCurPartyLevel`, `wBattleType`, and `wScriptVar` before the script's ordinary `randomwildmon`/`startbattle` continuation. `text_asm` is treated as the static-text/embedded-code boundary rather than parsed as text bytecode.
- Cry PCM rendering now follows the cartridge parameter boundary: it accepts `wCryPitch`/`wCryLength`, applies the 16-bit pitch addition to raw and symbolic tonal frequencies, replaces tempo only for non-noise channels, and preserves noise timing. The core exporter renders normal, Growl (`length + $c0`), and Roar (`length + $40`) PCM for every species, and Rust selects those exact species assets from `anim_cry` selectors 2/3, 0, and 1 respectively. Ordinary species cries also use the species-rendered normal asset instead of the unmodified shared base cry.
- Used move-effect command routing is now checked exhaustively against the source pointer table and compiled effect bodies for `checkhit`, `effectchance`, and `kingsrock`. The comparison includes Bide's release-only shared hit gate and PoisonMultiHit's per-hit chance sampler as explicit control-flow placements. King's Rock now follows the exact scripts that contain `kingsrock`; built-in flinch scripts such as Headbutt no longer receive a second held-item flinch roll.
- Battle animation packs and the visible timeline compiler now accept only the 36 opcodes in Crystal's `data/moves/animations.asm` command language (plus local labels). Rust previously validated commands as arbitrary nonempty strings and silently treated unknown opcodes as no-ops, allowing exporter drift or malformed packs to produce incomplete but plausible animations. Catalog and deserialization regressions reject unknown commands before presentation.
- Intro sprite-animation input now crosses a typed, fail-closed boundary shared by intro and field-move OAM consumers. Missing piece coordinates/tiles/attributes, missing frames or OAM targets, unknown frame commands, invalid frame flags, truncated 32×32 tile/attribute maps, missing palette banks, and out-of-range graphic tiles are errors instead of zero defaults, skipped sprites, palette-first substitution, short-map acceptance, or modulo wrapping. Pack-owned sprite initialization, local-label control flow, callback instructions, and framesets now drive the live runner, including the `wSlotsDelay`/`wIntroSceneFrameCounter` WRAM alias that switches Unown F at `$40`. The remaining intro gap is resource-transfer/scene-program execution and ROM checkpoint parity, not permissive asset or callback interpretation.
- The live intro sprite runner now consumes object frameset/function bindings and every frame/wait/restart/end/delete step directly from the validated pack-owned ASM sprite bundle. The duplicate Rust frameset table is deleted; it had replaced `SPRITE_ANIM_FRAMESET_INTRO_UNOWN_F`'s source `wait 0; end` sequence with an invented one-frame wait. Missing framesets, oversized durations, unknown animation functions, and unresolved graphic ownership fail the visible sequence instead of freezing, silently skipping a sprite, or selecting art by object name. Each allocation's graphic and tile base are derived from the exported VRAM transfer state. The outer scene scheduler remains part of the larger presentation-program replacement.
- Memory Game board initialization and pair accounting now follow `MemoryGame_InitBoard`/`MemoryGame_CheckMatch` rather than constructing and double-shuffling an invented 16-card species deck. Rust allocates the exact 9×5 board, consumes one of the three source count rows in placement-call order (`2,8,4,7,3,6,1`), rejection-samples `Random & $3f`, and fills the remaining cells with card 5 without another RNG call. Its live board, jumptable phase, counter, tries, five-byte match history, matched count, selected-card registers, cursor index, and last-drawn card now reside in validated typed core state; typed frame/joypad input replaces every `memory_*` string register and host card-index command. Each round performs 45 card-placement executions before spawning the cursor with five tries; `CheckTriesRemaining` decrements before the first-card loop, the sprite callback writes its one-based A-button choice after the jumptable for next-frame consumption, bounded 9×5 movement matches the source cursor, and B is ignored during play. Invalid choices remain latched in their distinct source phases. PickCard2's fallthrough decrement is followed by 63 more counter frames and a separate zero-counter resolution execution. Matches write `$ff` into both board cells, append the card id to the match history, increase the matched-card count by two, and retain the source last-card value; misses restore the face-down value. Zero tries enters RevealAll, whose first A reveals the board and whose blocking acknowledgement accepts A or B. Both apparent menu calls use the empty `UnusedCursor_InterpretJoypad_AnimateCursor` stub with carry clear, so ResetBoard always initializes and AskPlayAgain always loops to RestartGame; the invented proceed/restart/quit paths are removed. The obsolete parallel `memory_revealed` board and pair-at-once resolution shortcut are removed. Remaining gaps are presentation-owned tile rendering and text/audio timing.
- Card Flip now uses validated typed core state and inputs for its exact 24-byte deck, face-indexed discard pile, source phase, played-card counter, selection registers, face-up card, and finite payout loop. Entry preserves the two distinct source failures around its three-coin stake: the shared `CheckCoinsAndCoinCase` wrapper rejects exactly zero coins before checking the Coin Case or consuming shuffle RNG; once entered, one or two coins still reach `_CardFlip`, shuffle the deck, and then produce the game-local `Not enough coins…` result. The wrapper is not rerun between an accepted stake and that round's reveal/payout phases, so spending the last three coins does not abort the already-started game. `wCardFlipNumCardsPlayed` advances only when Play Again is accepted, and its twelfth increment reshuffles before the next stake, matching the ASM control flow.
- Event-flag integration coverage now preserves Crystal's loaded-object roster boundary. Ordinary `setevent` changes whether an object is admitted on the next map load; it does not despawn the already-loaded object. That temporary retention is now kept separate from persistent `appear`/`disappear` overrides, so it cannot leak through map memory and suppress the correct replacement object on re-entry. Immediate removal remains owned by source commands such as item-ball collection/`disappear`. Focused core coverage and the visible Mom story check both the live roster and the reloaded map.
- Every accepted battle-animation opcode now requires its exact ASM argument count. The interpreter's non-source `playsound`, `anim_minimizeopp`, and six-argument `anim_obj` compatibility paths were removed; source `anim_obj` is exactly object id, X, Y, and parameter. Malformed waits, sounds, objects, branches, and zero-argument commands therefore fail pack verification instead of being skipped, partially interpreted, or assigned invented coordinates.
- Shared `BattleAnimSub_*` routines are now exported as independently addressable animation sections, so the visible interpreter's `anim_call` resolves the same global targets as ASM. Local declarations are normalized from source `.Label:` syntax to the interpreter's `.Label` keys. The exporter previously recognized only `BattleAnim_*` labels, embedded every shared subroutine under the preceding move, preserved colons on all local declarations, and consequently left every shared call and real local branch unresolved. Export and Rust pack verification now reject any missing local/global control-flow target; the complete source inventory resolves 298 sections, including 16 shared subroutines, with zero missing targets. `BattleAnim_ReturnMon` preserves its unique nonempty fallthrough by appending `BattleAnimSub_Return` after its sound command; the five empty-label fallthrough aliases retain their copied targets.
- `anim_loop` now reproduces `BattleAnimCmd_Loop`'s exact count: `anim_loop 3` yields three total body passes, `anim_loop 1` does not jump, and zero remains perpetual. Rust previously jumped once on initialization and then counted down from `N - 1`, producing `N + 1` body passes and extra objects, sounds, waits, and RNG-independent frames in every finite animation loop.
- The visible animation byte parser now resolves the four item constants that occur in numeric command positions (`NO_ITEM`, `MASTER_BALL`, `ULTRA_BALL`, and `GREAT_BALL`) to their exact `constants/item_constants.asm` values. Capture animations previously passed pack verification but aborted timeline compilation at their symbolic `anim_if_param_equal` branch.
- `anim_resetobp0` now writes Crystal's exact non-SGB `$e0` palette byte instead of the invented `$e4`; the command's `$f0` Super Game Boy branch is retained in the isolated hardware-value authority, while the Rust runtime correctly selects non-SGB because it exposes no SGB execution mode. A focused Bevy regression covers both source values.
- The retained Master Ball animation now includes the complete source pre-shake sequence. Rust previously placed the Master Ball sound/sparkles at frame 76 instead of 92, collapsed and hid the enemy from frames 68/76 instead of 156/164, played the post-return sounds 16 frames early, and reached every wobble check and terminal branch 16 frames early. The corrected source boundaries are sparkle/special sound 92, `.Shake` entry 156, hidden target 164, change-mode sound 180, bounce sound 212, loop setup 316, and first `anim_checkpokeball` 364; a focused Bevy regression locks the full successful four-check total at 508.
- Fishing and Headbutt now preserve canonical `BATTLETYPE_FISH` and `BATTLETYPE_TREE` instead of entering as ordinary wild battles. The field-encounter exporter parses `data/wild/treemons_asleep.asm` and `TREEMON_SLEEP_TURNS` directly into required per-entry, per-time sleep counters; a selected night tree encounter such as Caterpie enters with seven turns of Sleep while its daytime counterpart remains awake. Rock Smash rejects tree-sleep metadata. Focused exporter, schema/catalog, and integrated battle-state regressions pass.
- Multi-step scripted leader movement now carries the follower's exact pre-command tile/facing into presentation and models the ASM movement queue: the initial ordinary step approaches the leader with X-axis precedence, each eligible leader command is enqueued one step behind, and the follower retains the queued command's stride, direction, and duration. Core authority and retained rendering consume the same lagged path instead of snapping the follower to the leader's final penultimate tile. This matches TypeScript's follower queue and ASM `QueueFollowerFirstStep`/`ApplyMovementToFollower`. Final runtime validation remains deferred by request.
- Visible battle animation objects now require every exporter-owned function, flags, fixed-Y coordinate, frame duration/flip, OAM tile offset, piece coordinate/flip/tile id, base offset, and palette field. Rust previously substituted null behavior, zero coordinates, tile 0, and disabled flips for malformed bundle data, producing plausible but source-wrong moves. The source-defined JSON `null` function remains the exact `BATTLE_ANIM_FUNC_NULL` representation used by TypeScript's exporter. Final visual validation remains deferred by request.
- Battle selection cursors are now the static solid `▶` used by TypeScript's menu snapshots and Crystal's ordinary menu cursor. Rust previously invented a global 16-frame solid/hollow blink for every battle surface by conflating ASM `PlaceHollowCursor`—a specific party-action acknowledgement write—with ongoing cursor rendering. Final visual validation remains deferred by request.
- The bitmap renderer now requires the complete ASM-loaded `font_battle_extra`, `font_extra`, up-arrow, and phone-icon banks. Rust previously treated every bank as optional and silently left unrelated base-sheet tiles installed, corrupting battle Lv text, arrows/cursors, phone glyphs, and extended punctuation while still presenting the screen. Final text/battle visual validation remains deferred by request.
- A zero-HP battler now remains drawable only while its side's started `BattleAnim_FaintMon` is active. Retained event state commits HP zero at the faint boundary; Rust's shared sprite guard previously hid the Pokémon immediately, so the ASM/TypeScript downward faint scroll and `SFX_FAINT` played over empty space. Once the animation drains, the ordinary zero-HP guard hides it. Final battle visual validation remains deferred by request.
- Battle animation object slots now run their lifetime/function deletion check at the actual rendered frame, not only at intervening command timestamps. Rust's replay reconstruction otherwise left finite sprites visible after their source lifetime whenever no later object command happened to trigger cleanup; TypeScript and Crystal update all animation structs every frame. Final move-animation visual validation remains deferred by request.
- Trainer battle art now retains the exported `champion` sprite stem. Rust rewrote `CHAMPION` to nonexistent `gfx/trainers/lance.*`, while both TypeScript normalization and the ASM-derived assets use `gfx/trainers/champion.*`; Champion entry/exit presentation therefore could not load. The necessary source-name exception for `POKEMON_PROF` → `oak` remains. Final trainer-battle visual validation remains deferred by request.
- Trainer sprite normalization now exempts the literal class `MEDIUM` from the compact gender-suffix rewrite. The generic trailing-`m` rule produced nonexistent `mediu_m.*`; the ASM-derived asset is `medium.*`. A static inventory of trainer constants against exported sprite stems now leaves only `TRAINER_NONE`, the non-battle sentinel, without art. Final trainer-battle visual validation remains deferred by request.
- Intentional `overworld_player_hidden` state now suppresses the player sprite without entering the missing-art error branch. Rust previously filtered the loaded standing frame by visibility and then interpreted the resulting `None` as a failed sprite load, breaking source-valid `hide_object PLAYER` and special-presentation sequences. Genuine visible-player art failures remain fatal. Final overworld presentation validation remains deferred by request.
- The input-owning battle move-learning/forget screen now requires its pending move record, exact party slot, and valid forget cursor. Rust previously returned silently from any missing boundary and left the battle accepting input behind a blank menu; failures now reach the existing visible render-error path. Final move-learning battle validation remains deferred by request.
- Battle PACK rendering now requires its pocket cursor, carried quantity, catalog description, derived action list, and action cursor. Rust previously returned silently for invalid cursors/actions and fabricated quantity zero or `INVALID ITEM DESCRIPTION`, leaving an input-owning blank or source-impossible screen. These failures now propagate through the battle render boundary. Final battle-item UI validation remains deferred by request.

- The exported `StartTitleScreen` phase now carries source-certified label-to-operation indices for all four scene routines and every reachable local branch. The exported `MainMenu` phase likewise retains its source loop back-edge and cancel-return label instead of ending silently after dispatch. TypeScript and Rust reject missing/out-of-range branch, result-branch, option-source, and internal dispatch targets. A generic Rust phase machine executes the emitted memory writes, wrapping byte/word arithmetic, input sampling and predicates, jumps, returns, option writes, exit bits, and explicit host-effect stream; reads before source initialization and unimplemented source opcodes fail instead of becoming zero or silent effects. The Bevy title loop now dispatches through the exported `TitleScreenScene` table, supplies only the hardware joypad byte, and consumes the interpreter's selected option. Direct A/Start entry uses that same source scene rather than a shell shortcut. The title crystal's OAM Y now initializes, increments, wraps, and stops from the exported `initialize_title_crystal_oam`/`animate_title_crystal` operands instead of `title.frame * 2` renderer arithmetic. Suicune rendering now reads the exported frame table and selector mask/shift/nibble-swap operands and observes the source pre-increment `wSuicuneFrame` boundary, fixing the host's one-frame-early transitions at ticks 8/16/24/32. Title art also rejects an out-of-range palette id instead of silently substituting palette zero. This removes the parallel handwritten entrance/timer/timeout/delete/clock/Start control flow and three renderer-owned errors. The remaining rendered title surface and subsequent main-menu/new-game presentation work stays tracked under the presentation-program rows below.
- Main-menu composition now comes from the ASM tables as well: the exporter emits all nine release `MainMenuItems` records, six `.Strings` rows, `.MenuHeader` coordinates, and its one-based default option. Rust pairs item indexes with the certified jumptable and chooses the no-save, Continue, or Mystery Gift record through the exported variant values. Bevy now resets the cursor from the source default and draws the main window from the exported rectangle. The former handwritten option lists, visible labels, initial cursor, and main-window geometry are removed; missing tables, invalid indexes/coordinates, and unsupported source dispatch targets fail closed.
- Desktop input now preserves Crystal's distinct A, B, Start, and Select boundaries throughout the title/new-game path. Enter maps only to Start: it enters the title menu, exits Options alongside B as `PAD_START | PAD_B`, and moves the naming-screen cursor to END; it no longer confirms time, Oak, gender, name choice, save deletion/reset, Mystery Gift, options values, title rows, Continue, or Start-menu rows. The gender `VerticalMenu` now accepts only Up/Down movement and A selection; the former Left/Right movement and generic Start confirmation are removed while its source `STATICMENU_DISABLE_B` behavior remains intact. A physical title edge is latched until exactly one source-frame `hJoyDown` sample, preventing a faster host render clock from dropping Start or replaying it after the title transition. Focused source guards reject reintroducing Enter-as-A aliases.
- The gender menu now consumes its already certified `InitGender` presentation data instead of repeating BOY/GIRL, a 6/4/12/9 rectangle, row zero, and a 10-frame completion delay in Bevy. Pack loading requires exactly two nonempty rows, the matching one-based cursor domain, Crystal's 0/1 gender values, an in-LCD non-inverted rectangle, a valid source default, and a nonzero byte-sized wait. Rendering, wrapping, selection, smoke driving, and tests all use that typed definition; invalid cursors and malformed source tables no longer clamp to a plausible row.
- Item export now distinguishes the cartridge's 256-entry byte-indexed attribute table from its 255 addressable nonzero catalog entries. The terminal `$00` wraparound attribute row is verified but not emitted as a fabricated 256th item; the 255-entry description table and integration catalog agree again.
- Game audio now has one stored representation end to end: exact ASM-rendered 22.05 kHz stereo signed 16-bit PCM. Stereo is required because Crystal exposes Mono/Stereo and 85 source audio programs author `stereo_panning` or `force_stereo_panning`; the former mono export irreversibly erased those commands. The runtime preserves authored channels for Stereo and collapses each frame to dual mono for Mono without storing a second representation. The obsolete MIDI files were hash-derived placeholder melodies rather than source audio and are deleted after every successful export. Rust no longer exposes a MIDI source enum, sequenced playback mode, MIDI parser/validator, snapshot variant, modpack branch, or synthetic MIDI fixture; legacy `.mid` paths and `source: "midi"` payloads fail closed. The TypeScript/web path likewise has no MIDI recorder, MIDI instruments, browser MIDI-to-MP3 converter, FFmpeg worker/binaries, alternate-format dependencies, generator/smoke script, or audio-generation UI; `/audio` redirects to the canonical PCM catalog. Its public audio-registration boundary rejects MP3, WAV, Ogg, FLAC, MIDI, and noncanonical manifest paths, and its manifest decoder accepts only typed PCM music/clip manifests; the former encoded `mixedPath`/stem/`assetPath` schemas are removed. Modpacks must use the same canonical PCM format rather than arbitrary rates, channel layouts, or sample widths. The exporter downsamples the former 44.1 kHz stereo payload to 22.05 kHz stereo before writing loose assets, halving raw size while PCM remains gzip-compressed in pack storage. Pack compilation validates and stores those canonical bytes directly. The TUI default resolver accepts only canonical `/api/audio/pcm/{music,sfx,cries}/*.json` sources and no longer discovers arbitrary MP3/WAV/PCM files or alternate audio roots. `MUSIC_NONE` is an ASM control value, not an audio program: export removes any stale file, compiled packs reject it as a stored audio asset, save/map validation accepts it only as music state, and runtime playback resolves it to a typed stop/reset action without creating a sink. Credits issue that reset directly before `MUSIC_CREDITS`; it no longer depends on a forbidden sentinel payload being present. The canonical stereo contract advances the compiled pack format to v12.
- Canonical rendering now explicitly uses the converter's accurate cartridge mode. The previous default selected an optional enhanced-output path that applied an extra smoothing pass and doubled master gain; those transformations have no ASM command or hardware-register authority and are no longer present in exported game audio.
- Native embedded audio and browser sidecars use the gzip encoder's best compression level. This changes only lossless pack storage, not the single decoded PCM representation or its samples.
- Browser export now garbage-collects obsolete hash-addressed `*.pcm.gz` files only after every currently referenced sidecar has been written and before publishing the new manifest-bearing pack. The mono-to-stereo migration therefore leaves exactly one 1,130-file canonical audio set instead of retaining 1,130 unreachable mono sidecars; unrelated files in the directory are preserved. The v12 provenance verifier is covered against the generated pack so format migrations cannot leave its independent release gate pinned to the prior version.
- The 44.1-to-22.05 kHz decimator retains complete left/right frames and maps loop boundaries with ceiling division. Odd source lengths therefore retain their final selected frame instead of advertising a loop end one frame early.
- The live Magnet Train presentation now preserves `PlayMusic2` rather than collapsing it into an ordinary replacement: it stops only music channels, leaves transient SFX/cry state intact, consumes the source `DelayFrame`, and queues `MUSIC_MAGNET_TRAIN` on the following frame. The prior same-update stop/start omitted a cartridge-visible silent frame.
- Destination map music now synchronizes during map setup even when a field notice remains visible, and an old queued copy of that destination track cannot suppress the authoritative replacement after an intervening music change. The former textbox and stale-queue guards could leave Elm's Lab music playing after the player exited to New Bark Town; neither guard exists in the ASM map-music path.
- Both authored `special WaitSFX` uses now enter the same autonomous transient-completion boundary as the `waitsfx` opcode. Fly cannot run `.ReturnFromFly`, and Rock Smash cannot start `SFX_STRENGTH`, until the pending/playing channels 5–8 program has ended. The former special branch drained the event and immediately cleared `waiting_for_sound_effect`, turning a blocking source call into a no-op.
- The title timeout now executes its exported `fade_audio` boundary through the shared ASM music-fade engine. `TitleScreenMain` writes the certified source rate byte `8` to the exported `wMusicFade` target and requests `MUSIC_NONE`; Rust previously stored the derived 64-frame duration in that WRAM register, decremented it in title-specific shell code, and never began the audible fade. The phase interpreter now validates the exact register/rate/target and the eight-volume-step duration, the visible title observes real fade completion, and `TitleScreenEnd` selects `TITLESCREENOPTION_RESTART` only after the shared engine clears the source register. Red-then-green interpreter coverage, all 10 visible-title regressions, and the exact shared fade regression pass.
- CrystalIntro's 28-entry scene domain now comes from the certified `IntroScenes` ASM jump table. The exporter records every entry's exact compiled-operation offset and source-authored completion waits in the loop dispatch contract; Rust independently derives those waits from each operation range and rejects missing, reordered, extra, out-of-range, operation-table-disagreeing, or timing-disagreeing entries. Bevy initializes, delays, and completes the intro from that source-derived contract. The invented descriptive scene-name array, clear-palette scene list, and special-case 6/3-frame delay policy are removed. Sprite activation is likewise keyed by exported `(dispatcher_entry, dispatch_tick)` operations and exact instance IDs rather than ten literal ASM source-line numbers; the load boundary verifies each activation remains inside its source scene and agrees with its lifetime or grouped-instance tick. All ten ordinary intro audio operations now carry source-derived dispatcher entry/tick metadata: allocation-adjacent cues inherit certified sprite activation ticks, persistent handlers use the independent ASM tick simulation, and Scene 28's whoosh derives from its exported `$80` pre-decrement sequence. Bevy tracks dispatcher invocation count separately from `wIntroSceneFrameCounter`, which is required when a scene inherits a nonzero counter. Scene 12's eight sentinel-terminated Unown sound cues likewise execute from that scene's exported `scheduled_audio` operation, including the certified SFX-channel stop semantics, instead of duplicated Rust frame/audio matches. Pack loading rejects misplaced ordinary cues and invalid schedule clocks, sentinels, channel behavior, unordered frames, or audio IDs absent from the certified catalog before gameplay begins. Unown pulse fades now resolve their three RGB555 colors from the exported `palette_fade_lookup` tables and source fold contract, while the final Crystal-word fade resolves both grayscale colors from the exported fast/slow hue vectors; the five handwritten renderer color generators are removed. Scene 20's indexed Unown reveal now selects its exact exported palette resource and destination slot from `copy_indexed_palette`, and Scene 24 converts the selected four RGB555 channels directly from `broadcast_indexed_palette` on the exported mask/shift cadence. The final broadcast naturally persists through Scene 25 instead of being reconstructed from a handwritten fade index, and the renderer no longer maps an invented numeric palette-set field to resource names. These exact operation boundaries now support replacing the remaining handwritten per-scene behavior without duplicating its topology, timing, allocation identity, palette data, or scheduled effects again.

## Search inventory triage

The raw inventory is intentionally not classified by keyword alone:

- Test fixture names containing `fallback`, `legacy`, or `noop` are normally
  test-only and must not be treated as production behavior.
- `unwrap_or` used only to format diagnostics is not automatically divergent.
- Every production occurrence is being entered above with its semantic use,
  source evidence, and required test before modification.

Current raw Rust-tree counts (before test/reachability exclusion): `TODO` 2, `FIXME` 2, `HACK` 2, `stub` 12,
`dummy` 34, `placeholder` 8, `fake` 2, `synthetic` 15, `approximate` 3,
`simplified` 2, `fallback` 854, `legacy` 527, `noop` 98, `unsupported` 97,
`temporary` 14, and 313 `unwrap_or`/`unwrap_or_default` occurrences. These
are an inventory queue, not classifications; fixture/validation names account
for many of the fallback/legacy hits.

The literal `TODO`, `FIXME`, and `HACK` scan over non-test `*.rs` sources
currently produces no matches. The earlier aggregate counts include unit-test
fixtures and non-Rust generated/auxiliary paths; they remain in the inventory
until their reachability is checked. The concrete production marker findings
above are therefore semantic, not keyword-only.

## Immediate stop conditions

- No new shell shortcut, presentation fallback, default game state, or
  map/story-specific branch may be added while its row remains unproven.
- Missing exported behavior must fail pack validation; it must not be rendered
  as placeholder UI or silently substituted.

## Repairs completed during this audit

- `VAR_UNOWNCOUNT` now reads the saved first-caught `wUnownDex` equivalent rather than counting the species id `UNOWN` in the ordinary caught-species set (which could never exceed one). Every caught Unown records its exact one-based A–Z form from Crystal's four-DV bit formula, duplicate forms retain their original single entry, malformed saved indices/order duplicates are rejected, and the Ruins scientist's source `ifgreater 2` boundary can now become true.
- `VAR_BADGES` now counts both eight-bit badge arrays exactly like ASM's two-byte `wBadges` bit count. It previously counted Johto only, making Oak's 16-badge Mt. Silver branch and Vermilion's Kanto-progress dialogue unreachable or incorrect.
- Source `loadvar VAR_MOVEMENT` and `writevar VAR_MOVEMENT` now update the live player movement mode and the saved overworld snapshot together. The common interpreter previously changed only its generic variable map, so exact Bike/Fly/Dig/Teleport/Surf scripts could report the intended `wPlayerState` while collision, sprite, and persisted traversal stayed in the old mode. All five source bytes (`NORMAL`, `BIKE`, `SKATE`, `SURF`, and `SURF_PIKA`) are decoded explicitly; unsupported bytes fail instead of becoming a traversal fallback.

Crystal invokes `PursuitSwitch` from the switch routine before recall; Rust's abstract turn list preserves the same outcome by ordering Pursuit ahead of a switch and passing the switching flag for doubled damage. On equal move priority, a single Quick Claw holder consumes one roll. With two holders, the external-clock side is sampled first: enemy first in ordinary/external-clock play, player first for the internal-clock link player, and the first activation returns immediately. Parameter zero is a valid never-activate chance that still consumes its roll. | Added the pre-move player-action and link-switch gates, retained structural Pursuit interception, allowed zero Quick Claw parameters, and replaced unconditional player-then-enemy sampling with the exact serial-clock-dependent branch and short-circuit RNG consumption. | `engine/battle/core.asm::DetermineMoveOrder`, `PursuitSwitch`, and `BattleMonEntrance`; focused regressions cover non-move no-RNG ordering, link double-switch clock inversion, dual-holder ordinary/internal-clock order, and exact RNG consumption. |
| ASM battle badge-stat parity | Outside link and Battle Tower battles, the player's Zephyr/Mineral/Plain/Glacier badges boost Attack/Defense/Speed/Special Attack by `floor(base/8)` before stages and Burn/Paralysis; enemies never receive badge boosts. Crystal's clobbered accumulator bug makes Glacier Badge boost Special Defense only when unboosted Special Attack is 206–432 or at least 661. | Persisted the explicit enable flag, mapped the exact badge bits at shared damage/confusion/Beat Up/turn-order boundaries, disabled boosts for link and Battle Tower construction, and replaced the unconditional TypeScript-shaped Special Defense boost with the cartridge's exact Special Attack-dependent boundary. | Direct comparison against `engine/battle/core.asm::BadgeStatBoosts` and the source's documented bug; focused boundary coverage checks 205/206, 432/433, and 660/661. |
| Bug-Catching Contest score byte width | `ContestScore` reads only the low byte of max HP, all five battle stats, and current HP. It adds four copies of low max HP plus those low stat bytes, the DV term, low current HP divided by eight, and the held-item point through the two-byte `hProduct` accumulator. | Replaced full-width saturating host arithmetic with the exact low-byte inputs and wrapping 16-bit accumulator. High-byte stat changes can no longer alter the player's contest score. | Direct comparison against `engine/events/bug_contest/judging.asm::ContestScore`; a red-first high-byte regression expected the cartridge score 35 and observed Rust's former 4931. |
| Bug-Catching Contest caught provenance rewrite | Both party insertion and box insertion call the source caught-data writer before forcing the National Park landmark. The box-full bug applies that same rewrite to the first existing boxed Pokémon: level, current time, and player gender are overwritten along with location. | Contest finalization now writes the live time and player gender instead of preserving stale caught provenance or fabricating an absent time/male identity. The box-full corruption path updates all four modeled caught-data fields. | Direct comparison against `engine/pokemon/caught_data.asm::CheckPartyFullAfterContest`, `SetCaughtData`, and `SetBoxMonCaughtData`; focused party and full-box regressions cover newly created and overwritten provenance. |
| Wild flee-attempt byte overflow | `TryToRunAwayFromBattle` increments `wNumFleeAttempts` with an 8-bit `inc a` before comparing Speed, so attempt 255 wraps the stored counter to zero even when the faster-player shortcut succeeds without `BattleRandom`. | Both Rust escape entry points now use wrapping byte increment instead of saturating at 255. The ordinary and exact DIV-traced paths retain the same no-RNG fast escape while reporting/persisting counter zero. | Direct comparison against `engine/battle/core.asm::TryToRunAwayFromBattle`; the red-first boundary regression observed the former saturated 255 and now covers both public RNG adapters. |
| Wild escape loaded-Speed authority | The ordinary RUN menu passes `wBattleMonSpeed` and `wEnemyMonSpeed` directly to `TryToRunAwayFromBattle`. Those words already reflect the live battle load/stage/status/badge boundaries and cartridge cached-stat bugs; the routine does not reconstruct either stat from species data. | Core's integrated and pack-facing exact run paths now obtain both words from the shared battle-Speed authority and pass them into the escape calculation. Species-stat recalculation can no longer reverse the live ordering or consume an ASM-absent random byte. | Direct comparison against `engine/battle/core.asm::BattleMenu_Run` and `TryToRunAwayFromBattle`; a red-first combat regression gives the loaded words the opposite ordering from species data and proves the source fast path takes no RNG. |
| Fainted-player escape Speed pointer bug | `AskUseNextPokemon` calls `TryToRunAwayFromBattle` with `hl = wPartyMon1Speed`, not the fainted active battler's `wBattleMonSpeed`. It therefore compares party slot 0's raw saved Speed against the live enemy Speed, even when another party slot fainted. | The exact active-battle escape boundary now admits the source-valid fainted active slot only when a living replacement exists and selects party slot 0's raw Speed for that caller. Ordinary RUN continues using the loaded battle Speed pair. | Direct comparison against `engine/battle/core.asm::AskUseNextPokemon` and `BattleMenu_Run`; the red-first integration fixture previously failed before reaching escape because the shared active-index helper rejected the fainted slot, then distinguishes raw slot-0 Speed from the opposite active-Speed ordering and verifies the exact failed roll. |

## Pending inventory partitions

1. `crystal-bevy/src/bevy_shell.rs`: hotkeys, `Visible*` states, render and
   audio paths.
2. `crystal-bevy/src/lib.rs`: runtime mutation wrappers and synthetic battle/
   world adapters.
4. `crystal-core`: defaults, RNG, scripts, battle effects, save state.
5. `crystal-assets` and exporters: unexported ASM data and handwritten inputs.

## Audit status

This is an in-progress blocking audit, not a parity certification. The
remaining production findings above are deliberately still classified
`unproven` or `divergent`; therefore the repository does **not** meet the
requested acceptance criterion that none remain. No broad cleanup, UI rewrite,
or behavior-preserving refactor may be treated as a repair until the relevant
row has an ASM source mapping and a failing parity fixture.

The source searches used for the raw inventory intentionally include all Rust
production crates, while `#[cfg(test)]`/test-module occurrences are excluded
only after individual reachability review. The next pass must expand every
remaining production marker occurrence in the five partitions into the same
file/line/evidence/classification table above; aggregate keyword counts are
not evidence of either fidelity or divergence.
| Overworld warp/event priority | TypeScript/ASM only activate warp events on live warp-class collision, require matching facing on directional carpets, and preempt wild encounters for warps, coordinate scripts, trainer sight, and connections | Rust activated warps from coordinates alone and could roll an encounter on the same movement | Gate warp resolution through live collision/facing and suppress encounter rolls behind higher-priority movement events | Static cross-comparison complete; final validation deferred |
| Visible overworld event order | ASM checks trainer sight first, then connection, warp, coordinate event, `CountStep`, and finally encounters; phone-call dispatch owns an event boundary rather than interrupting an LCD walk | Rust previously ran `CountStep` first, allowing Repel expiry, Egg hatch, or poison feedback to suppress a trainer or tile event. Its visible shell also omitted incoming calls from the event boundary, so catch-up could run behind the call and either a special call or an ordinary call sampled during retained interpolation could begin before the walk visibly landed. | Reordered the authoritative overworld frame path to the cartridge sequence; trainer/transition/coordinate events now preempt counters, while an interrupting `CountStep` result still preempts only the wild roll. Incoming calls now stop catch-up on their source frame and retain a dedicated post-landing script boundary across both newly triggered and already-retained movement. | Focused trainer/Repel, coordinate/counter, warp/hatch-ready-Egg, and incoming-call catch-up/landing regressions pass. |
| Dynamic/backup warps | TypeScript/ASM resolve negative destination warp ids through previous/backup warp memory, preserving the originating center/elevator across shared intermediary maps | Rust rejected every dynamic destination warp | Added serialized previous/backup warp memory and stateful resolution for Pokécenter 2F link rooms, shared elevators, and ordinary backup returns | Static cross-comparison complete; final validation deferred |
| Visible trainer-loss continuation | TypeScript continues after a trainer loss only for exact `BATTLETYPE_CANLOSE`; ordinary losses white out | Failed-Run retaliation completed every trainer loss with `can_lose = true` | Derive loss continuation from the active battle type so ordinary losses enter the whiteout path | Static cross-comparison complete; final validation deferred |
| Trainer sightline classification | TypeScript/ASM scan only exact `OBJECTTYPE_TRAINER` objects with a dispatchable trainer script | Rust also auto-engaged `OBJECTTYPE_SCRIPT` NPCs whenever their radius was nonzero | Restrict automatic sightline engagement to exact trainer objects | Static cross-comparison complete; final validation deferred |
| Defeated trainer sightlines | TypeScript skips trainers whose compiled trainer event flag is already set | Rust sightline resolution did not consult compiled trainer flags, allowing repeated automatic engagement | Filter visible sightline candidates through the map's trainer-script catalog and saved event flags | Static cross-comparison complete; final validation deferred |
| Trainer sight cutscene | `SeenByTrainerScript`/TypeScript retain the sighting, show `EMOTE_SHOCK` for 30 frames, pause one frame, walk the trainer with `slow_step` until one tile from the player, face both actors toward each other, and only then start the trainer script. Player input remains locked throughout. | Rust dispatched the trainer script immediately on sight detection, omitting every visible cutscene stage and allowing no approach movement. | Retain an explicit trainer-sight presentation state, block player/menu/save boundaries, render the shock emote, advance each source-speed approach tile over 16 frames with an interpolated object position, settle both facings, then dispatch the retained interaction. | Static comparison against TypeScript `trainer-sightlines.ts` and ASM `SeenByTrainerScript` complete; final validation deferred. |
| Visible Run turn retaliation | TypeScript/ASM run Berserk Gene and choose the opponent's normal AI move before resolving a player RUN attempt; a failed escape consumes the turn, applies player residuals, evaluates `TryEnemyFlee`, and only then permits the surviving enemy action. | Rust formerly supplied `BattleAction::Run` for the enemy, then selected a move outside the shared turn; that later split skipped the core start-of-turn/AI boundary and could reorder exact RNG around escape and retaliation. | The visible path now stages RUN through the same in-core enemy selectors as Fight/Bag/Ball. One replayable command owns Berserk Gene, enemy selection, escape, residuals, wild flee, and retaliation. | Core four-read source-order and runtime atomic replay regressions pass; final grouped validation deferred. |
| Visible step-event interruption and poison whiteout | `Script_MonFaintedToPoison` plays poison feedback, calls `PrintText` separately for every party member that just fainted, and only then far-jumps through `OverworldWhiteoutScript`; `FadeOutToWhite` finishes before `pause 40` begins | Rust skipped the individual faint texts when the party fully whited out, combined multiple names on non-terminal paths, and counted the eight fade frames as part of the 40-frame pause | Retain the step event through the landing boundary, present one exact `<nickname> fainted!` notice per affected party member, enter whiteout only after the final acknowledgement, and hold all 40 post-fade frames | Focused poison text ordering, multiple-faint, whiteout handoff, and hold-count regressions pass |
| Egg hatch typed consumer | `HatchEggScript` calls `OverworldHatchEgg`, which writes egg caught data and player OT identity, records Pokédex state and the Togepi event, recalculates every party stat, then presents `Huh?`, an 80-frame hold, the 344-frame increasing wobble/crack loop, 130 shell-fragment frames, `ANIM_MON_HATCH`, caught-mon sound, exact hatch text, and nickname prompt before restarting map music | Rust only cleared the egg marker, repaired HP, played a cry, and displayed one invented `hatched from the EGG` notice; OT/caught/Pokédex/story state, most stats, source art/timing/music, and nickname handling were absent | Added atomic exact hatch mutation with live-map landmark ownership, source-timed modal presentation using `egg_hatch.2bpp` and exported frontpic animation, exact source text/audio, YES/NO naming through the canonical party rename mutation, and delayed `RestartMapMusic` | Focused core mutation, timing/audio/frontpic, caught-location atomicity, and nickname-boundary tests pass; full core/assets and canonical export pass |
| Item-ball typed consumer | `FindItemInBallScript` prints the found-item text, then on success plays `SFX_ITEM`, pauses exactly 60 frames, runs `itemnotify`, and waits at the pocket text; PACK-full instead waits on the found text and the exact three-line failure text without a fanfare or notification | Rust queued `SFX_ITEM` immediately for both outcomes, made found text wait for input on success, skipped the 60-frame pause and `itemnotify`, and flattened the PACK-full path into a generic queued notice | Added explicit found/fanfare-pause/pocket and PACK-full phases, autonomous success transition after text printing, exact 60-frame input lock, canonical pocket text, correct prompt arrows, and no success audio on failure | Focused source-order, frame-count, pocket-text, and PACK-full tests pass |

Its failure branch still prints that received text, waits for A/B, and then runs `pocketisfull` to show `The <POCKET> is full…`, without sound. | Visible Rust omitted the hidden-item presentation entirely, started fruit and verbose-grant sounds as soon as their first text appeared, always used the ordinary item cue, did not wait for decoded sound completion, cleared item-ball text during its authored pause, hid fruit-tree prompt arrows until the last queued page, and replaced verbose PACK-full choreography with invented generic prose. | Unified the typed reward presentation around explicit trigger text, source-specific fixed-pause versus decoded-sound waits, exact pocket-based cue selection, retained text, source-authored queued-page prompts, verbose grant's leading `waitsfx` and trailing prompt, immediate hidden/fruit notification after sound completion, and source-ordered, pocket-specific, soundless PACK-full branches. | Red/green hidden-item success/failure, fruit prompt/sound, and verbose PACK-full regressions plus focused item-ball, fruit-tree, verbose Potion, and verbose TM/HM tests pass. |

A protected move therefore consumes neither Lock-On nor an accuracy byte; damaging and status moves share this boundary. Selfdestruct still executes its following self-KO command, and Jump Kick still crashes. Bide enters this boundary only when stored energy is released. | Rust deferred Protect to individual damage helpers after ordinary accuracy and Lock-On processing, so protected attacks shifted RNG and consumed Lock-On; direct status moves such as Sleep ignored Protect entirely. | Preserve the source-backed ordinary `checkhit` effect inventory, run Dream Eater and Protect at its common pre-accuracy boundary, retain the effect-script-specific Selfdestruct and Jump Kick continuations, and route only Bide's release turn through the shared gate before its ordinary damage tail. | Focused damage/status and Bide-release regressions prove no accuracy RNG, no Lock-On consumption, the `MoveProtected` result, and shared Substitute/survival/Rage/Bide/faint/King's Rock settlement; full grouped validation remains deferred. |
| X Accuracy sure-hit substatus | `XAccuracyEffect` sets `SUBSTATUS_X_ACCURACY`; repeated use fails, switching clears the volatile bit, and `BattleCommand_CheckHit` returns after Fly/Dig eligibility but before perfect-accuracy handling, stat modifiers, BrightPowder, or `BattleRandom`. It is not an Accuracy-level increase. | Rust routed X Accuracy through its generic X-item stage helper, raising Accuracy by one stage and leaving low-accuracy moves subject to BrightPowder and RNG. Battle state had no X Accuracy substatus. | Added side-specific persistent X Accuracy state, dedicated item handling with no stage mutation in both the turn resolver and visible-runtime bag boundary, repeated-use rejection, switch clearing, and the exact sure-hit gate for ordinary, per-hit, and OHKO moves. X Accuracy does not make Fissure hit a flying target. | Four focused core regressions prove the Pokemon-only item helper and turn resolver leave Accuracy unchanged, repeated use fails, switching clears the flag, a 1%-accuracy move hits through BrightPowder without a miss event, and the OHKO Fly gate remains first; grouped core/assets/Bevy checks pass. |
| Foresight accuracy/evasion interaction | In `CheckHit.StatModifiers`, when the target's Evasion level is at least the user's Accuracy level and the target is identified, the routine returns before both multiplier lookups and retains the move's base accuracy byte. When Accuracy is higher, the advantageous modifiers still apply. | Rust used Foresight only for Normal/Fighting type effectiveness and always applied the net accuracy/evasion stage, so identified targets retained their evasion advantage. | Pass the live identified substatus into ordinary and OHKO accuracy calculation and skip both multipliers only at the exact non-advantageous comparison boundary. | Focused regression covers extreme negative and positive stage differences with an identified target. |
| Bide release `CheckHit` and damage tail | `storeenergy` ends the initial and storing turns before `checkhit`. On release it clears Bide, prints the unleashed-energy text, builds doubled retained damage, skips to `unleashenergy_command`, then runs reset-type, shared `checkhit`, animation/failure, ordinary damage, faint, Rage, and King's Rock. | Bide phase advancement now precedes the common hit boundary: start/store return directly, while release clears retained state, emits a distinct source-ordered `BideUnleashed` narration event, and alone enters Protect/Lock-On/airborne/accuracy. Successful doubled damage now uses Substitute, Endure, Focus Band, retained-damage, Rage, opposing Bide storage, faint/Destiny Bond, and King's Rock processing instead of direct HP subtraction. | The Bide-specific prepared damage retains its narration event but otherwise follows the same post-`checkhit` authorities as ordinary application. | Five focused Bide regressions cover start/store/release, protected Lock-On preservation, Substitute/Rage/King's Rock, Endure/retained damage/opposing Bide, and Destiny Bond; core and Bevy production compilation pass. |
| OHKO shared damage handling | A successful Crystal OHKO supplies maximum damage to the ordinary application command, so Substitute receives the hit first and Endure/Focus Band can preserve one real HP; surviving hits continue through held recovery, retained damage, Rage/Bide, and Destiny Bond ordering. The OHKO script ends after `buildopponentrage` and has no King's Rock command. | Rust directly assigned the target's HP to zero and emitted faint, bypassing every shared damage mechanic and its visible narration. | Feed maximum OHKO damage through Substitute and the ordinary survival/item/bookkeeping/faint tail while retaining level, immunity, and special accuracy checks, without inventing a King's Rock tail. | Focused execution now proves Substitute break, Endure survival, and Destiny Bond retaliation; the existing OHKO path also records retained damage, Rage, and Bide through the shared authorities. Focus Band and between-turn held recovery remain covered by their shared helper regressions. |
| OHKO `CheckHit` integration | After immunity and the higher-level rejection, Crystal writes the level-adjusted accuracy byte and calls shared `CheckHit`: Protect precedes Lock-On consumption, Lock-On normally guarantees the hit, Fissure retains its flying exception, Fly/Dig precedes X Accuracy, and unlocked attempts use Foresight-aware accuracy/evasion plus BrightPowder before one battle byte. | Rust originally returned through a standalone OHKO branch before the shared checks. Its dedicated boundary now mirrors Protect, Lock-On, airborne exceptions, X Accuracy, Foresight, stages, BrightPowder, and RNG ordering without weakening the level gate. | Keep the special level-adjusted accuracy byte but reproduce every shared hit gate in source order. | Focused OHKO regressions cover ordinary hit, level rejection, immunity, Lock-On/Fly behavior, and X Accuracy versus grounded/flying targets. |
| Damaging stat effects after KO | AncientPower-class boosts and damaging stat drops apply after `checkfaint`; a knockout terminates the effect stream before those mutations. Their pre-damage `effectchance` command has already consumed its RNG byte, however. | Rust mutated stats before faint resolution, visibly granting effects that Crystal skips on a KO. Simply moving the whole helper after faint would instead lose the source RNG draw. | On a lethal hit, consume only the pre-damage chance sample and suppress the post-faint stat mutation/events; on a surviving hit, retain normal effect application. | Focused AncientPower regressions cover both the surviving-target boost and knockout suppression paths; chance-failure and immunity cases separately prove no mutation while preserving their source gates. |
| Rapid Spin and Hyper Beam pre-faint commands | Rapid Spin's `clearhazards` and Hyper Beam's `rechargenextturn` both precede `checkfaint`. Rapid Spin therefore clears even when its damage KOs, and a successful Hyper Beam requires recharge after either a KO or a Substitute hit. | Rust grouped Rapid Spin with post-faint stat effects and conditioned recharge on a surviving real-HP target, suppressing both source commands at common terminal boundaries. | Keep Rapid Spin clearing before faint termination and set recharge after every successful Hyper Beam damage application, including Substitute, without setting it on misses/immunity/Protect. | Focused regressions now prove Rapid Spin clears before a target-KO event and Hyper Beam starts recharge on both target KO and Substitute damage. Existing miss/immunity/Protect coverage proves recharge is not set without successful application. |
| Rapid Spin clearing scope | Crystal's `clearhazards` removes the user's partial-trapping counter, Leech Seed, and side Spikes; Mean Look is a separate escape-trap substatus and is not cleared. | Rust merged partial trapping and Mean Look into the Rapid Spin clear operation and reported either as the same visible cleared trap. | Restrict Rapid Spin and its event payload to partial trapping, Leech Seed, and Spikes while preserving Mean Look. | Focused execution covers simultaneous Bind, Leech Seed, Spikes, and Mean Look: the first three clear and the escape trap remains exact; a no-damage immunity regression preserves all conditions. |
| King's Rock effect-script eligibility | Crystal executes `kingsrock` only in move scripts that contain that command. Damaging status/stat families, Tri Attack, partial trapping, Dream Eater, Flame Wheel, and Sacred Fire omit it; built-in flinch scripts also do not receive a second held-flinch roll. | Rust appended King's Rock to every ordinary damaging effect, consuming extra RNG and creating flinches for scripts that never invoke the held command. | Gate held flinch by the source effect-script families in addition to the existing built-in-flinch exclusion. | An exhaustive source-backed regression pairs every `EFFECT_*` constant with `effects_pointers.asm`, resolves consecutive-label fallthrough in `effects.asm`, and proves Rust eligibility exactly matches the literal `kingsrock` command. Focused RNG coverage proves `FLINCH_HIT` consumes no held roll; grouped King's Rock tests pass. |
| Burn defrost and sunlight freeze gate | Crystal's successful `burntarget` command jumps to `Defrost` when the target is already frozen, before Safeguard is checked. `freezetarget` silently returns in sunlight after its effect-chance byte has already been consumed. | Rust treated burn against a frozen target as a generic existing-status failure and allowed secondary freeze to apply during sun, producing incorrect status HUDs and subsequent action availability. | Defrost a frozen target at the burn-command boundary with the source message, and suppress an otherwise-successful freeze mutation in sun while retaining the earlier chance RNG. | Static comparison against `BattleCommand_BurnTarget` and `BattleCommand_FreezeTarget` complete; final validation deferred. |
| Same-turn sleep/freeze interruption | After successfully applying sleep or freeze, Crystal invokes `OpponentCantMove` when the held cure did not immediately activate. The not-yet-acted target does not re-enter `CheckTurn`, print a duplicate status line, or consume its recharge that turn; its normal post-action residual boundary still follows. Freeze additionally calls `EndRechargeOpp`. | Rust later executed the target's selected action normally, where recharge was checked before status, producing an incorrect recharge message/state and duplicated sleep/freeze feedback. | Recognize a still-active sleep/freeze applied earlier in the current event batch as an already-ended opposing action, preserve its residual settlement, let an immediately cured holder act, and clear recharge on successful freeze. | Static ASM control-flow comparison complete; final validation deferred. |
| Built-in flinch order and recharge | `BattleCommand_FlinchTarget` consumes its pre-damage chance but applies flinch only when the opponent has not already acted, then clears the opponent's recharge. King's Rock has its own source behavior and is not subject to the same went-first gate. | Rust applied and narrated built-in flinch after a faster opponent had already acted, and did not clear recharge when a valid flinch landed. | Carry action-order context into the damage tail, suppress only the late built-in mutation after retaining its chance draw, and clear recharge on valid built-in or held flinch. | Static ASM comparison complete; final validation deferred. |
| In-batch switched battler identity | Switch narration is followed by entry hazards, held-item activation, status, damage, and faint text using the newly active battler's identity. TypeScript updates the active battle subject at the switch boundary before projecting those later effects. | Rust captured player and enemy names once from the pre-turn snapshot, so messages after a switch in the same resolved event batch could name the Pokémon that had already left the field even though the sprite/HUD had changed. | Advance the projector's current side identity immediately after each `Switched` event while retaining the explicit switched-party name for the send-out/dragged-out line itself. | Static event-order comparison complete; final validation deferred. |
| Protect/Endure/Destiny Bond action-boundary lifetime | Crystal clears the acting user's prior Destiny Bond before `DoPlayerTurn`/`DoEnemyTurn`, including recharge, status, disobedience, switch, item, and failed-run exits. After that action it clears the opponent's Protect, Endure, and Destiny Bond. Consequently these effects protect through the opposing action that follows their application but are gone before between-turn Future Sight. | Rust cleared Protect/Endure globally after between-turn effects and cleared Destiny Bond only after a usable move reached PP consumption. Old states could survive recharge/status/menu exits, and delayed Future Sight could incorrectly observe Endure or activate Destiny Bond. | Model the source pre-action and post-opponent-action clears on every action path, including the split enemy-response and newly immobilized paths; keep the final global clear only as an invariant/counter boundary. | Static battle-core ordering comparison complete; recharge, ordinary opposing-action, and Future Sight/Endure regressions updated; final validation deferred. |
| Time-based healing and animation parameter | `BattleCommand_TimeBasedHealContinue` starts at half HP, reduces to quarter when Morning Sun/Synthesis/Moonlight does not match morning/day/night outside link battles, then doubles in sun or halves in rain/sandstorm. The resulting `0..3` index selects eighth/quarter/half/full HP and is retained as `wBattleAnimParam`. | Rust ignored time and link context, used half HP normally, a fabricated two-thirds HP in sun, quarter HP in adverse weather, and always rendered animation parameter zero. | Persist battle time/link context, derive the exact multiplier index once, use it for HP restoration, publish it with the typed heal result, and feed it into the compiled move animation including called moves. | Static comparison against `BattleCommand_HealMorn/Day/Nite` and `BattleCommand_TimeBasedHealContinue`; source matrix regression updated; final validation deferred. |
| Baton Pass volatile reset and presentation | Gen II Baton Pass preserves stat levels, confusion, Focus Energy, Substitute, Leech Seed, Curse, and other unlisted passable state. `ResetBatonPassStatus` clears Disable, attraction on both battlers, Transform, Encore, last move, and both Wrap counters; Nightmare survives only when the incoming battler is asleep. The incoming battler remains visually behind the passed Substitute. | Rust destroyed the passed Substitute in both core and the visible switch projector, retained attraction and partial trapping, and left Nightmare active until a later residual check, allowing the incoming battler to show or suffer states the source had already reset. | Preserve Substitute through the exact Baton Pass `Switched` event only, clear both attraction and partial-trap sides, and apply Nightmare's post-switch sleep condition immediately while retaining the already-modeled stat/confusion/Focus Energy transfer. Consume the presentation marker so a later ordinary switch in the same event batch still clears the doll. | Static ASM reset-list and renderer event-order comparison complete; combined pass/reset regression expanded; final validation deferred. |
# Overworld party screen (2026-08-02)

- Start-menu Pokémon selection now uses the same full-screen six-slot source geometry as battle switching instead of the narrow diagnostic list.
- It displays animated compiled species/egg icons, held-item/mail overlays, nickname, HP, status, level, HP gauge, CANCEL, and the source selection prompt.
- Party actions and summaries retain their existing state-specific surfaces; this change does not add controller policy or fallback data.

# Trainer Shift prompt staging (2026-08-02)

- Trainer replacement in Shift mode now queues the upcoming-Pokémon announcement as its own acknowledged battle paragraph.
- Only after that acknowledgement does the source YES/NO window ask whether the player will change Pokémon.

# Pokémon stats interaction (2026-08-02)

- Rust now retains the source three-page summary state instead of treating SUMMARY as a one-page diagnostic overlay.
- Left/right wrap STATUS, MOVES, and STATS; A advances and exits from page three; B exits from any page; eggs exit on A.
- Up/down changes the viewed party member, resets to STATUS, and queues the new non-egg Pokémon's cry.
- The visible summary is now a full 20×18 page-tinted screen with shared identity header and distinct STATUS, MOVES, STATS, and egg content rather than a narrow debug panel.
- Selecting a party member now retains the full party screen and overlays the source right-side 14×18 action region instead of replacing the screen with a detached diagnostic panel.
- GIVE/TAKE likewise retains the party screen and uses the source lower-right 8×6 choice region.
- Party reordering now stays on the animated full party surface, marks both the original slot and current destination, and presents the destination prompt in the bottom text region.
- Move reordering no longer invents a selectable CANCEL row: the source screen lists only learned moves and uses B to cancel. Its dedicated renderer follows the 20×11 move list and 20×7 detail geometry with PP, type, power, origin marker, and `Where?` swap prompt.
- The lower move detail region now parses the authoritative ASM description table once, verifies it covers the compiled move order, and displays the selected move's real description; missing/misaligned source data remains a loud error rather than fallback prose.
# Overworld Pack screen (2026-08-02)

- Replaced the detached compact Pack rows with a full 20×18 source-layout surface: left pocket panel, seven-row scrolling item region, fixed item/quantity columns, trailing CANCEL, and bottom description box.
- Pocket navigation remains authority-driven by the existing Rust cursor state; the visible arrows and pocket label now make it understandable on screen.
- Item action choices retain the Pack underneath and use the source right-side seven-tile overlay with dynamic vertical placement.
- Battle ITEM/BALL selection now uses the same full-screen Pack geometry and compiled item descriptions instead of a detached battle submenu. Both lists include the source trailing CANCEL row; confirming it returns to battle commands without selecting an enemy action or consuming a turn.
- Battle PACK now exposes the canonical ITEM, BALL, KEY, and TM/HM pocket cycle, including empty-pocket CANCEL rows, and uses ASM's battle-only submenu: usable items offer USE/QUIT and unusable items offer only QUIT. Field-only GIVE/TOSS/SEL actions are not exposed in battle; B/QUIT return to the item list.
- Opening PACK with no carried items now still enters ASM's empty Items pocket and its CANCEL row; Rust no longer substitutes an invented `You don't have any items.` battle acknowledgement.
- Field and battle Pack now retain the last standard pocket across closes, expose all four standard pockets even when empty, and give Left/Right opposite wrapping directions. Rust no longer jumps to the first nonempty pocket, hides empty pockets, refuses an empty field Pack, or advances both shoulder directions identically.
- Each standard Pack pocket now retains its own selected row across pocket switches and Pack closes, mirroring Crystal's separate pocket cursor registers; reopening clamps a retained row only when inventory shrank. Battle KEY ITEM/TM-HM navigation no longer marks the surface as a field Pack and gets torn down by battle cursor synchronization.
- Battle HP/status item targeting now displays the full party surface with `Use on which <PKMN>?`; PP-item targeting uses a full move list with current/max PP and an explicit move-selection prompt.
- Ordinary battle Pokémon selection now retains the party surface and opens Crystal's per-Pokémon `SWITCH / STATS / CANCEL` submenu instead of switching immediately. STATS opens the existing three-page summary and returns to the same selected Pokémon on the rebuilt party list, matching `BattleMenuPKMN_ReturnFromStats`; forced faint replacement, trainer Shift choice, and move-driven switching remain direct-selection flows. Invalid active/fainted/trapped switch attempts return to the party list.
- The battle Pokémon action window uses the source `BattleMonMenu` rectangle at tiles `(11,11)` through `(19,17)`, with SWITCH/STATS/CANCEL on rows 12/14/16 instead of obscuring the upper party entries.
- Battle party selection uses the source `Which <PKMN>?` prompt. Medicine targeting retains `InitPartyMenuWithCancel` semantics: CANCEL is rendered, reachable through wrapped vertical navigation, and returns to the battle Pack without consuming the item or the turn.
- Battle PP items now follow the same two-stage source flow: first choose a Pokémon on the cancellable `Use on which <PKMN>?` party screen, then choose one of that Pokémon's moves. B from the move list returns to party selection; B/CANCEL there returns to the Pack. Horizontal input no longer changes the target Pokémon behind the move list.
- A battle item that cannot affect its selected target now follows `BattlePack.BattleField`: after the visible no-effect acknowledgement it returns to the retained Pack pocket and row. Rust no longer clears every item cursor and ejects the player to the main FIGHT/PKMN/PACK/RUN menu.
- No-effect active, party, PP, escape-item, and Guard Spec. attempts no longer append a deterministic `BattleAction::Item` frame before validation. The action journal is committed only after authoritative mutation accepts the item, matching the fact that a refused target neither spends the item nor advances the battle turn.
- A capture attempt with full storage now stops at Crystal's pre-throw `The POKéMON BOX / is full. That / can't be used now.` boundary: no `<PLAYER> used the BALL` line, deterministic item action, Pack teardown, ball consumption, RNG advance, or enemy response is staged. Dismissing the notice returns to the retained BALL pocket.
- `CheckPlayerHasUsableMoves` parity now includes the zero-move case: an active Pokémon with no moves, all PP exhausted, or every remaining-PP move disabled enters automatic STRUGGLE. Snapshot generation, visible resolution, core priority calculation, and core move execution all accept the synthetic STRUGGLE slot without indexing an absent learned move, eliminating the empty FIGHT-screen softlock.
- Enemy no-legal-move handling now chooses synthetic slot zero for STRUGGLE before wild move-slot RNG or trainer AI scoring. The command snapshot no longer fabricates an “available” learned slot for exhausted/disabled moves; empty, exhausted, and fully disabled enemy movesets therefore neither error nor enter an empty wild/trainer rejection loop, and consume no move-selection byte at that source boundary.
- Forced player STRUGGLE now stages `BattleText_MonHasNoMovesLeft` (`<MON> / has no moves left!`) before the ordinary move-use narration instead of silently substituting the move. The retained pre-turn battle scene remains visible for that notice.
- Repel reuse now checks the active step counter before inventory mutation. Both Pack USE and registered-item paths retain the current surface and show the exact `The REPEL used / earlier is still / in effect.` text; the authoritative asset mutation also rejects direct callers, preventing consumption or replacement of the remaining duration.
- Bicycle toggling now presents the source `<PLAYER> got on the BICYCLE.` / `<PLAYER> got off the BICYCLE.` acknowledgement instead of silently closing the Pack. `ENGINE_ALWAYS_ON_BIKE` is preflighted on both Pack and registered-item paths and shows `You can't get off here!` without calling the mutation or changing movement mode.
- Idle overworld SELECT now always reaches the registered-item menu, even when no item is registered. The empty-registration branch retains the field scene and opens the compiled `_MayRegisterItemText` textbox exactly as `SelectMenu`; Rust previously discarded the button at its input gate and exposed only an unreachable host status branch.
- CARD KEY and BASEMENT KEY now implement their source `ITEMMENU_CLOSE` field effects. Their item ownership, exact map, optional facing requirement, border-adjusted facing tile, and queued script are exported from `card_key.asm`/`basement_key.asm` into the field-move catalog and verified against compiled map content before loading. The authoritative mutation consumes only those pack-owned rules, records the nonconsuming item use, and reproduces `QueueScript` without overwriting `wLastTalked`; the visible Pack path closes and continues the compiled story script. Rust previously declared both items field-usable but fell through to a fatal missing-payload error at these mandatory story gates.
# Pokédex main and entry screens (2026-08-02)

- The shell snapshot now exposes the exact seen species set in addition to counts and caught species, allowing the renderer to distinguish unseen, seen, and owned entries.
- Completely unseen entries no longer open and leak classification, measurements, types, and description data.
- Replaced the narrow diagnostic list/detail with full-screen dark Pokédex surfaces: seven-entry scrolling list, number/name masking, caught marks, selected preview/question mark, SEEN/OWN counts, and entry sprite/header/measurement/description regions.
- Pokémon catalog snapshots are explicitly sorted by authoritative numeric species ID; the Rust `BTreeMap`'s alphabetical key order no longer leaks into Pokédex navigation.
- Pokédex list navigation now clamps at the species boundaries, pages seven rows with Left/Right, preserves detail mode while Up/Down seeks adjacent seen species, and cycles multi-page entry text with A; B returns to the list.
- Pokegear now owns the full LCD instead of falling through to the narrow diagnostic command window: unlocked card tabs, clock/phone/radio content, and a navigable landmark map with current/selected markers are visible on their source-shaped surfaces.
- Pokegear Map now derives Johto/Kanto from the player's current landmark, initializes its cursor at the player location, and restricts cursor order, labels, and markers to that region instead of combining both maps.
- Fly destination selection now follows TypeScript's region boundary: Johto and Kanto lists are mutually exclusive, Kanto requires the Indigo Plateau unlock, the source region's terminal destination remains the default even without its flag, and player labels no longer expose internal spawn identifiers.
- FLY now replaces the party/list diagnostic with a full-LCD regional map surface, draws every unlocked destination at its exported landmark coordinates, highlights the current cursor, and shows the selected town name.
- Confirming a FLY destination no longer opens the fabricated `<MON> used FLY!` textbox or requires a second acknowledgement. `FlyFunction` closes the map selection and enters `.FlyScript` directly; TypeScript does the same. Rust now retains the source map for the complete 128-frame `FlyFromAnim`, hides the ordinary player/NPC sprite layer, reconstructs the byte-wrapped bird-icon and repeatedly spawned leaf OAM functions, emits `SFX_FLY` at the source counter cadence, settles the selected spawn only between phases, and locks input through the destination map's 64-frame `FlyToAnim`. The renderer uses TypeScript's deterministic bird-icon stand-in; selecting the actual flying party species' icon remains dependent on exposing the source icon mapping at this presentation boundary.
- Active marts now render a dedicated full-LCD BUY/SELL surface with scrolling inventory, money, quantities, authoritative buy/sell prices, and compiled item descriptions instead of truncating the shop into the four-row field dialogue box.
- Mart A selection now enters the TypeScript quantity boundary instead of transacting one immediately: Up/Down adjust one, Left/Right adjust ten, affordability/carried limits cap the value, B returns to the list, and A commits the selected quantity with a visible total-price window.
- The displayed BUY maximum now applies the same stack and pocket-capacity boundary as the authoritative shop core before opening that prompt: Items/Balls/Key Items respect 20/12/25 occupied slots, existing stacks cap at 99, and Key Item/TM-HM shop stacks cap at one. A full pocket is refused visibly rather than offering an impossible quantity.
- Standard marts now enter through the explicit BUY / SELL / QUIT menu rather than an implicit BUY list with a hidden Left/Right mode toggle; B from either inventory returns to that menu and B/QUIT there closes the mart.
- Opening a standard mart now presents TypeScript's visible `Welcome! How may I help you?` acknowledgement before the BUY/SELL/QUIT menu accepts movement or selection; A/B dismisses only that greeting.
- Mart success and refusal outcomes now appear on the game surface with an acknowledgement arrow and block further menu movement until A/B, instead of existing only in the disabled host-debug status banner.
- Successful mart acknowledgements now use TypeScript's player-facing copy (`Here you are. Thank you!` and `Sold for ¥…!`) rather than exposing the internal item identifier and host transaction verb on the game screen.
- Battle FIGHT move details and battle PP-item move targeting now display TypeScript's move-definition PP denominator rather than expanding it with the saved PP-Up count; the same battle state no longer reports different visible PP maxima between implementations.
- Selecting a PP-targeting battle item on a Pokémon with no moves now produces the visible `It won't have any effect.` battle acknowledgement; it no longer closes targeting with feedback only in the disabled host banner.
- Player battle-item narration now matches TypeScript's shared boundary: it resolves the compiled catalog display name and prints `PLAYER used the ITEM.` for ordinary and escape items instead of exposing underscored identifiers with divergent punctuation.
- Ball throws now stage that same catalog-backed player item-use line before blocked, caught, or breakout capture text. Capture presentation no longer jumps directly to its result while omitting the TypeScript action narration.
- Reapplying a partial-trapping move to an already trapped target leaves the existing duration unchanged and emits no extra failure textbox, matching `BattleCommand_TrapTarget`; the ordinary move and damage presentation remains visible.
- Rapid Spin's trap-release message now resolves the compiled move display name instead of formatting the internal move identifier by replacing underscores.
- Partial-trap application, residual-damage, and release messages now use that same compiled move-name boundary for every non-special-cased trapping move.
- Failed RUN attempts now use TypeScript's canonical visible `Can't escape!` text rather than the divergent `<Pokémon> couldn't escape!` sentence.
- A trapped switch turn now uses Crystal's separate `<Pokémon> can't be recalled!` message; it no longer reuses the RUN-only `can't escape` wording.
- Residual narration remains ASM-authoritative where TypeScript simplifies it: poison/burn, Leech Seed, Curse, Nightmare, sandstorm, and partial-trap text use the exact Crystal sentence shapes, and Leech Seed healing changes HP without inventing TypeScript's extra `absorbed nutrients` textbox.
- Miss and exhausted-PP battle events now use the TypeScript/ASM named forms: `<user>'s attack missed!` and `<user> has no PP left for <move>!`, with the compiled move display name. The prior anonymous/invented sentences are removed.
- REST now uses Crystal's HP-only eligibility gate: full HP fails even with a status, while a damaged sleeping user can refresh REST. Its paired heal/status bookkeeping events coalesce into one source textbox: `<user> went to sleep!` with no prior status, or `<user> fell asleep and became healthy!` when curing an existing status. Rust no longer adds generic regained-health and fell-asleep boxes.
