- Move-menu refusal text now uses the separate ASM menu strings `The move is DISABLED!` and `There's no PP left for this move!`; it no longer conflates those pre-action boundaries with emitted turn-event narration.
- USE on a non-field-usable Pack item now opens TypeScript's visible `It won't have any effect.` acknowledgement over the Pack. A/B dismiss it and Select is blocked; the refusal no longer exists only in the disabled host banner.
- Runtime field-item refusals now use that same modal acknowledgement boundary, including party-target and registered-item paths. Directional, Start, and Select input cannot mutate the covered menu, and Pack teardown clears stale notice state.
- Successful field healing, revival, status/confusion curing, level gain, and stat-item use now project the authoritative mutation outcome into visible player-facing dialogue instead of immediately returning to the overworld with only a disabled host status.
- TM/HM compatibility refusals are now visible over the Pack and resolve compiled move display names; internal move identifiers no longer leak into the player-facing sentence.
- Transform, evolution, and egg-hatch presentation now uses Crystal's canonical species-name conversion (including FARFETCH'D, HO-OH, MR.MIME, and NIDORAN gender glyphs) rather than replacing underscores heuristically.
- Held escape-item narration now resolves the compiled item display name instead of exposing its internal identifier.
- Held escape-item staging now retains the canonical item ID until the final fled message; it no longer resolves the display name early and then mistakenly attempts to resolve that display string as another item ID.
- Repel expiry, egg hatching, and poison-step fainting now create blocking overworld dialogue using the TypeScript/ASM player-facing text. These step events no longer report only through the disabled host status banner.
- Party field-move refusals now remain on the party surface with visible ASM/TypeScript acknowledgement text (`Can't use SURF here.`, `There's nothing to CUT here.`, Sweet Scent's empty-area text, or the shared generic refusal) instead of disappearing into host status.
- Trainer victory completion now visibly reports `Sent some to MOM!` when the authoritative payout actually increases Mom's balance. Pay Day payout narration also matches TypeScript's shared `You picked up ¥…!` boundary and remains ordered after trainer prize text.
- Pokédex lists/entries, party summary species rows, gift presentation, and compact party summaries now share the canonical species-name formatter; special Crystal names no longer vary between battle and overworld screens.
- Bill's PC deposit mode remains TypeScript's direct party-selection flow, but last-party-member and full-box refusals now appear as blocking overlays on that party surface. Start cannot escape the modal boundary.
- Bill's PC withdrawal now displays the source `The party's full!` refusal on the storage surface when all six party slots are occupied, and an empty release surface reports `The BOX is empty.`. Both cases previously updated only the hidden host status even though the selected PC action remained active.
- Pack GIVE can no longer bypass the party menu's Egg restriction: every persisted Egg representation is refused visibly. Normal held-item TAKE-with-none and full-pocket GIVE/TAKE failures are now player-facing modal outcomes rather than disabled status or propagated runtime errors.
- Attempting the party-origin GIVE flow from a pocket whose entries cannot be held now shows a modal `That item can't be held.` acknowledgement instead of appearing to ignore the selection.
- Successful whole-party revival, PP restoration, Rare Candy level gain, and item evolution now produce blocking player-facing result dialogue. These effects previously consumed the item and changed state while exposing the outcome only through host status.
- Field result dialogue now has rendering priority over pending script and move-learning surfaces, so a Rare Candy's level result is actually acknowledged before the subsequent learn-move prompt instead of being cleared while hidden.
- A zero-wobble capture failure now names the active wild Pokémon, matching TypeScript's visible `Oh no! The <name> broke free!` text instead of substituting the generic word `POKéMON`.
- Successful Surf, Cut, Whirlpool, Strength, Flash, Waterfall, Fly, Dig, and Teleport actions now display the authoritative party actor's `<nickname> used <MOVE>!` acknowledgement. Map mutation/travel no longer occurs with confirmation confined to host status.
- Headbutt, Rock Smash, and Sweet Scent now preserve the same use acknowledgement whether or not they start a wild battle. Encounter-producing uses prepend it to battle entry rather than losing it when battle preparation resets the message queue.
- Silent visible battle mutations now retarget the preceding textbox scene just like ordinary neutral damage. Counter/Bide damage, Future Sight damage, self-destruct HP loss, Leech Seed healing, and similar no-extra-text effects animate the HUD before dialogue clears instead of jumping afterward.
- A/B input priority now matches notice rendering priority. A visible field/PC result is dismissed before covered text-speed, phone, or move-learning state can advance, preventing an unseen prompt from being accepted or cancelled through the overlay.
- Left/right/up/down now obey that same modal ownership before TM/HM, phone, yes/no, move-learning, Start-menu, storage, or party cursors are considered; covered selections cannot drift behind a notice.
- Selecting SELL with no sellable inventory now opens TypeScript's visible `You don't have anything to sell.` acknowledgement; it no longer appears to ignore A because the only feedback was written to a disabled debug banner.
- Dismissing a BUY/SELL success or refusal now returns to the top mart menu through TypeScript's `Can I do anything else for you?` acknowledgement instead of silently leaving the player inside the inventory list.
- QUIT or B from the top mart menu now stages TypeScript's `Please come again!` acknowledgement and closes/resumes the overworld script only after A/B dismisses it.
- B from a BUY/SELL list now exits that sub-flow and stages `Can I do anything else for you?`, while B from its quantity selector returns to the same list, matching TypeScript's nested menu boundaries.
- Bill's PC withdraw/move mode now owns the full LCD with box name/count, seven-row scrolling slot list, nickname/level, party capacity, and the active WITHDRAW/RELEASE or PLACE controls instead of exposing box contents through four truncated dialogue rows.
- Bill's PC RELEASE no longer deletes the selected boxed Pokémon immediately on Select. It now captures the exact box/slot, opens a visible ASM-defaulted YES/NO confirmation, blocks selection drift, and mutates storage only after explicit YES, matching TypeScript's outer PC release boundary.
- Box browsing now opens a visible per-Pokémon action menu with A; Select no longer acts as a hidden destructive shortcut. B closes the action menu, and RELEASE proceeds into the separate confirmation.
- The boxed-Pokémon action menu now has TypeScript's full WITHDRAW/STATS/RELEASE/CANCEL order. STATS retains the exact box/slot and opens a three-page full-screen summary for health/item/experience, moves/PP, and OT/battle stats; A cycles pages and B returns without changing storage.
- Canonical ITEM, BALL, KEY ITEM, and PC item storage now preserves the source slot order instead of alphabetizing through `BTreeMap`. ITEM/BALL/PC additions fill every existing matching stack in order and append duplicate stacks when quantity remains; removals target the selected stack and never spill across duplicates. Save and runtime snapshots retain those duplicate entries, and PC transfers carry the cursor's stack index so an identically named later stack cannot mutate the first one.
- TM/HM additions and removals now reject overflow/underflow atomically at 99. Key-item receive/toss follows its separate ASM representation: receive appends one byte regardless of the generic quantity register and toss removes one selected/matching entry.
- `MovePcPokemonWithoutMail` now implements all four source jump-table paths (`BoxToBox`, `PartyToBox`, `BoxToParty`, and `PartyToParty`) with insertion-cursor adjustment in the same container, destination-capacity checks, party blackout prevention, and mail rejection on the selected party Pokémon. Every insertion into a box—including ordinary DEPOSIT—restores move PP from the compiled move table as `RestorePPOfDepositedPokemon` does.
- The authoritative boxed-release mutation now rejects the typed `is_egg` representation of the ASM party-species `EGG` sentinel. Egg nickname/species string aliases were removed so malformed split-authority states cannot enter storage behavior.
- Boxed RELEASE now checks Egg and held-Mail restrictions before opening confirmation and presents the TypeScript refusal text on the game surface. Empty-box and successful-release outcomes are also visible acknowledgement boundaries instead of disabled debug-banner messages.
- PC item withdrawal now has a full-LCD seven-row storage list with quantities and compiled descriptions; depositing still reuses the complete Pack surface and returns to this storage view.
- Bill's PC CHANGE BOX now uses a full-LCD seven-row scrolling box catalog with names and occupancy counts instead of hiding most of the fourteen boxes behind the four-row dialogue viewport.
- Repel, Escape Rope, and Itemfinder success now resolve their exported ASM text labels onto the visible field surface. Fishing shows the source no-bite or bite acknowledgement after its retained cast animation and before any wild-battle entry.
- Battle item turns now visibly acknowledge HP recovery, revival, status/confusion cures, PP restoration, and battle-stat/focus effects before the opponent response instead of mutating those values behind the generic item-use line.
- Battle-item stat acknowledgements now use Crystal's `StatNames` strings, including `SPCL.ATK`/`SPCL.DEF`, rather than exposing normalized internal identifiers such as `SPECIAL ATTACK`; unknown stat IDs remain visibly invalid.
- Successful TM/HM teaching now presents the selected Pokémon and compiled move display name, and opening either trophy box presents the exported `SentTrophyHomeText` instead of silently closing the Pack after mutation.
- Pokémon picture labels and gift-selection rows now use canonical species and held-item display names; Eggs render as `EGG` instead of leaking internal species/item identifiers onto the game surface.
- Successful party GIVE, held-item replacement, and TAKE now display the source-equivalent Pokémon/item acknowledgement using compiled display names instead of silently mutating the party behind diagnostic status.
- Replacing a held item now pauses at the ASM `PokemonAskSwapItemText` boundary with a retained YES/NO cursor. YES performs the existing atomic return-and-replace mutation; NO/B leaves the Pokémon, Bag, selected replacement, and party target unchanged.
- Held MAIL now blocks replacement before the swap question with `Please remove the MAIL first.` at both the visible menu and authoritative mutation boundary, matching `TryGiveItemToPartymon` instead of returning attached MAIL to the Bag as an ordinary item.
- Field Pack action menus now derive `USE/GIVE/TOSS/SEL/QUIT` from the selected item's compiled `CANT_TOSS`, `CANT_SELECT`, and field usability attributes, matching `ItemSubmenu` instead of assigning one action set to an entire pocket. Battle Pack uses its separate ASM submenu and never exposes field-only `GIVE`, `TOSS`, or `SEL` actions.
- Field Pack `TOSS` now retains the selected item through Crystal's quantity selector and separate YES/NO confirmation, removes exactly the confirmed quantity, and displays `Threw away ITEM(S).`; Up/Down change one and wrap at the ends, Left/Right clamp ten at a time, and cancelling either flow preserves inventory.
- TOSS now reclamps the active pocket cursor after removing a depleted stack, preventing an out-of-range retained row from blanking the Pack when its acknowledgement closes.
- Pack `SEL` now registers the item whose action menu is actually open instead of reading an unrelated retained key-item cursor, and both outcomes use the exact blocking Crystal text (`Registered the ITEM.` / `You can't register that item.`) rather than debug status alone.
- Held-item transfer remains field-Pack-only as in ASM; the swap renderer no longer performs an unnecessary replacement-item lookup, and Pack teardown clears any retained toss transaction.
- Held Mail now has a saveable, validated ten-message mailbox and the party ITEM flow follows `MonMailAction`: READ / TAKE / QUIT, the “send to PC?” and destructive-message confirmations, atomic mailbox transfer, atomic Mail-item return to the Bag, and the source mailbox-full / Bag-full refusal outcomes. Player PC now opens the source WITHDRAW ITEM / DEPOSIT ITEM / TOSS ITEM / MAIL BOX / LOG OFF surface instead of jumping directly into PC items. MAIL BOX lists authors and exposes ASM's READ MAIL / PUT IN PACK / ATTACH MAIL / CANCEL actions; each transfer is atomic, attaching refuses Eggs and occupied held-item slots, and deleting/putting away compacts the mailbox.
- The bedroom PC now uses TypeScript's separate six-row WITHDRAW / DEPOSIT / TOSS / MAIL BOX / DECORATION / TURN OFF menu, while Pokémon Center access retains its five-row menu and LOG OFF wording. DECORATION now opens ASM's pack-backed owned-category and owned-item menus, including PUT IT AWAY, CANCEL, ornament-side selection, exact setup/replacement/removal notices, and the changed-decoration carry result that closes the PC through `PlayersHousePCScript`'s bad-warp room reload.
- Player-PC item-list instructions and cancellation retain the selected top-level operation: TOSS displays `A TOSS ITEM` rather than claiming A will withdraw, and B returns to TOSS instead of moving the cursor to WITHDRAW. WITHDRAW retains its own label and return row. WITHDRAW, DEPOSIT, and TOSS now all enter the visible quantity mode at one; Up/Right increment, Down/Left decrement, values clamp to the selected stack, B returns without mutation, and A carries the exact amount into the atomic transfer. TOSS then preserves ASM `TossItemFromPC`'s separate destructive confirmation before removal; this deliberately corrects the current TypeScript simplification that commits immediately from quantity.
- Player-PC transfers now display ASM-shaped `Withdrew N ITEM(S).`, `Deposited N ITEM(S).`, and `Discarded ITEM(S).` result text instead of status-log-only mutations. Destination stack/slot capacity is checked after the chosen quantity, producing the visible PACK/PC-full refusal without a partial transfer. PC TOSS checks the compiled `CANT_TOSS` property before quantity selection and the authoritative mutation repeats that guard, so important items cannot be removed through a non-visible API.
- Player-PC WITHDRAW/TOSS with empty storage and DEPOSIT with an empty Pack now display ASM's exact `No items here!` acknowledgement and retain the selected top-level PC row. These ordinary empty-list choices previously depended on hidden host status or used invented `There are no items…` wording.
- Mail identity no longer uses `item_id.contains("MAIL")`, which incorrectly classified arbitrary mod items and made save legality depend on spelling. Rust now uses the exact ten-entry ASM `MailItems` table, and saved party/box/mailbox records reject half-attached or held-item/type-mismatched Mail states. Bag Mail enters `_ComposeMailMessage`'s dedicated 20×18 LCD: the exact ten-column uppercase/lowercase grids, blank-space keys, three grouped command keys, wrapping cursor, SELECT case toggle, B delete, START-to-END behavior, and fixed 16+16-character message are controlled only through Game Boy inputs. END commits one atomic v42 compose-and-attach mutation after the existing Egg, attached-Mail, replacement-confirmation, and Bag-capacity gates. Saved Mail now retains the full source identity tuple—message, author, nationality, author ID, species, and Mail type—so mailbox and party transfers cannot synthesize it later.
- `checkpokemail` no longer searches the whole party and silently removes the first matching carrier. The visible script boundary now opens the party screen and evaluates exactly the chosen Pokémon with the ASM/TypeScript result values (`WRONG_MAIL`, `CORRECT`, `REFUSED`, `NO_MAIL`, `LAST_MON`); cancellation is preserved, non-Mail and wrong-message outcomes are distinct, and successful delivery alone compacts the party. This restores the Route 31 Kenya/Spearow handoff path.
- NPC trades no longer exchange the first matching species automatically or mark the persistent trade flag in the generic script opcode. An unfinished trade now presents its dialog-set-specific ASM intro with an ASM-defaulted YES/NO choice, opens the visible party selector only after YES, evaluates exactly the selected Pokémon (including source MALE/FEMALE/EITHER matching), and records completion only after the selected monster is removed, the remaining party is compacted, and the compiled offered Pokémon is appended as ASM does. A valid selection now stops at the source `NPCTradeCableText` prompt with the party and `wTradeFlags` equivalent unchanged; acknowledging it commits the exchange, then presents `Text_NPCTraded` and the exact dialog-set completion page in source order. The visible text formatter preserves `GetTradeMonNames`' exact buffer ownership: GIRL intros and completions name the requested Pokémon with its required gender glyph rather than the offered Pokémon, GIRL after-text retains its second requested-Pokémon paragraph, and HAPPY after-text names the species the player actually traded away. NO/B, party cancellation, wrong species, successful completion, and repeat interaction each show their dialog-set-specific source text without unintended mutation; completed trades do not reopen selection. The exported graphics are present, but `TradeAnimation`'s full-screen program and its exact music/fanfare waits remain a presentation gap between the commit and `Text_NPCTraded`.
- Party field-move actions now follow the Pokémon's learned move-slot order before STATS/SWITCH/MOVE/ITEM, matching `GetMonSubmenuItems`; Rust no longer reorders them through a shell-preferred field-move list or changes the eight-entry cap outcome.
- SOFTBOILED and MILK DRINK now appear in that learned-move order and open Crystal's party-recipient loop. The source must have at least `floor(max HP / 5)` HP; self, Egg, fainted, and full-health targets show the source refusal and remain selectable. A valid choice atomically subtracts the full one-fifth cost, restores the recipient with max-HP capping, reports the actual recovered HP, and returns the cursor to the move user.
- Escape Rope now closes the Pack and displays the exported `UseEscapeRopeText` on the source map before destination arrival is settled; the registered/alternate use path no longer omits that acknowledgement. A/B dismissal then processes destination map load, callbacks, and music.
- Fly and Dig now preserve their source-map use acknowledgement until A/B before settling destination arrival. Teleport uses Crystal's `Return to the last #MON CENTER.` text instead of the invented `<nickname> used TELEPORT!` line, holds it for the source's 60-frame pause while ignoring dismissal input, and then closes it and settles arrival automatically.
- Itemfinder now follows `ItemFinder::.ItemfinderSound` through all four ordered
  `SFX_SECOND_PART_OF_ITEMFINDER` / `SFX_TRANSACTION` pairs. A typed
  `WaitPlaySFX` sequencer waits for any already-active transient channel before
  promoting the first cue, then promotes exactly one cue after each preceding
  sound finishes, so the generic same-frame transient coalescer cannot erase
  the loop. Both Pack and registered-item entry paths withhold the exact
  `_ItemfinderItemNearbyText` page until the eighth wait completes; the no-item
  path remains silent and uses `_ItemfinderNopeText` immediately. Navigation
  resets clear unfinished typed sequences instead of leaking cues across maps.
- Fishing battle entry no longer invents a pre-bite `<PLAYER> used a FISHING ROD!` textbox. The authoritative ASM cast sequence proceeds directly to `Oh! A bite!` after its rod animation and pauses, and the no-bite result uses ASM's `Not even a nibble!` punctuation instead of TypeScript's ellipsis. TypeScript's extra use-text helper is not source-authentic; Rust now carries the cast/hook timing, shock emote, rod graphic, and direction-specific player fishing tiles.
- Battle HP palette selection now uses ASM `GetHPPal`'s inclusive thresholds: a 48-pixel bar is green from 24 pixels upward, yellow from 10 through 23, and red below 10. Rust previously colored exactly 24 yellow and exactly 10 red, changing both persistent HUD boundaries one pixel late.
- The battle `HP:` label tiles now share the active side HP palette with their bar, matching TypeScript's row attr assignment and Crystal's palette slot. Rust previously rendered those two tiles in a fixed invented blue while the adjacent bar changed red/yellow/green.
- The visible FIGHT move-detail panel now derives maximum PP from the learned move's stored PP Up stages through the shared authoritative calculation. It previously displayed compiled base PP as the denominator even while the battle engine and other Rust move surfaces honored the increased maximum.
- Missing compiled metadata in the visible FIGHT detail panel is now an explicit `INVALID MOVE` state. Rust previously disguised broken export/save references as a valid `NONE`-type move whose current PP was also presented as its maximum, violating the no-fallback data boundary.
- The text/accessibility FIGHT surface now reads the effective `battle.player_moves` list just like the bitmap menu and command authority. It previously reread the saved party record, so Transform could display its temporary moves in the game canvas but the original moves in the parallel visible command representation.
- The battle party action submenu now uses Crystal/TypeScript order `STATS`, `SWITCH`, `CANCEL`, with execution indices changed alongside the labels. Rust previously defaulted to and displayed `SWITCH` first, changing the source cursor contract and making the same input choose a different action.
- The battle Pack PP-target screen now includes stored PP Up stages in each maximum and displays `PP INVALID` when compiled move metadata is absent. It previously showed base PP for enhanced moves and silently converted a missing move export into maximum PP zero.
- Field party summaries, move reordering, retained battle summaries, and battle Pack targeting now share one PP display boundary. All honor PP Ups and expose missing compiled move metadata as `PP INVALID` instead of independently manufacturing `/0` denominators.
- START-menu availability now follows Crystal's state rather than host configuration: link mode removes PACK and SAVE, Bug Contest mode removes PACK and replaces SAVE with QUIT, and ordinary play always includes SAVE. Rust previously left PACK visible in link mode and omitted SAVE whenever the optional Bevy save path field was absent, changing the source menu layout for a host concern.
- OPTION input now preserves ASM button roles: LEFT/RIGHT change values but do nothing on CANCEL, while A does nothing on value rows and exits only from CANCEL. Rust previously routed A and RIGHT through the same positive-delta function, so A unexpectedly changed settings and RIGHT on CANCEL unexpectedly closed the menu.
- START now exits OPTION from any row, matching ASM's `PAD_START | PAD_B` exit boundary. Rust previously let START fall through to the overworld START-menu toggle while the modal options surface remained open.
- PRINT brightness now cycles in ASM order `LIGHTEST`, `LIGHTER`, `NORMAL`, `DARKER`, `DARKEST`. Rust's ring began with `NORMAL`, making RIGHT from NORMAL wrap to LIGHTEST instead of advancing to DARKER.
- Existing-file SAVE now preserves ASM's two confirmations: `Would you like to save the game?` is always first, and YES advances to `There is already a save file. Is it OK to overwrite?` before any write. Rust and the current TypeScript path previously collapsed these into the overwrite prompt alone.
- A successful menu SAVE now queues the exact typed `SFX_SAVE` event when the saved confirmation becomes visible. Rust previously completed the write and displayed the success text silently. ASM's surrounding 16/32/30-frame delays remain part of the open transient presentation scheduler work.
- Trainer Card page 1 now clears the exact two-row Pokédex/status band until `ENGINE_POKEDEX` is active, matching ASM and TypeScript. Rust previously printed `#DEX 0` before the player had received the Pokédex; the parallel text surface exposed the same premature row.
- Trainer Card money now places `¥` immediately before the first printed digit inside the six-digit field, matching TypeScript and ASM `PRINTNUM_MONEY`; Rust previously pinned the symbol to the far-left edge.
- Trainer Card A/RIGHT now transition from the information page to the source Johto badge page; LEFT returns and A exits. The badge page uses the shipped leader portraits, owned-badge sprites, trainer/badge palettes, source positions, horizontal flips, and the absolute-VBlank-gated eight-frame animation sequence instead of closing the card immediately. The unreferenced ASM Kanto state is intentionally not exposed.
- Trainer Card's play-time colon now follows ASM's absolute 32-VBlank toggle (`hVBlankCounter % 64 >= 32`) instead of Rust's invented 30-on/30-off cadence derived from a frozen gameplay snapshot. It continues blinking while the card pauses overworld play.
- `hVBlankCounter` is now distinct wrapping HRAM state rather than an alias for gameplay-command or host-render frames. VBlank_Normal advances it in the same replayable VBlank mutation as hRandom, cutscene/credits handlers leave it unchanged, ResetWRAM preserves it, and Continue retains the live title value plus DIV/hRandom stream instead of restoring those hardware registers from SRAM. Text prompt arrows, Trainer Card absolute phase, Mom's selected bank digit, and the Unown Puzzle cursor now read its source bits; ordinary frontpic/trainer-slide frames keep advancing while BattleAnim-backed frames freeze them.
- Cleared roaming slots now use ResetWRAM's exact `$ff/$ff` map bytes at the `RoamingPokemonState` construction boundary. Saved-state and catalog validation reject the invented `0/0` pre-initialization fallback; only `$ff/$ff` before `InitializeWorld` or the pack's exact inactive map afterward is accepted.
- Bicycle toggling now updates authoritative current music to `MUSIC_BICYCLE` when mounting and restores the compiled current-map track when dismounting. The Bevy music synchronizer waits until the visible field acknowledgement closes, matching TypeScript's `finishBicycleUse` ordering instead of changing tracks before the player has seen the result.
- Overworld music selection is now movement-mode aware at the shared transition boundary: Surf uses `MUSIC_SURF`, Bike uses `MUSIC_BICYCLE`, and Normal/Skate use the compiled map track. Initial Surf, preserved-mode connections/transitions, fresh starts, and save resumes no longer silently select the ordinary map song. Movement tracks are validated against the pack audio catalog where transition callers provide it.
- Successful Surf keeps rendering the source tile while `<nickname> used SURF!` is acknowledged; the already-authoritative step into water and Surf sprite/music become visible only after dismissal. Previously the shell dirtied its snapshot immediately and showed the player in the water behind the pre-movement text.
- Surf now selects a persisted typed `SurfPika` movement mode when the actual chosen Surf user is PIKACHU, matching `GetSurfType`/TypeScript rather than collapsing both source states into generic Surf. SurfPika shares water traversal, dismount, Waterfall, contextual-field-move, and `MUSIC_SURF` behavior while remaining distinct in saves and snapshots.
- The overworld renderer now selects the source player sprite table by gender and movement mode: `chris`/`kris`, `chris_bike`/`kris_bike`, shared `surf`, or `surfing_pikachu`, with Surf blue and Surfing Pikachu red palette ownership. It also keys retained player entities by movement mode, so mounting in place cannot incorrectly retain the walking texture.
- Surf/SurfPika movement now executes Crystal's `GetOutOfWater` boundary when entering a land collision: mode changes to Normal before the landing step is reported, the renderer returns to the gendered walking sprite, and the authoritative frame path restores compiled map music. Rust previously allowed the landing but remained indefinitely in Surf mode with Surf music and art.
- Map entry now runs the source `CheckUpdatePlayerSprite` mode precedence before music/rendering: `ENGINE_ALWAYS_ON_BIKE` forces Bike, water arrival selects or retains Surf/SurfPika, Surf arriving on land normalizes, and Bike normalizes in environments that prohibit it. Warps, connections, new starts, fixture starts, and save resumes share this boundary instead of carrying impossible indoor/water movement art and music.
- Held-item replacement YES/NO now actually owns directional input while its question text is visible. The general field-notice guard previously ran first in Left, Right, and Up/Down routing, so the rendered cursor could not move.
- Contextual A-button Surf, Waterfall, Cut, and Headbutt now stage their exported ASM question text and a real YES/NO cursor. NO/B closes without mutation; YES enters the same authoritative move path used by the party menu. Rust previously skipped `AskSurfScript` and its peer prompts and mutated immediately.
- Cut, Whirlpool, Flash, Surf, and Waterfall no longer dirty the live or rendered world before their `<nickname> used <MOVE>!` acknowledgement is dismissed. Waterfall additionally commits one upward runtime tile per exported `.loop@Script_UsedWaterfall` `applymovement`, samples the new live collision through `.CheckContinueWaterfall`, and requires the source `wScriptVar` to remain zero until the final non-Waterfall tile returns one.
- Whirlpool now retains its 32-frame post-text field-animation boundary instead of snapping directly from the intact obstruction to its replacement block. Rust composes TypeScript's fixed Johto metatile `$07`, substitutes the four exported `$32/$33/$42/$43` whirlpool VRAM tiles from `1.2bpp` through `4.2bpp`, advances their four phases on the tile-animation cadence, applies the source tileset palettes, and reveals the authoritative replacement only when the animation ends; the existing `SFX_SURF` cue remains attached to the start of that boundary.
- Waterfall no longer reveals the core's already-committed terminal tile immediately after the use acknowledgement. Rust retains the pre-travel scene, plays `SFX_BUBBLEBEAM`, derives every upward segment from the authoritative `from_tile`/`steps`/`to_tile` outcome, and advances the surf player plus scrolling viewport at TypeScript's half-walk (bike-speed) four-frame cadence before releasing the destination snapshot. Input remains locked across the complete climb, and a path that does not end at the authoritative tile is surfaced as a runtime fidelity error rather than silently snapped.
- Flash now honors the exceptional ASM `UseFlashTextScript` boundary: after the use line finishes printing, Rust automatically queues `SFX_FLASH` and begins the 16-frame white-out/reveal sequence instead of incorrectly waiting for an A press. The retained dark scene is released at peak white so the lit map appears during the fade back in, matching the TypeScript ordering and ASM `BlindingFlash` transition.
- Active Strength now preserves the source's split player/object scheduling.
  `CheckStrengthBoulder` sets `BOULDER_MOVING_F` plus the direction but returns
  player movement code 2, which `.TryStep` deliberately treats as a bump; the
  player does not enter the vacated tile in the push frame. The later object
  scheduler consumes that request without an invented facing turn, commits
  the boulder's live destination, retains its old tile for the complete
  16-frame slow step, and emits `SFX_STRENGTH` only when movement actually
  starts. `SPRITEMOVEDATA_STRENGTH_BOULDER`, `BIGDOLLASYM`, and `BIGDOLL` use
  `MovementFunction_Strength`; `BIGDOLLSYM` has the palette flag and accepts
  the request but intentionally never consumes it through its BigStanding
  movement function.
- `StepFunction_StrengthBoulder` now owns the landing boundary. Map-object
  coordinates remain at their pre-push values during all 16 frames, then copy
  from the live struct when the step lands and `OBJECT_LAST_MAP_X/Y` normalize
  to the destination. The CMDQUEUE stone-table scan runs before player events
  on the following overworld frame, requires an exact standing
  `SPRITEMOVEDATA_STRENGTH_BOULDER` on `COLL_PIT`/`COLL_PIT_68`, and only then
  queues the matching warp/object script. Rust previously evaluated the stone
  table in the push call before either the slow step or landing existed.
- Pending `BOULDER_MOVING_F` direction and active Strength-step state are now
  part of the current object-struct save image. Continue retains the exact
  remaining duration and old-tile collision interval; malformed saves with a
  conflicting phase, wrong movement function, or unequal step/collision
  durations are rejected.
- `LoadMovementDataPointer` now applies its source `STEP_TYPE_RESET` ownership
  before an `applymovement` body runs. Scripted movement cancels an in-flight
  Strength duration, normalizes `OBJECT_LAST_MAP_X/Y`, and releases the old
  collision tile while deliberately retaining the independent flags2
  `BOULDER_MOVING_F` request.
- Strength stone tables are now retained for the lifetime of their map rather than drained as diagnostic script records. A boulder pushed onto a matching warp queues the exported table target with its original map context, allowing Ice Path and Blackthorn Gym landing scripts to disappear the source object, update event flags, animate the fall, and show their compiled dialogue. The table is cleared before the next map's callbacks install its own entries.
- Enemy battle-item narration now follows TypeScript's shared event boundary (`<active enemy nickname> used <item>!`). Rust previously replaced it with an invented three-line trainer-name/target sentence that did not match either the emitted action actor or the TypeScript LCD text.
- WATERFALL now performs TypeScript's terminal landing step: after climbing consecutive waterfall collisions, it may enter the first passable non-wall tile and then stops because that tile is no longer a waterfall. Rust previously rejected every non-water destination before moving, leaving the player on the final waterfall collision instead of completing the ascent.
- WATERFALL now stages `SFX_BUBBLEBEAM` at the source boundary after its use textbox is dismissed. The visible shell previously omitted the sound entirely; the deferred notice-audio slot prevents it from playing early behind `UseWaterfallText`.
- WHIRLPOOL now uses that same proven post-text audio boundary for `SFX_SURF`, matching TypeScript's `_run_whirlpool_animation` and ASM `PlayWhirlpoolSound`. CUT now retains a distinct 32-frame post-text presentation state and queues `SFX_PLACE_PUZZLE_PIECE_DOWN`, matching TypeScript `_run_cut_animation` and `OWCutAnimation`; it does not reuse Rock Smash's earthquake effect or the unrelated battle-move `SFX_CUT`. Tree variants load the shipped four-tile `cut_tree.2bpp`, replay the packed TREE_1/CUT_TREE_2/3/4 OAM cadence, and use the source facing offsets. Grass variants load `cut_grass.2bpp` and render four `PAL_OW_TREE` leaves from the parity-sensitive spawn table with the source three-angle/two-byte-amplitude sine/cosine expansion. The use text retains the pre-action scene, then animation presentation switches to the authoritative post-replacement tilemap so the intact tree cannot leak behind the split OAM; the state clears only after the 32-frame boundary.
- HEADBUTT now enters the exported `HeadbuttFromMenuScript` instead of using a parallel eager field-battle command. Menu dispatch only records `wCurPartyMon`; the source `GetPartyNickname`, `UseHeadbuttText`, `refreshmap`, and 32-frame `ShakeHeadbuttTree` boundary run before the exact `TreeMonEncounter` callasm may consume RNG. The animation queues `SFX_SANDSTORM`, masks the four background tree tiles with the tileset's required tile `$05`, loads the shipped eight-tile `headbutt_tree.2bpp` with `PAL_OW_TREE`, and replays the packed TREE_1/HEADBUTT_TREE_2/reverse-X frameset at the source facing location. Misses then follow `NoTreeMonScript`; hits reach the script's ordinary `randomwildmon`/`startbattle` continuation. The old direct field-encounter battle materializer is deleted.
- Contextual A-button WHIRLPOOL now opens the exported `AskWhirlpoolText` YES/NO boundary before mutation, matching ASM `Script_AskWhirlpoolOW`; NO/B leaves the map unchanged. TypeScript currently skips this source prompt, so ASM is authoritative for this edge.
- The Smash Rock object script now opens ASM's exported `AskRockSmashText` YES/NO boundary before breaking the object or rolling an encounter, and shows `MaySmashText` when requirements are unmet. Rust's generic standard-script fallback could display a prompt but had no continuation capable of executing ROCK SMASH.
- Strength boulder A-button interaction now enters the object's exported `StrengthBoulderScript -> AskStrengthScript` control flow instead of a Rust-side collision preemption. The compiled `TryStrengthOW` callasm chooses the already-active, unmet-requirements, or `AskStrengthText` YES/NO branch; YES jumps directly to `Script_UsedStrength`, while party-menu use enters `Script_StrengthFromMenu` as in ASM.
- Contextual field-move prompts now retain the first eligible party member selected by ASM `CheckPartyMove`. A stale cursor from an earlier party-menu visit can no longer silently choose a later Pokémon for Surf, Cut, Strength, Whirlpool, Waterfall, Rock Smash, or Headbutt; explicit party-menu use still honors the selected member.
- Encounter-producing HEADBUTT, ROCK SMASH, and SWEET SCENT now retain the pre-mutation overworld while their use acknowledgement is visible and prepare the battle only after dismissal. Rust previously opened the battle first and prepended the field text over the battle scene. ROCK SMASH also queues `SFX_STRENGTH`, runs ASM's 20-frame `earthquake 84` shake, retains the rock through the following 10-frame `StepFunction_RockSmash` standing/action alternation, and only then exposes removal or battle.
- SWEET SCENT's no-encounter path now preserves both source pages: the selected Pokémon first “used SWEET SCENT!”, then `SweetScentNothingText` reports that there is nothing here. This applies both when an eligible grass/water surface has a zero/failed encounter selection and when `CanEncounterWildMon` would reject the player's current terrain; the latter does not consume encounter RNG or skip directly to the refusal. Rust deliberately does not import TypeScript's extra fade/`SFX_SWEET_SCENT` delay because `engine/events/sweet_scent.asm` contains neither.
- Field-move acknowledgements now own an explicit retained overworld scene rather than relying on cache invalidation order. Surf, Cut, Whirlpool, Flash, Waterfall, Headbutt, Rock Smash, and Sweet Scent therefore keep their source map, lighting, movement mode, and objects through their scripted text/effect boundaries. Their authoritative mutations occur at the corresponding exported command rather than being made early and merely hidden by the retained scene. Strength now uses the ordinary compiled-script presentation path described below.
- Rock Smash now retains the exact object tile across the committed map mutation, shakes the retained scene for ASM's first 20 frames, alternates that object's standing/action frames for the final 10 frames using `StepFunction_RockSmash` parity, and only then reveals the removed object or deferred battle. Missing required action art is surfaced instead of replaced with a static fallback.
- New-map transitions now clear the three fields owned by ASM `ResetBikeFlags` (`ENGINE_STRENGTH_ACTIVE`, `ENGINE_ALWAYS_ON_BIKE`, and `ENGINE_DOWNHILL`) before destination callbacks, matching TypeScript's shared map-manager boundary. Movement mode is checked again after callbacks so a destination callback can authoritatively re-enable always-on-bike behavior; stale Strength no longer leaks between maps.
- Tutorial battle actor text now displays `DUDE`, including the command prompt and item/Ball use messages. TypeScript and ASM temporarily replace `wPlayerName` with Dude's name for the demonstration; Rust retains the player's saved identity internally, so the visible layer applies that source-scoped name without corrupting the save.
- Script `_2dmenu` is now exported through the same typed selectable-menu path as `verticalmenu`, retaining its authoritative coordinates, row/column declaration, and column spacing. The overworld shell renders the source-positioned grid, gives Left/Right column movement and Up/Down row movement with wrapping, commits A as Crystal's one-based `wScriptVar`, and commits B as zero before resuming the compiled script. The Academy blackboard no longer strands play at a diagnostic `Menu2D` boundary or substitutes the generic bottom dialogue panel.
- Script menus now own B input ahead of the dialogue box beneath them. Rust previously prioritized `text_window_open`, so cancelling a menu created inside `opentext` could close the underlying text surface while leaving the selectable menu alive; menu cancellation now closes/resumes the actual modal boundary and writes Crystal's zero cancellation result to `wScriptVar`.
- `checkver` now resolves to ASM's `GS_VERSION = 0`, matching TypeScript's explicit Crystal-content branch. Rust previously injected the host modpack identifier into `wScriptVar` and raised a diagnostic VersionCheck boundary, which could derail Bill's grandfather after the Water Stone request instead of continuing to Growlithe.
- Successful `giveitem`, `verbosegiveitem`, and Mystery Gift delivery now retain their typed item grant through `itemnotify` and display Crystal's `<PLAYER> put the <ITEM> in the <POCKET>.` acknowledgement with the catalog-derived ITEM/KEY/BALL/TM pocket name. Rust previously consumed `itemnotify` silently after the received-item text and sound, omitting a visible source textbox used throughout story rewards.
- `giveitem`, `verbosegiveitem`, `checkitem`, and `takeitem` now write their exact success/failure result to the script accumulator before a following conditional, matching `Script_giveitem`, `Script_checkitem`, and `Script_takeitem`. This prevents stale true results from entering unrelated branches such as Mr. Pokemon's Red Scale trade after the first Pokedex visit. `verbosegiveitemvar` now also resolves its authored quantity variable, grants the complete reward, and calls the standard receive/full-pocket script before returning to the source command after all seven Kurt Ball rewards.
- Wild and trainer battle entry now retain the gendered player backpic during `Go! <MON>!` and reveal the player Pokémon sprite, HP HUD, and EXP bar only after that textbox is dismissed. Rust previously replaced the trainer with the Pokémon one acknowledgement too early, making the send-out result visible before its source action in both battle kinds.
- Battle-entry cries now follow those same source boundaries: the enemy cries at wild appearance or trainer send-out, and the player's Pokémon cries only after `Go! <MON>!` is dismissed. Rust previously enqueued both cries together at battle creation and keyed the intro scheduler to active-enemy identity, allowing the pair to replay on later enemy replacements despite dedicated switch cries already existing.
- Battle HUD level alignment now follows ASM `PrintPlayerHUD` / `DrawEnemyHUD`: a genderless battler's level marker shifts one tile left, while status text retains its fixed three-tile position.
- `blackoutmod` now updates the typed last-spawn identifier and `wLastSpawnMapGroup` / `wLastSpawnMapNumber`, then resumes the script as ASM does. Story travel through Mr. Pokémon's house, both ports, and the Fast Ship no longer stops at a diagnostic runtime boundary, and a later whiteout uses the declared destination.
- Elm's Lab `NameRival` now opens Rust's real ASM-shaped naming screen, applies the entered seven-character name (or ASM's `SILVER` default for blank input), and resumes the officer script. Normal play previously failed because the Bevy shell required an externally injected `_rival_name` variable and replaced the source interaction with a special boundary.
- `InitRoamMons` now remains a state-only Burned Tower script step after initializing Raikou, Entei, and Suicune, matching ASM and TypeScript. Rust previously interrupted the release scene with a synthetic diagnostic screen before its remaining map/event mutations.
- State-query specials used by ordinary scripts (`GameboyCheck`, first-party happiness/egg/species/level checks, `MonCheck`, `BeastsCheck`, `CheckCaughtCelebi`, and `CheckPokerus`) now return their already-computed `wScriptVar` result directly to the script. Rust previously invented a visible diagnostic screen for each source-silent predicate before allowing its branch to run.
- Additional source-silent state routines now remain invisible: random phone/wild selections, fishing-swarm activation, player palette mutation, Snorlax's Poké Flute/proximity check, DST/time updates, and Lucky Number/timer bookkeeping. Their Rust mutations were already authoritative; only the synthetic debug interruption was removed.
- Party/gift routine results now return directly to their source scripts after the shell's selection or mutation: Shuckie give/return, Dratini moveset, Bill's grandfather selection, Kurt's Apricorn choice, Odd Egg, and Mystery Gift check/unlock/delivery. Rust previously inserted a debug outcome screen before the scripts' canonical success, refusal, party-full, or received-item text.
- Bug-Catching Contest setup/teardown no longer opens debug screens while assigning PARK BALLs/timer/contestants, masking or restoring the party, or placing the caught contest Pokémon. These ASM routines are state-only; the gate and judging scripts own the visible dialogue, fades, warps, and result presentation. Judging now retains its computed placements and presents the source winner/score sequence before returning to those scripts.
- `BillsGrandfather` and `ReturnShuckie` now intercept their compiled `special` commands before core execution, open the existing party screen, apply the selected slot, and resume at the following script command. A on the party screen's `CANCEL` row and B both take the true cancellation path; Bill writes zero to `wScriptVar` exactly as ASM does, while Shuckie uses its typed cancelled-selection mutation. Normal scripts previously had no way to supply core's required selection variables and could fail before producing any outcome.
- `SelectApricornForKurt` now intercepts the normal compiled script and opens its own source-ordered, carried-Apricorn-only scrolling menu over the overworld. Choosing a type advances to ASM's quantity window (Up/Down by one, Left/Right by ten); B from quantity returns to the type list, B from the list writes false, and A removes only the confirmed amount before resuming Kurt's script. The unreachable legacy helper that treated an arbitrary Pack selection as an Apricorn and consumed the entire stack was removed.
- Bug Contest placement is no longer fabricated from `script_command_cursor % 3`. Core judging now scores the retained caught Pokémon with TypeScript/ASM's HP, stats, DV, remaining-HP, and held-item formula; rolls each participating contestant's ASM species/placement score and 0–7 perturbation through the gameplay RNG; inserts ties with the source's later-candidate priority; writes the computed rank; and advances the RNG state. The visible shell retains all three computed winners and presents ASM's separate third-, second-, and first-place announcement/score text boundaries in that order before resuming the result script.
- Bug Contest judging no longer invents `POKEMON` or `TRAINER` names for zeroed winner records. No caught Pokémon retains the cartridge's zero species/score semantics internally; malformed state with fewer than three active contestants is rejected before any divider read, while source-valid judging must fill all three winner slots before presentation.
- Visible compiled dialogue now resolves TypeScript's full runtime identity context: `<RIVAL>` reads the name saved by `NameRival` (with the source pre-name `RIVAL` default), and `<TODAY>` reads the current SUN–SAT game weekday. Missing `text_ram` buffers render blank as TypeScript does instead of leaking `[missing_text_buffer]` host diagnostics onto the game screen. Field dialogue, trainer result text, and Pokégear radio transcripts share this boundary.
- Route 29's compiled `catchtutorial` command now starts the scripted Rattata battle at its own source command index, using the existing tutorial battle mode, DUDE actor art/text, temporary POKé BALL path, forced capture, and ordinary scripted-battle completion. Rust previously advanced the command accumulator and opened a host `battle_type=BATTLETYPE_TUTORIAL` acknowledgement, so Dude's demonstration never began and the story script could not naturally reach its debrief.
- Game Corner prize Pokémon now follow `GameCornerPrizeMonCheckDex`: an already-caught prize continues immediately, while a newly recorded species opens the real Pokédex entry on that exact species and exits the entire scripted entry surface with B before resuming the prize script. Rust previously replaced `NewPokedexEntry` with a generic host boundary exposing internal species IDs and booleans.
- `PrintTodaysLuckyNumber` and the unused seen-species setter now remain source-silent buffer/state operations. The Radio Tower lucky-number script can consume `STRING_BUFFER_3` in its own compiled dialogue without first displaying a Rust implementation-detail boundary.
- `PlaceMoneyTopRight`, `DisplayCoinCaseBalance`, and `DisplayMoneyAndCoinBalance` no longer pause scripts at generic effect dumps. They retain typed balance overlays at the ASM window coordinates while the following text/YES-NO/menu is active, render the MONEY/COIN labels and fixed-width balances, and restore the underlying tiles when the compiled `closewindow` executes. This restores the Goldenrod/Celadon prize vendors, Move Tutor, haircut, medicine, and bargain-shop balance surfaces.
- `BankOfMom` is now a retained playable modal instead of a raw `money=/moms_money=` effect boundary. First use presents Mom's source introduction and save-money choice; later use presents the money question and GET / SAVE / CHANGE / CANCEL menu. GET and SAVE use Crystal's six-digit, movable decimal cursor, reject zero/insufficient/overflow transfers without mutation, update the exact wallet and saved balances atomically, and retain the entry screen after refusals. B follows the source cancellation path, CHANGE updates Mom's battle-reward saving flag, and successful/cancelled completion resumes the calling map script.
- Field use of the COIN CASE and BLUE CARD now closes the Pack and displays the ASM textboxes (`Coins: N` and `You now have N points.`). Rust previously opened generic special-boundary screens containing internal item IDs and balance-field names.
- Scripted new-Pokédex-entry screens now advance with A and close/resume the prize script after the final compiled page; they no longer wrap back to page one indefinitely or require the non-source B escape. Prof. Oak's PC also normalizes its required compiled rating text through the same player/rival/day and control-token renderer as field dialogue instead of displaying raw ASM text payloads or a missing-label fallback.
- Pokégear phone contacts now call directly from the list instead of opening Rust's invented contact/map detail screen. The typed runtime follows `MakePhoneCallFromPokegear`: link mode and unavailable contact times enter exported `LoadOutOfAreaScript`; same-map contacts select `PhoneScript_JustTalkToThem`; other valid calls store the dynamic callee and enter it through exported `LoadPhoneScriptBank`. The visible shell now owns the surrounding `PokegearPhone_MakePhoneCall` state exactly where ASM does: service maps play two sequential `SFX_CALL` rings before entering the callback, then wait ten frames and require A/B to play `SFX_HANG_UP` and return to the contact list. A map with no phone service never invokes the callback; it plays `SFX_NO_SIGNAL`, retains the modal out-of-service prompt until A/B, and returns to the contact list without a hang-up sound. Pack verification also rejects missing or non-executable caller, callee, special-call, and Pokégear callback roots.
- Incoming `Script_ReceivePhoneCall` callasm presentation is now a typed blocking script boundary instead of a fabricated batch of `waitsfx`/audio events. `RingTwice_StartCall` plays the first ring immediately, the second after the source's three 20-frame waits, and resumes the dynamic caller only after the second three-wait interval. `HangUp` plays its beep and blocks the following `closetext` for the exact initial wait plus six boop waits (140 frames total). This prevents Bevy's one-shot audio coalescing from deleting the first ring and prevents caller dialogue or post-call commands from running before their ASM presentation boundary. The obsolete save validator for synthetic phone-callasm audio events is removed.
- Source specials that lead into party selection now render their exported ASM text instead of Rust-authored substitutes. Name Rater preserves the complete introduction, which-Pokémon prompt, Egg/traded/cancel branches, all three better-name pages with the final source page beneath its YES/NO box, what-name prompt, named page, and every same-name/finished page; dynamic nickname insertion uses the source text renderer. Move Deleter preserves its complete introduction/final YES/NO page, party prompt, Egg/one-move/cancel branches, move prompt, dynamically buffered delete confirmation, and post-`WaitSFX` completion text. Photo Studio renders its party prompt, Hold Still, Egg, selection-cancel, and disconnected-printer cancellation pages from the exported source. Poké Seer now does the same for its introduction, cancellation, Egg, unknown-history, met/traded, caught-time/level, event-location, and every advice branch. Paragraph boundaries can no longer be collapsed into one host screen on these paths.
- Pokégear phone rows now use each compiled contact's source display lines, including the trainer-class second line, while transient call status remains visible without replacing the contact list. `OverworldTownMap` now opens directly on the current-region map and current landmark as ASM does; Rust previously sent wall/decorative town maps to the clock card.
- The Pokégear map card now keeps the selected landmark name in its map header and treats A as a no-op. Rust previously drew a scrolling landmark list over the map and opened a host detail page exposing regions, coordinates, compiled constants, IDs, and map counts; TypeScript and ASM expose none of that diagnostic UI.
- The removed Pokégear detail mode is now gone from shell state, hashing, input routing, resets, and quick-load blockers rather than surviving as a dead legacy path. The clock card now displays the SUN–SAT weekday, MORN/DAY/NITE period, and 12-hour AM/PM play clock used by TypeScript; radio frequencies use one decimal and Up/Down tune in the source direction; and maps without phone service show `NO SERVICE` before a call attempt.
- Route 34's deposited daycare Pokémon now shows Crystal's `It's <NICKNAME> that was left with the DAY-CARE MAN/LADY.` dialogue, plays its cry after that first page, and then shows the source compatibility message when both residents exist. Rust previously exposed caretaker booleans, internal species IDs, and levels in a generic effect screen. Multi-page special boundaries now also report their actual next label instead of incorrectly changing every queued sequence's host status to `CONTEST RESULTS`.
- The outside daycare man now performs TypeScript's default `collect_egg` action instead of merely checking `egg_present` and walking away without transferring anything. Successful pickup moves the concrete egg into the party, clears the breeding egg state, shows the received page, plays `SFX_GET_EGG` at its post-page ASM boundary, and then shows the take-care page; party-full and no-egg results show Crystal's player-facing refusal text instead of caretaker/action/result diagnostics.
- Prof. Oak's lab Pokédex check now uses the same compiled seen/owned counts and required ASM rating text as the PC menu entry. Rust previously fixed only the PC-hub route while the ordinary `special ProfOaksPCBoot` used by Oak's map script still opened the generic special-effect screen.
- `CelebiShrineEvent` now remains a state-only battle-type setup step as in TypeScript, allowing Ilex Forest's immediately following compiled `loadwildmon CELEBI, 30` and `startbattle` commands to run without an invented `battle_type=BATTLETYPE_CELEBI` diagnostic screen. ASM's dedicated 160-frame Celebi/leaf sprite animation remains a presentation-fidelity item rather than being falsely represented by host text.
- `TrainerHouse` now silently returns the saved Mystery Gift trainer-house flag, matching TypeScript and ASM's two calls in Trainer House B1F. Rust previously always returned true, copied unrelated link-battle win/loss/draw counters, set a synthetic pending battle type, and displayed a diagnostic screen, forcing the CAL2 branch even when no Mystery Gift trainer data existed.
- Ruins of Alph `HoOhChamber` and the Omanyte/Aerodactyl/Kabuto chamber checks now remain silent state/flag operations. Their scene callbacks can immediately test the result or newly opened event flag and defer the compiled wall-opening script; Rust previously interrupted map entry with internal party/beast/chamber booleans before those source-owned sequences.
- `Reset` no longer displays its internal `$0` effect or a host diagnostic. The typed `reset_requested` runtime flag rebuilds the boot runtime at the intro/title entry, matching TypeScript's direct soft-reset special and ASM's non-dialogue reset routine.
- Lucky Number checking now stays invisible when no ID matches, but a winning result fills `STRING_BUFFER_1` with the matched species and displays the required compiled party/PC congratulations text before the prize branch. Rust previously exposed the lucky number, numeric tier, Rust source enum, species ID, and text label. Mobile-adapter status and the GS/trainer-ranking healing counters are now silent return/bookkeeping routines instead of interrupting ordinary Pokécenter and story healing scripts with implementation details.
- Lake of Rage's `CheckMagikarpLength` now intercepts the compiled special at its natural script position and opens the real party screen. Choosing MAGIKARP runs the typed measurement mutation, formats `STRING_BUFFER_1`, retains the special-owned `Let me measure… / …Hm, it measures …` page, and only after acknowledgement resumes into the map script's too-short or reward branch. Non-Magikarp selection branches immediately as ASM does; B/CANCEL writes the refused result and reaches the refusal dialogue. Rust originally invoked the special without its required party-selection variables; the first visible implementation made selection playable but silently skipped the source measurement page.
- The Lake of Rage record sign now displays Crystal's `CURRENT RECORD`, formatted length, and record-holder name surface. Rust previously opened a diagnostic special boundary containing separate feet/inches fields and the formatted implementation value.
- Cianwood's Photo Studio now intercepts its natural `PhotoStudio` special and opens the party screen instead of failing on a missing injected `_party_slot`. Cancellation shows `Oh, no picture?`, and an EGG gets Crystal's refusal without invoking `PrintPartymon` or opening a Pokémon-picture surface. For a valid Pokémon, `HoldStillText` remains on the map first; only its acknowledgement commits the typed print mutation and opens the selected species' picture beneath the Game Boy Printer status. With no printer connected, B cancels `PrinterError2`, closes that picture through the typed runtime boundary, restores the map, and only then shows `NoPhotoText`, matching `ReturnToMapWithSpeechTextbox` before the source checks `hPrinter`. Rust previously opened the picture before `HoldStillText`, printed an EGG before refusing it, and left the picture active behind the final map dialogue. Injected empty/out-of-range party slots now fail atomically rather than being coerced into a fabricated cancellation result. The connected-printer success path still depends on implementing an actual printer connection rather than inventing one in the shell.
- Cianwood's Poké Seer now opens the source party selector at the compiled `PokeSeer` command instead of failing on a missing host-injected `_party_slot`. Selection produces the exact exported Egg, unknown-history, met/traded location, caught-time, caught-level, event-location, and growth-advice pages; cancellation produces the exported `Fufufu!` response, and the script resumes only after the reading closes. The ASM exporter preserves every `text_ram` operand as `<RAM:exactName>` instead of erasing non-`wStringBufferN` operands into `@`, treats `text_start` as a command boundary rather than a fabricated paragraph, writes the root and modular ASM-text catalogs together, and the generic Rust renderer resolves those exact operands. The met/traded classification reproduces `ReadCaughtData`'s cartridge bug: its commented-out low-byte compare means matching only the high OT-ID byte counts as the player's Pokémon. Rust originally replaced the interaction with an internal party-slot/species/OT/ID dump. Persisted caught data retains the source two-bit Morning/Day/Night value and presents it instead of substituting `Unknown` for ordinary captures.
- Route 34's Day-Care services now render their complete exported ASM text instead of Rust-composed approximations: first/repeat Lady introductions, Man introduction, which-Pokémon prompt, every deposit refusal and result, withdrawal preview/growth/fee/confirmation/result, Egg offer/refusal/full-party/success/care pages, and the two outside resident/compatibility readings. The source pre-choice active bit is a typed `open` mutation and remains saveable without a deposited Pokémon; Rust previously conflated that introduction flag with resident occupancy and rejected the cartridge state. `text_decimal` now preserves its exact source operand/width tuple as `<DECIMAL:...>` and the generic renderer resolves the Day-Care level growth and fee operands without `@@@` placeholders. Receiving an Egg plays `SFX_GET_EGG` after its first result page and retains that page for the exact 120-frame source delay before `Take good care of it.` appears.
- Goldenrod and Lavender's Name Raters now intercept their compiled special and retain every decision boundary in source order: introduction/YES-NO, `WhichMon`, party selection, Egg/traded refusal or `BetterName`/YES-NO, `WhatName`, the ten-character naming screen, then named plus same-name/finished pages. Confirming persists a genuinely changed nickname before the result pages; an empty/all-space result or a byte-identical name preserves the existing nickname and buffers that effective name for the same-name pages, matching `IsNewNameEmpty` and `CompareNewToOld`. The authoritative core mutation previously copied all-space input into Pokémon state even though ASM treats it as unchanged. Declining either choice or cancelling party selection presents `Come again sometime.` Rust originally called core before either required input existed, and the first visible implementation still skipped `WhichMon`, `BetterName`, and `WhatName` by jumping directly from YES to party and from an eligible party member to naming.
- Both Goldenrod haircut brothers and Daisy's grooming now open the party selector at their natural compiled specials. B/CANCEL returns `0`, Eggs return `1`, and an eligible selection consumes one authoritative Crystal RNG byte, applies the exported happiness table, and returns its result code so the existing map scripts own payment, fades, music, dialogue, event flags, and cry. These three overworld services previously failed before selection because Rust required externally injected party and RNG fields.
- Blackthorn's Move Deleter now owns the complete source introduction plus ASM-defaulted entry, retains `DeleterAskWhichMonText` before party selection and `DeleterAskWhichMoveText` before the move list, then presents the ASM-defaulted deletion confirmation at the compiled `MoveDeletion` special. Eggs, one-move Pokémon, cancellation, and refusal show Crystal's source dialogue; YES applies the typed move/PP deletion, waits for the current sound channel, plays `SFX_MOVE_DELETED`, waits for that cue to finish, shows the completion text, and only then resumes the house script. Rust originally entered core before either required slot existed; the first visible implementation made both nested selectors playable but silently dropped their two source prompt pages, and its deletion result text appeared concurrently with the sound instead of following ASM's two `WaitSFX` boundaries.
- Goldenrod's Move Tutor now renders the exported common move-learning text with the exact nickname and move buffers instead of rebuilding it in Rust. The three-page four-move warning retains its final question beneath YES/NO, the source `Which move should be forgotten?` page remains beneath the move list, and already-known, incompatible, HM, stop-learning, did-not-learn, count, Poof/forgot/And, and learned results all come from the ASM text table. The replacement sequence still honors both 30-frame `text_pause` boundaries, queues `SFX_SWITCH_POKEMON` after the count, and queues the Pokédex fanfare only after the final forgot page. Active host confirmations now explicitly block compiled-script continuation while their preceding source pages drain.
- Radio Tower's Buena Prize receptionist now opens Crystal's source-ordered nine-item, four-row scrolling prize list with each point cost and the live two-digit BLUE CARD balance. Selection opens the ASM-defaulted item confirmation; successful redemption uses the typed bag/balance mutation and `SFX_TRANSACTION`, while insufficient points and full Pack retain the menu with source refusal text. B closes through `Please come back again!` before resuming the receptionist script. Rust previously required an externally injected item/quantity, and its only visible helper silently redeemed the first affordable alphabetic-map entry.
- All four Ruins of Alph `UnownPuzzle` specials now retain the core's authoritative shuffled 6×6 layout in a playable modal instead of opening a layout/move-count debug dump and returning false. Directional input follows the ASM's bounded outer-ring/center movement (including the bottom-corner jump), uses `SFX_POUND` empty-handed and `SFX_MOVE_PUZZLE_PIECE` while holding, and invalid A presses use `SFX_WRONG`. A picks up and places through repeated typed `UnownPuzzle` mutations with `SFX_MEGA_KICK` / `SFX_PLACE_PUZZLE_PIECE_DOWN`; B returns false; and completing the target layout plays `SFX_1ST_PLACE` and returns true after acknowledgement so each chamber's compiled floor-collapse/event branch can run. The visible 20×18 LCD now composes the shipped puzzle-specific PNG, doubled 24×24 pieces, source border overlays, exact outer ring/central void geometry, OAM-style held piece and cursor, and source START/CANCEL tile strip instead of symbolic glyphs.
- Unown puzzle state no longer migrates unscoped host variables into a puzzle-specific key on read. Only the current scoped layout is accepted; an empty hand is represented by absence of the scoped holding-piece key rather than the string sentinel `null`, and stale unscoped state remains rejected/unmodified. Puzzle selection accepts the four source `UNOWNPUZZLE_*` constants or their compiled byte indices, not bare host aliases such as `KABUTO`.
- Unown puzzle state and effects no longer expose or persist an invented move counter. `_UnownPuzzle` clears its scratch union, initializes the board, and tracks only the layout, held-piece state, cursor/jumptable state, and `wSolvedUnownPuzzle`; there is no placement-count byte in the source routine. The retained multi-frame Rust abstraction keeps only source-observable puzzle state.
- Day-Care saved state now stores the egg countdown as the source-sized one-byte `wStepsToEgg`. The non-source elapsed-egg counter, resident step totals, initial-EXP mirror, duplicate initial-level field, and persisted `last_interaction` result have been removed; progression remains defined solely by the stored resident record (including its deposited level and three-byte EXP), source flags, and exact countdown byte. Typed Day-Care effects now carry their immediate level/refusal result to the presentation caller without turning it into save data. The visible withdrawal flow runs the same EXP-to-level calculation as `GetBreedMon1LevelGrowth`/`GetBreedMon2LevelGrowth` and subtracts the deposited level in the resident record, instead of comparing two unchanged duplicate levels and always reporting zero growth. Stale payloads carrying the deleted host fields or a countdown above `$ff` reject instead of being truncated during `DayCareStep`.
- Bug Contest state no longer persists the unused host `last_rank` and `last_result` mirrors. Contest setup, caught-Pokémon routing, and judging already write their source return byte to the live script accumulator and return it in the typed immediate effect; no production consumer read either mirror. The parallel `caught_species`/`caught_level` mirrors are also removed: Crystal stores those fields inside the single `wContestMon` party struct, which Rust already models as `caught_mon`; the status surface now reads that authoritative record directly. Stale saves carrying any deleted mirror now reject.
- Battle Tower state no longer saves `last_rule_failure` or `last_sprite_constant`, and the special routines no longer create parallel string variables for either value. Rule checking returns the complete source-ordered failure list in its immediate typed effect, while opponent loading returns the selected source sprite and writes the actual live battle/trainer state; none of the four mirrors had a production reader. The same audit removed write-only host variables for the reward item, opponent identity/party count/target object, mobile handshake count, and leaderboard count; their authoritative typed state/effects remain. Stale saves carrying the deleted state fields reject.
- Buena password selection now retains only the packed category/option state corresponding to `wBuenasPassword` and the live `wScriptVar` result. The generated category/type/password strings and remember-password result are carried by immediate typed effects, while the host choice bridges are consumed when their source-owned menus return instead of leaking into saved script variables. Battle Tower level-group load likewise relies on the authoritative saved/live level-group state, and `BattleTowerAction` no longer persists `_value`, `_truthy`, or a duplicate loaded-level string after returning through the script accumulator. The Day-Care outside-man and mobile leaderboard specials also stopped duplicating their `wScriptVar` return into `_value`.
- PC, Oak-rating, town-map, map-radio, Bug Contest contestant, and Game Corner cabinet specials no longer persist write-only underscore mirrors of values already held by their typed effects, source text buffers, saved subsystem state, or live script accumulator. Each routine also clears a stale copy if one entered through an older host-issued state, so opening the source surface cannot preserve a parallel authority channel; slot, card-flip, and unused-memory-game coin displays now read the authoritative coin register directly.
- `last_special_routine` is now explicitly process-local presentation history rather than cartridge state. Save format 22 omitted it from serialization and deterministic state checksums, deserialization rejects stale payloads that contain it, runtime-pack save validation no longer treats it as a durable catalog reference, and the unused mutation API that allowed callers to drain it has been removed. Mom's DST confirmation now renders from the live DST flag, while cable-club matchmaking retains the typed `LinkAction` room and accepts it only when it agrees with both authoritative link-session bytes; deliberately contradictory routine history cannot change either behavior. The remaining production exposure is diagnostic snapshot/debug text rather than gameplay authority.
- Link session state no longer persists the host-only `last_result`, `failed_link_to_past`, `quick_save_requested`, `friend_ready`, or string `active_room` mirrors. Link specials carry immediate results in typed effects, while `TryQuickSave` carries its save request as the immediate typed effect consumed by the shell. `WaitForLinkedFriend` now retains only `hSerialConnectionStatus` plus its script result, so the established serial byte itself is the saveable pre-room boundary instead of requiring a second readiness flag. Cable Club room identity is derived exclusively from `wLinkMode` values 1/2/3, including trade progression, Colosseum battle admission, deterministic save validation, and direct multiplayer activation, so saved state no longer requires a synthetic room name to resolve against the special-routine catalog. Save format 23 rejects all five deleted fields, and public snapshots no longer expose them as second authority channels.
- The synchronized `wLinkBattleRNs` stream is required only for `wLinkMode = 3` Colosseum state. Time Capsule and Trade modes now save without an unused zero-filled battle stream, and direct multiplayer creates the pre-negotiation placeholder only for Colosseum. Mode 3 still fails closed without a valid stream, preserving deterministic link-battle continuation.
- Link result specials no longer surface host diagnostic boundaries. `WaitForLinkedFriend` carries the source success-only 50-frame tail while timeout returns immediately; `CheckBothSelectedSameRoom`, `CloseLink`, and `WaitForOtherPlayerToExit` carry their fixed silent 1-, 6-, and 12-frame tails, and `FailedLinkToPast` carries its 40 fixed frames before the separately audited synchronization boundary. `TryQuickSave` writes its live host save success/failure to `wScriptVar` and always consumes the source 30 frames, including host save failures. Bevy advances these waits through a deterministic process-local presentation timer included in the visible-shell hash, without adding another saved result mirror.
- `CableClubCheckWhichChris` now implements its misleadingly named ASM body: it returns true only when `hSerialConnectionStatus == USING_EXTERNAL_CLOCK`. Rust previously required a host `_player_gender` input and branched on Chris versus Kris, which could assign the wrong cable-club side independently of link clock ownership. The typed effect now names the actual clock-owner result and returns silently instead of opening a diagnostic boundary.
- Time Capsule compatibility now follows the source's party-wide priority: reject any species numbered 152 or later before scanning any held mail, then reject the first move with source index 166 or later. Rust previously interleaved species and approximate substring-mail checks per Pokémon and never examined moves, so early-party mail could mask a later Johto species and every Gen II move was admitted. Rejection names now use the species name in `wStringBuffer1`; move rejection preserves the move in `wStringBuffer2`, rather than reporting a nickname through unrelated buffer 3. The result returns silently so the authored receptionist script alone selects and displays the rejection text.
- The void cable-club setup/room specials no longer overwrite `wScriptVar`. `SetBitsForLinkTradeRequest`, `SetBitsForBattleRequest`, and `SetBitsForTimeCapsuleRequest` mutate only their authored link-action/room bytes; `TradeCenter`, `Colosseum`, and `EnterTimeCapsule` mutate link mode without inventing a boolean return. The room `TimeCapsule` special now sets `wLinkMode = LINK_TIMECAPSULE` while preserving `wPlayerLinkAction` and `wChosenCableClubRoom`, instead of clearing those bytes and fabricating true.
- `CheckLinkTimeout_Receptionist` now writes the source handshake action byte `1` regardless of the selected Trade/Colosseum room; Rust previously copied `wChosenCableClubRoom`, sending action `2` for Colosseum. Its typed result also retains the two unconditional pre-check frames, plus the three reset frames on failure, while the transport-owned exchange itself remains the live synchronization input.
- Link closure no longer uses a broad host reset. Timeout resets only `hSerialConnectionStatus`; `CloseLink` and `WaitForOtherPlayerToExit` additionally clear only `wLinkMode`, preserving the source `wPlayerLinkAction`, `wChosenCableClubRoom`, `wOtherPlayerLinkMode`, and link RNG bytes. `FailedLinkToPast` now preserves all represented WRAM and `wScriptVar` while carrying its fixed pre-sync delay as a void `LinkDelay`; Rust previously forced Time Capsule mode, disconnected serial state, and fabricated false.
- `WaitForLinkedFriend` now derives readiness exclusively from `hSerialConnectionStatus`. The duplicate `_link_friend_ready` script variable and `ready` mutation-command field have been removed from core, hosted multiplayer, and the public runtime boundary; mutation schema v48 rejects v47 frames rather than compatibility-decoding the deleted authority channel.
- Hosted transport disconnect/settlement no longer replaces the complete link-session state with defaults. It marks only `hSerialConnectionStatus` disconnected, preserving mode, action, chosen room, peer mode, and synchronized RNG until the authored ASM close/exit boundary mutates the bytes it owns.
- `CheckLinkTimeout_Receptionist` now derives timeout exclusively from `hSerialConnectionStatus`; the duplicate `_link_timeout` variable and `timeout` mutation-command field are gone. Peer link mode remains a live transport input only on the connected branch. Mutation schema v49 rejects v48 frames containing the deleted boolean.
- Hosted online room selection no longer opens a synthetic `LinkAction` acknowledgement modal before the receptionist's waiting text. The silent request starts/updates the real hosted matchmaking queue, the compiled script reaches its authored `Please wait` text and suspends at `WaitForLinkedFriend`, and matched peers complete that suspension from exchanged room selections plus serial clock ownership. Internet matchmaking remains transport-owned and does not inherit the cartridge's short local-cable discovery timeout; after a peer is established, the core resumes through the exact `WaitForLinkedFriend` result and 50-frame success tail.
- Trade Center, Time Capsule, and Colosseum console specials no longer use a manual diagnostic modal as a surrogate for the online session.
