Their typed `LinkRoom` effect distinguishes the nonblocking `EnterTimeCapsule` map-entry handshake from a blocking room session. Hosted trade cancellation/completion and terminal synchronized Colosseum results now release the retained script cursor automatically into the authored `newloadmap MAPSETUP_LINKRETURN`; online party, trade, battle, and result transport continues while the script itself is suspended.
- Hosted transport loss no longer strands the retained receptionist or room script cursor. A cancelled match before room entry feeds the disconnected serial byte through the real false `WaitForLinkedFriend` result so the authored `Your friend is not ready` branch runs; a loss inside Trade Center, Time Capsule, or Colosseum closes any active link battle as a draw and releases the console script through its authored `newloadmap MAPSETUP_LINKRETURN` before reconnecting to the online lobby. Explicit peer disconnect frames take the same settlement path instead of leaving a half-live hosted session.
- Hosted Trade Center sessions no longer settle after the first accepted or declined offer. A declined offer clears only that exchange and returns both online peers to the party menu; selecting CANCEL sends a distinct pre-offer false confirmation, the peer acknowledges it, and only the synchronized two-sided cancel exits the room. Successful exchanges remove the offered party slot, shift later party members, append the received Pokemon exactly like `RemoveMonFromPartyOrBox` plus `AddTempmonToParty`, apply forced trade evolution (with Time Capsule held-item restrictions), retain pending evolution move learning, and run the mandatory post-trade save before another offer. The `CheckAnyOtherAliveMonsForTrade` rule also rejects an exchange only when neither an unoffered local Pokemon nor the incoming Pokemon has HP.
- Time Capsule transfer application now passes through the Gen I party-structure boundary instead of cloning every Gen II host field. On receipt it restores base happiness 70, clears Pokerus and caught data, omits Mail, recalculates split Special Attack/Defense, and clears battle-only volatile fields; ordinary Trade Center transfers retain their Gen II party fields while also discarding battle-only state that is absent from the serialized party structure.
- Goldenrod and Celadon's `SlotMachine` special now opens as a retained, repeatable game instead of requiring an injected bet, resolving one invisible spin, and dumping coins/payout fields. Its validated typed core state owns cabinet mode, the one-time seven-streak chance, persistent bias, bet, exact reel offsets/order, matched symbol, and payout remainder; typed start, ordered reel-stop, result, payout-frame, acknowledgement, replay, and quit inputs replace the former `slot_*` string protocol. Up/Down select the source-ordered 1–3 coin bet while Left/Right are ignored, the returned three reel windows and result remain visible through a separate acknowledgement and play-again phase, and B quits back to the compiled map script. Missing Coin Case and zero-coin entry use the source refusal text. Start samples the cabinet's seven-streak mode once, samples only `Slots_InitBias`, commits the stake, and retains the source position-14 reel state; the LCD then performs the 32-frame spin-up and advances every live reel once per four frames until three separate A presses. Each stop submits that live position to the core's four-slot bias manipulation, reel-two skip-to-seven eligibility/RNG ordering, and reel-three normal/slow/Golem/Chansey logic. Slow, Golem, and Chansey retain their source actor counts, reel movement, animation, and sound boundaries. A winning third stop runs the 16-frame OBJ-palette flash before result; seven-streak retention consumes its source mask roll there. `SlotsAction_PayoutAnim` transfers one authoritative coin every other frame, preserves the overlapping-sound bug, and continues decrementing at a full Coin Case. Cabinet exit ignores B while reels are active and follows `_SlotMachine`'s exact quit-sound waits; spending the last coin follows `Slots_AskPlayAgain` through the ran-out message and 60-frame hold before exit.
- Goldenrod's `CardFlip` no longer performs one invisible default-index mutation or substitutes the unrelated six-species 6×4 flip-any-card game. Its typed core state owns Crystal's encoded 24-card deck (Pikachu/Jigglypuff/Poliwag/Oddish at levels 1–6), face-indexed discard pile, phase, played-card counter, selection registers, face-up identity, and payout remainder. The source rejection shuffle and all 48 cells of the ASM species/level betting table produce the exact 6/9/12/18/72-coin awards. The visible flow selects one of two cards, navigates the irregular 6×8 betting grid, retains twelve two-card rounds, reshuffles at the source boundary, and reports both species and level. Typed `Start`, `Continue`, `ResumeAfterShuffle`, `Reveal`, `PayoutFrame`, `AcknowledgeResult`, and `Quit` inputs replace the former string protocol. Accepting play shuffles before the source insufficient-funds check and deducts exactly three coins before `ChooseACard`; reveal only marks the chosen discard and calculates the award; each payout step adds one coin; and the played-card counter increments only when replay is accepted. This permits a three-coin player to reveal after the stake reaches zero. `ChooseACard` preserves the two source 20-frame placement delays, ignores directional input, automatically alternates `PlaceOAMCardBorder` every four delay frames with `SFX_KINESIS`, and runs the selected card's three four-frame-on/four-frame-clear flashes before exposing the bet grid. Wins pay one authoritative coin every two frames, retain the result until a separate A/B acknowledgement, and only then open `PlayAgain`. Accepting the twelfth replay reshuffles and shows the source `The cards have been shuffled.` A/B prompt before the next stake. The retained animation state uses decoded transient-channel completion for every source `WaitSFX`; the prior invented insufficient-funds, result-acknowledgement, and shuffle-prompt sound effects are removed.
- Route 34's indoor `DayCareMan` and `DayCareLady` specials now open through their natural compiled map commands instead of relying on unreachable host-only deposit/withdraw helpers. Empty residents present the source ASM-defaulted introduction and real party picker; the shell refuses a sole party member, Egg, MAIL holder, or selection that would leave no healthy battler before invoking the typed deposit. Success removes the chosen Pokémon, retains its concrete resident state, plays its cry between the source deposit/come-back pages, and resumes the map script. Occupied residents show nickname, levels gained, and the exact 100 + 100-per-level fee; accepted withdrawal uses the typed money/party mutation and distinguishes party-full and insufficient-money refusals, while refusal and success retain source-facing dialogue and cry sequencing.
- Goldenrod's weekly `MoveTutor` special now consumes the compiled `setval` selector (1/2/3 = FLAMETHROWER/THUNDERBOLT/ICE BEAM), opens the real party screen, and returns ASM's `FALSE` on a successful lesson or `255` on cancellation instead of failing on missing host-injected `_party_slot`/`_move`. Eggs and incompatible learnsets display the source compatibility refusal, while an already-known move uses the distinct `KnowsMoveText`; both acknowledge back into the retained party selector instead of prematurely ending the NPC interaction. A Pokémon below four moves learns directly. A full moveset now presents the source delete-an-older-move confirmation, detailed move selection, HM refusal loop, stop-learning confirmation and did-not-learn result; accepting a replacement stages the `1, 2 and…`, ` Poof!`, forgotten-move prompt, and learned-move page before the source coin-payment/farewell branch. The two `TextCommand_PAUSE` boundaries each advance automatically after 30 frames or immediately on A/B. `SFX_SWITCH_POKEMON` is emitted only between the count and ` Poof!`, and `SFX_DEX_FANFARE_50_79` only as the forgotten-move prompt hands off to `LearnedMoveText`; Rust previously queued both together before the first page, its one-transient-channel policy discarded the poof cue, and both source pauses incorrectly required extra button presses. The timed state participates in deterministic hashing and is cleared by global presentation resets. A successful lesson applies the exported `HAPPINESS_LEARNMOVE` tier. The arbitrary dead helper that selected `script_command_cursor % all_moves` has been deleted. Replacing these retained pages with the generic exported text-command interpreter remains part of the broader presentation-program work.
- Fishing rods now require the player to be on foot and facing a live water collision before any RNG or state mutation, matching TypeScript's terrain gate; casting on land, beyond the map edge, or while Surfing produces `There's no water to fish here.` instead of rolling an encounter anywhere on a map with a fishing group. Both ordinary Pack USE and registered-item entry now follow ASM `Script_FishCastRod` rather than Rust's invented `<PLAYER> used a FISHING ROD!` textbox: the retained overworld shows the shipped rod graphic in the facing direction for the 40-frame cast pause; a bite runs four eight-frame `fish_got_bite` beats, exposes the loaded `EMOTE_SHOCK`, waits the following 40 frames, restores the rod pose, and only then shows `Oh! A bite!`. Failure goes directly from the cast pause to `Not even a nibble!`. Input and premature battle music remain locked throughout, a prepared encounter is exposed only after the bite text closes, and player/rod composition now follows `FacingFish*`: exact direction-specific `LoadFishingGFX` lower tiles, `$fc` for vertical rods, `$fd` for horizontal rods, left-only X flip, and the source OAM offsets.
- All 20 `QueueScript` targets in `engine/events/overworld.asm` are now exported as shared executable global roots. Runtime verification no longer exempts every global `Script_*` `callasm`; only labels proven to be exact members of the compiled phone-script table retain asynchronous phone-engine dispatch. Fishing's `Fishing_CheckFacingUp` CPU boundary now reads the live retained player direction and synchronously writes the source `wPlayerDirection`/`wScriptVar` result. Source-fingerprint-locked boundaries also consume `LoadFishingGFX`, `BlindingFlash`, `CutDownTreeOrGrass`, and `DisappearWhirlpool`. Cut and Whirlpool requests now only validate and retain the exact actor, source block, replacement block, coordinates, map, tileset, and animation variant. The live map and saved override remain unchanged through the use text; the exact `CutDownTreeOrGrass` / `DisappearWhirlpool` callasm then atomically replaces the block immediately before the visible animation. Flash likewise retains its validated actor/flag transition without illuminating the map early; `BlindingFlash` sets authoritative `STATUSFLAGS_FLASH` only after the source use text and immediately before the white fade. Surf now retains its validated actor, source/destination tile, and exact Surf/Pikachu movement mode without moving the live or saved player early. After `UsedSurfText` closes, Rust executes the exported `callasm`, `readmem`, `writevar`, sprite/music specials, `SurfStartStep`, and dynamic `wMovementBuffer` `slow_step` in source order; the visible movement resolver now understands the exported `PLAYER_FACING` direction instead of rejecting that canonical buffer. Waterfall likewise retains its complete validated climb, then consumes one `turn_waterfall` movement and exact continuation `callasm` per four-frame source step. The underlying movement engine now routes `turn_away`, `turn_in`, and `turn_waterfall` through real tile movement like ASM `TurningStep` rather than treating them as stationary rotations. Fly, Dig, Teleport, and the Escape Rope branch shared by `.UsedDigOrEscapeRopeScript` now retain their validated source action and exact destination while departure text, sound, delay, and movement execute on the source map; a distinct deterministic mutation commits the map transition only at the visible source warp boundary. Headbutt menu use now queues `HeadbuttFromMenuScript`, and the exact tree roll remains after its use text and shake boundary instead of running at menu dispatch. Strength menu use now queues `Script_StrengthFromMenu`; its source-certified `SetStrengthFlag` instruction body is the sole boundary that sets `ENGINE_STRENGTH_ACTIVE`, writes `wStrengthSpecies`, and fills the nickname buffers. Sweet Scent now queues its exported local root and resolves encounter RNG only at the certified `SweetScentEncounter` callasm after the source text and wait. A roaming result retains `BATTLETYPE_ROAMING`, its exact slot, saved HP/DVs, and the scripted resume origin through the later `randomwildmon`/`startbattle` boundary instead of being rematerialized as an unrelated normal static encounter. Every battle exit now executes ASM `BattleEnd_HandleRoamMons`: roaming wins clear their slot, roaming non-wins preserve low HP and always move roamers, and ordinary/link battles consume the exact 1-in-16 gate before any route update. Whiteout recovery now requires that battle cleanup has already persisted a LOSS instead of silently deactivating an active battle outside its RNG frame. Pending field moves are pack-validated on every retained mutation/save boundary, and mutation protocol v30 rejects the old movement, eager-travel, eager-Headbutt, eager-Strength, eager-Sweet-Scent, slotless scripted-roamer, divider-less roaming escape-item, incomplete battle-terminal RNG, and fabricated queued-target semantics.
- Surf now enforces both pre-script gates at both relevant source boundaries. The authoritative field-move mutation rejects atomically when `BIKEFLAGS_ALWAYS_ON_BIKE_F` / `ENGINE_ALWAYS_ON_BIKE` is set, preserving the player's bike mode and tile, and contextual A-button dispatch does not open `AskSurfText` in that state. `CheckDirection` now combines the directional permission of the tile being left with the facing water tile's entry permission; a current `RIGHT_WALL`, for example, blocks rightward Surf even when the destination itself is ordinary water. Rust previously ignored the bike flag and current-tile permission, offered the prompt, and could commit the player through that boundary despite both `SurfFunction.TrySurf` and `TrySurfOW` branching to failure before the Surf script.
- Contextual Whirlpool now completes `TryWhirlpoolMenu`'s exact tileset/block replacement lookup before opening `AskWhirlpoolText`. A facing collision that looks like a whirlpool but has no matching `WhirlpoolBlockPointers` entry follows `Script_MightyWhirlpool` immediately instead of prompting YES/NO and discovering the unsupported mutation only after acceptance.
- Contextual Headbutt now applies `CheckHeadbuttTreeTile` to the exact live facing collision byte. Rust previously searched all four collision tokens of the facing metatile, so a non-tree quadrant could inherit `COLL_HEADBUTT_TREE` from another quadrant and incorrectly open `AskHeadbuttText`.
- Headbutt dispatch now preserves the two source script roots: party-menu use queues `HeadbuttFromMenuScript`, while accepting contextual `AskHeadbuttScript` queues `HeadbuttScript` directly. Rust previously routed both through the menu wrapper and therefore inserted its menu-only `refreshmap` / `UpdateTimePals` before an overworld Headbutt. Mutation protocol v32 records the dispatch origin explicitly and rejects the ambiguous v31 command shape without a compatibility shim.
- `getcurlandmarkname` now writes the current map's verified Pokégear landmark name atomically at the shared script-interpreter boundary, matching `GetWorldMapLocation` followed by `GetLandmarkName`. The core previously wrote an empty string and depended on the asset adapter to overwrite it afterward; it also omitted this opcode from exact string-buffer validation. Missing map-derived input and invalid buffers now reject before state mutation.
- Every name/number-buffer opcode now requires one of the three source constants `STRING_BUFFER_3` through `STRING_BUFFER_5`, matching `GetStringBuffer` and `NUM_STRING_BUFFERS`; the interpreter previously accepted invented labels such as `BUFFER_1` for trainer, item, Pokémon, raw-string, and `getnum` output. `getnum` now also formats only a byte-width `wScriptVar` and rejects impossible retained values above 255 instead of printing them through a fabricated 16-bit path. `getitemname` no longer treats `ITEM_FROM_MEM` as a valid dynamic-name sentinel: ASM reserves `$ff` for `GiveItem`, while `Script_getitemname` recognizes only byte zero (`USE_SCRIPT_VAR`) before ordinary item lookup.
- Pack-derived name opcodes (`gettrainername`, `gettrainerclassname`, `getitemname`, `getmonname`, `getstring`, and `getlandmarkname`) now require their resolved display value as an explicit interpreter input and commit it once. The shared core previously wrote raw IDs or label operands such as `COOLTRAINERM` into the target buffer and relied on a later asset-layer overwrite; missing resolution now rejects atomically instead of exposing a second, non-ASM interpretation of the opcode.
- `verbosegiveitemvar` no longer inserts a fabricated named-buffer entry keyed by its item operand (for example `POTION -> VAR_REWARD_QUANTITY`). ASM treats those bytes as `wCurItem` and a quantity-variable selector, then `ReceiveItem`/`CurItemName` populate the canonical item buffers; Rust's pack-backed grant path already performs that mutation, so the stray operand-as-buffer state is removed and regression-checked.
- `catchtutorial` no longer overwrites the script accumulator with a fabricated true result. `Script_catchtutorial` stores only `wBattleType`, runs the tutorial, and reloads the map; Rust now records the visible tutorial boundary while preserving the prior script value, so a later conditional cannot observe a write the cartridge never made.
- The shared event-command validator no longer exposes Game Boy CPU `stop`, `push`, `pop`, `ret`, `ld`, or `ldh` instructions as Crystal script opcodes. The removed `stop` path fabricated a host `script_stop_requested` mutation; `push`/`pop` maintained a string stack and projected popped CPU state into arbitrary named buffers; and `ret`/`ld`/`ldh` could be individually selected as no-op or recorded “effects” outside their routine control flow. Crystal's event table defines none of those commands. Real script termination remains owned by `end`, `endall`, `stopandsjump`, and their control interpreter paths, while loads, typed conditional returns, and AF stack operations remain in the intact exported definition bodies and execute only through the source-certified callasm CPU interpreter. The obsolete persisted string stack and CPU “ASM directive” save entries are removed without compatibility shims.
- `teleport_from` is no longer duplicated as a selectable event-runtime command. It is a movement opcode from `macros/scripts/movement.asm`; the movement interpreter remains its sole execution path and still publishes the exact two-phase visual effect/flag. The deleted shared-runtime branch could set that flag without applying any movement at all.
- `writecmdqueue` no longer turns its map-data pointer into a runnable script target. For the canonical Ice Path B1F and Blackthorn Gym 2F uses, Rust now resolves the exact `CMDQUEUE_STONETABLE` structure, requires its sentinel-terminated `stonetable` body, canonicalizes every local landing script, and installs the typed warp/object/script entries atomically at the event opcode. `cmdqueue` and `stonetable` data macros are no longer discoverable or executable as event commands. Pack verification rejects malformed queue data before play; the Strength boulder landing hook continues to consume the installed table exactly where `CmdQueue_StoneTable` would call the map script.
- Menu coordinates, elevator-floor rows, conditional background-event wrappers, and raw `dw`/`dn`/`dba`/`dbw` declarations are no longer discoverable or executable through the shared event-command interpreter. Menus and elevators continue through their typed definition parsers, while background-event flag gating reads the exact `conditional_event` wrapper from compiled script data. The obsolete persisted menu-coordinate, elevator-row, and raw-ASM record channels are removed without compatibility shims.
- `wSpecialPhoneCallID` is now one authoritative persisted optional slot instead of a drainable record vector. The visible shell no longer clears Elm, Bill, Bike Shop, or Pokérus calls while draining presentation history; a queued call survives until `CountStep` dispatches it or `specialphonecall SPECIALCALL_NONE` explicitly clears it. `CheckPokerus` is restored to its source carry-only party query: the Pokécenter standard script owns the warning's engine flag and subsequent call assignment. The save-format bump rejects the prior vector shape without a compatibility shim.
- Persisted NPC-trade completion is no longer exposed through the presentation-record drain protocol. Rust's `completed_trades` is the typed equivalent of ASM `wTradeFlags`; draining it could reopen an already completed one-time trade even though only `NPCTrade`'s flag action may change that state. The obsolete completed-trade drain route is not accepted through a compatibility shim.
- `blackoutmod` now owns only the authoritative saved-spawn mutation. ASM writes `wLastSpawnMapGroup` and `wLastSpawnMapNumber` and immediately returns; Rust previously also serialized a duplicate `blackout_mod` marker, exposed a mutation command that could consume it, showed it in host diagnostics, and cleared it during whiteout. The invented field, snapshot surface, consumer, and save validation are removed. The bumped save and mutation schemas reject the prior shapes without compatibility shims; source-backed tests still require exact spawn-table resolution before committing the command.
- `catchtutorial` no longer appends its battle type to serialized, drainable command history. `Script_catchtutorial` writes `wBattleType`, calls `CatchTutorial` synchronously, and jumps directly to `Script_reloadmap`; the Bevy command boundary already owns that retained tutorial sequence. The redundant saved vector, snapshot/debug output, pack-save validation, and record-drain route are removed, while the live command still leaves `wScriptVar` unchanged and starts the authored Route 29 demonstration.
- Script commands no longer append a second host-only execution history beside their authoritative mutations. `writevar` writes only the selected variable, `getnum` writes only `STRING_BUFFER_3`, and mail commands retain only their real queued/application state; generic effects, variable-write records, numeric-buffer records, decoration-description records, and checked/given-mail target histories are absent from Crystal WRAM and are no longer serialized, surfaced, or drainable. Save format v15 and the mutation-schema bump reject the removed shapes without compatibility shims.
- `describedecoration` now performs `Script_describedecoration`'s source tail dispatch instead of accepting the selector as a no-op. The pack boundary resolves the equipped `wDecoPoster`, `wDecoLeftOrnament`, `wDecoRightOrnament`, or `wDecoConsole` value, fills `STRING_BUFFER_3` through the exact decoration-name table for ornaments and consoles, and queues the selected poster/common ornament/giant-doll script as `ScriptJump` does. The compiled global module now contains the source control flow for all seven targets, including the Town Map text/wait/`OverworldTownMap` sequence. `InitDecorations` also initializes the Feathery Bed and Town Map WRAM values at `InitializeEvents`; current mutation schema v42 requires resolved decoration input, deterministic setup/put-away commands, and composed-Mail attachment without a compatibility shim.
- The player-room decoration callbacks now read all eight equipped `wDeco*` values. `ToggleMaptileDecorations` writes the exact bed, plant, poster, and four carpet block values at the source coordinates and resets the poster event flag from actual equipment; removing an item clears its persisted room override. `ToggleDecorationsVisibility` selects the exact console, left/right ornament, and big-doll variable sprites from `DecorationAttributes` and hides only empty slots. The former shortcut always installed blocks `$1b`/`$1f` and hid every object regardless of saved equipment. Category-invalid saved values now reject before any graphics, flag, or block mutation.
- Every roaming-battle terminal now enters the already source-matched `BattleEnd_HandleRoamMons` implementation. Defeat and capture clear the selected slot without consuming route RNG; RUN, Poké Doll, and other DRAW/non-WIN exits save the low enemy-HP byte, update every active roamer, shift map history, and record/replay the exact divider samples atomically. The former partial helper only wrote HP or cleared the selected slot and silently skipped `UpdateRoamMons`.
- Queued script execution no longer falls back to treating an unresolved saved target string as an executable label. `QueueScript`/`FarQueueScript` store concrete script pointers in ASM; Rust now resolves the retained target against the exact global or originating-map definition set before removing it from the queue, and rejects malformed state atomically. Mutation protocol v30 excludes the prior fabricated-target replay semantics.
- A source-certified synchronous branching `callasm` can no longer fall back to the asynchronous command queue when its required live WRAM/register source is absent. Certified routines such as `SetStrengthFlag` now either finish on the invoking script command exactly as ASM does or reject the staged mutation atomically; partial effects cannot be discarded before a fabricated later retry.
- The modeled `CheckWaterfallTile` host call now matches `home/map_objects.asm` exactly: only `COLL_WATERFALL` and the unused-but-source-valid `COLL_CURRENT_DOWN` set Z. The directional Waterfall permission bytes no longer incorrectly keep `.CheckContinueWaterfall` in its climb loop, and an impossible out-of-byte accumulator rejects instead of being coerced to `$ff`.
- The branching `CheckEngineFlag` host call now preserves the routine's exact CPU return state: `a` is zero and Z is set whether the flag exists or not, while carry alone reports an unset flag. Rust previously returned `a = 1` and cleared Z for a set flag, which happened not to break the current badge callers because they branch on carry but made other certified CPU control flow diverge from `engine/events/overworld.asm::CheckEngineFlag`.
- The certified nickname host-call chain now preserves `GetNickname`/`CopyName1`/`CopyName2` as three distinct ASM buffer boundaries. `GetNickname` requires the selected party index in `a` and `hl = wPartyMonNicknames`, writes only `wStringBuffer1`, and returns `de = wStringBuffer1`; the two copy calls then populate buffers 2 and 3 from their exact pointer registers. Rust previously ignored `a`/`hl`, depended on hidden selected-party interpreter state, eagerly wrote all three buffers during `GetNickname`, and treated both source copy routines as no-ops. The direct `GetPartyNickname` host service now requires live `wCurPartyMon` rather than accepting that hidden state as a fallback.
- `CheckPartyMove` now begins by clearing live `wCurPartyMon`, scans only the contiguous party list, and defines its exact `a`, `e`, Z, and carry return state. On failure the cursor remains zero and the modeled species-list terminator leaves `a = $ff`, Z set, and carry set; on success the matching index is committed and `xor a` leaves A/Z/carry in their source state. Rust previously preserved a stale cursor after failed field-move checks and exposed only carry. Mutation protocol v31 rejects replay under the prior `callasm` host semantics.
- Dot-prefixed CPU/script targets now resolve only inside their current ASM parent scope. Already canonicalized `.Label@Parent` fallthrough edges are accepted only when `Parent` matches the caller; raw `.Label` edges are canonicalized to that same parent. Rust previously looked for a bare `.Label` definition first, so `TryStrengthOW`'s `jr c, .nope` could enter an unrelated exported `.nope`, return zero without writing `wScriptVar`, and incorrectly offer Strength despite a party with no user. The source-backed failure regression now reaches `.nope@TryStrengthOW`, returns 1, clears `wCurPartyMon`, and leaves no asynchronous queue.
- The same RGBDS parent-scope rule now covers every remaining overworld resolver: core runtime target validation, conditional background dispatch, runtime script-subset closure, menu/elevator data, movement programs, trainer callbacks, text/name references, and custom gift name/OT data. A bare `.Label` elsewhere in a compiled module is never a fallback or an ambiguity for `.Label@CurrentParent`; already-qualified edges must name that current parent. Collision regressions distinguish the scoped body from the bare body on each independently implemented resolver, and the complete compiled corpus remains valid.
- `HealMachineAnim` no longer disappears as a drained graphics event after the nurse has already healed the party. The shell retains a source-typed animation state, reveals one required `heal_machine.png` Poké Ball every 30 frames with `SFX_SECOND_PART_OF_ITEMFINDER`, draws the two Pokécenter/Elm machine lamps and six source OAM positions (including Elm's pixel offset and the Hall of Fame arrangement), rotates the exported four-color OBJ palette every ten frames for eight flashes, plays `MUSIC_HEAL` for Pokécenter/Elm or the Hall of Fame's two source SFX, locks input, and resumes the compiled script only after the animation. Heal music now owns playback through the script's following 30-frame pause until `RestartMapMusic`, preventing the ordinary map synchronizer from cutting it off immediately. This remains a shell interpretation of the source sequence rather than the broader exported generic presentation program required above.
- Ordinary wild, static, scripted, tutorial, fishing, Sweet Scent, Rock Smash, and trainer encounters now enter a retained, input-locked battle-transition boundary before any battle background, battler, HUD, or intro text is exposed. The transition selects Crystal/TypeScript's stronger-enemy rule (`player level + 3 < enemy level`) and source cave environments, performs the three packed-palette alpha sweeps except on maps declaring the source `PALETTE_DARK`/`dark` palset, and uses the corresponding cave wave, cave-stronger zoom, outdoor spin, or outdoor-stronger scatter before revealing the battle. The flash sentinel is not rendered: Rust retains the prior palette across the first two scene changes and exits after the third, for TypeScript's 25+25+24 visible draws. Trainer transitions compose the source 16×16 Poké Ball cutout inside its black 20×18 map; cave-stronger draws all nine centered boxes; outdoor weak accumulates the exact twenty quadrant/wedge table entries for two frames apiece and holds three; outdoor stronger replays TypeScript's seed-zero LCG, rejects already-black mask tiles, adds twelve cells on each of sixteen frames, and holds three. The cave wave now reproduces TypeScript's increment-before-add counter, reaches `$60` after fourteen draws, and slices the retained 160×144 viewport into two-source-line strips with its phase/amplitude displacement instead of showing no effect. The retained shell viewport substitutes for TypeScript's separately widened transition tilemap at this boundary; execution is intentionally deferred to the final grouped validation pass.
- Split-file audio callers in the battle/minigame and special-message surfaces now use one `BevyRuntimeShell` adapter backed by the runtime audio catalog and `pending_audio`. They no longer reference the pre-refactor `audio_catalog` and `audio` shell fields, preserving the concurrent decomposition while restoring those visible SFX paths.
- Trainer entry now observes TypeScript/ASM's `ShowBattleTextEnemySentOut` boundary: after the challenge page the enemy trainer leaves, the player's backpic remains, and neither the incoming enemy Pokémon nor its HUD is exposed beneath the send-out text. Dismissing that page reveals the enemy and plays its cry immediately before the player's `Go!` page. Rust previously replaced the trainer with the enemy Pokémon and HUD one page early and fired the cry at the start of the send-out text.
- Later trainer replacements now use the same presentation gate even though core has already committed the next enemy. While `<TRAINER> sent out <MON>!` is visible, the incoming enemy sprite/HUD stays hidden and the player's active Pokémon/HUD remains onscreen; dismissal reveals the enemy and plays its deferred cry. Forced and Shift player replacements likewise defer their cry until after `Go! <MON>!` is acknowledged instead of playing it when the textbox is first queued.
- Active battle rendering now consumes the per-message battle snapshots Rust was already staging instead of consulting them only after core had removed the battle. HP, status, battler identity, faint state, and switch state therefore advance with their corresponding text acknowledgement instead of the final resolved turn leaking under the first message. Player send-out presentation also recognizes ordinary and Baton Pass `Go! <MON>!` pages: the incoming player sprite/HUD remains hidden, the enemy stays visible, and the cry is keyed to dismissal of that exact page even when withdrawal or move narration precedes it.
- Whiteout recovery is now a retained visible sequence rather than an immediate heal/warp hidden behind the loss textbox. Rust holds the defeated battle or poison-faint overworld scene through `<PLAYER> is out of useable POKéMON! … whited out!`; acknowledgement begins the source eight-frame white palette fade, then holds all 40 frames required by the following `pause 40` before healing, halving money, resolving the declared spawn, aborting the relevant battle state, and running arrival callbacks while the LCD is white. Poison whiteout first presents each source `PrintText` faint notice independently. The destination map appears only through the matching eight-frame fade-in, with input locked for the whole sequence. `BATTLETYPE_CANLOSE` remains on its separate source loss-text/script path and never enters whiteout.
- Neutral battle damage no longer rewrites the retained `used <MOVE>!` scene with post-hit HP. Its post-damage snapshot is queued against that exact message and applied only on acknowledgement; multiple neutral hits from opposing actions retain independent ordered boundaries. Critical/effectiveness and subsequent action text is withheld while the HP bar is moving, and the command menu likewise remains hidden until the tween completes. Rust previously began draining HP beneath the move-use textbox and could expose the next text or command menu before `AnimateHPBar` finished.
- Ball use now owns a retained capture-animation boundary after `<PLAYER> used the <BALL>.` instead of jumping directly to the shake-result text. The shell composes Crystal's required `gfx/battle_anims/pokeball.2bpp` through exported OAM sets $0a–$0d and framesets 1–5, follows the ASM throw arc and 36/16/16-frame opening cadence, collapses the enemy through the source 7→5→3 `RETURN_MON` sizes before hiding it, stages the bounce and 48-frame wobble checks from `animation_shakes`, leaves a caught target hidden, and expands a break-free target through 3→5→7 after the two-frame poof lead-in. Ball art uses `ball_colors.asm`'s green Master, yellow Ultra/Friend, blue Great/Lure/Fast, gray Heavy/Moon, brown Level, and red Poké/Love object palettes rather than one grayscale cache. Both the initial capture poof and failed-capture release poof are rendered by the generic object path at their command frames; trainer-blocked throws likewise emit the source `BATTLE_ANIM_OBJ_HIT_YFIX` at `(112, 40)` after the twenty-frame throw wait. Master Ball additionally emits `SFX_MASTER_BALL` and all eight `$30..$37` circling sparkle objects for its 64-frame branch. `SFX_THROW_BALL`, `SFX_BALL_POOF`, `SFX_CHANGE_DEX_MODE`, `SFX_BALL_BOUNCE`, and `SFX_BALL_WOBBLE` occur at their source command boundaries; Master Ball offsets the latter two command boundaries by its exact 72-frame pre-shake branch. Initial ordinary, Master Ball, and trainer-blocked throws replay `BattleAnimFunc_ThrowFromUserToTarget`'s wrapped two-pixel X step, one-pixel Y ascent, decrementing angle, and integer sine accumulator from their distinct script-authored coordinates and amplitudes; the blocked ball additionally follows its ASM down-four/back-two rejection path instead of disappearing at the host's twenty-frame hit marker. The retained ball uses stage 8's radius-32 sine for the second opening object, remains visible in flattened frameset 3 while `RETURN_MON` completes, and uses stage 4's four 32-update radius-16/12/8/4 vertical waves before checks. Failed captures deinitialize it at the break check rather than leaving a closed ball over the release poof. The first shake check occurs at frame 276 for ordinary balls and 348 for Master Ball, replacing the fabricated shared frame-240 boundary; total caught/break-free lifetimes derive from those exact setup frames.
- Failed and trainer-blocked capture attempts no longer resolve the enemy action while Ball-use/breakout text is still queued. The enemy turn is deferred against the exact final breakout page or `Don't be a thief!`; only its acknowledgement selects/resolves the response and stages its own per-message snapshots. This prevents post-attack HP/status from leaking beneath the capture animation and restores TypeScript's action order.
- Ordinary successful move use now owns the same source ordering boundary as TypeScript/ASM: acknowledging `<MON> used <MOVE>!` selects the move's exported `BattleAnimations` table entry, retains the pre-hit battler/HUD snapshot, hides subsequent result text and the command menu, and blocks input/state presentation across its exported command/wait cadence. Misses deliberately skip this boundary while type-immune hits retain it, matching `usedmovetext -> checkhit -> moveanim`. On completion the queued damage/status snapshot retargets the HP tween and only then exposes critical/effectiveness/result text. The script timeline follows local labels, calls, jumps, loops, returns, variables, and the default parameter branch; repeated/shared `anim_sound`, `anim_cry`, `anim_obj`, `anim_clearobjs`, and `anim_bgeffect` commands therefore retain their source ordering. Animation cries resolve the visible user (including Transform identity) through the pack cry catalog. Cry playback uses species-rendered normal PCM and selects the exact `+$c0` Growl or `+$40` Roar length variant from the retained `anim_cry` selector. Ordinary move events enter with parameter zero; non-zero capture/send-out/effect-specific parameters remain separate presentation boundaries rather than guessed from a move name. Objects consume the authoritative bundle and render exact frameset durations, OAM pieces/flips, 2bpp tiles, palettes, enemy-relative mirror/fix-Y transforms, and LCD coordinates. Every exported ordinary movement function is now represented by the generic renderer; the only exported function names intentionally outside it are `POKEBALL` and `POKEBALL_BLOCKED`, which use the retained dedicated capture boundary. Generic lunge effects move the correct user/target through the source 2/4/6/8/6/4/2/0 offsets, X/Y screen shake uses exported duration/amplitude/frequency, and flash/white/black/alternate/OBJ/BG/light-dark/Acid Armor/water/Night Shade/Psychic/Teleport effects apply TypeScript's exact full-canvas colors, alphas, frequencies, and default lifetimes. Hide/Withdraw/Show replay persistent visibility for the correct user or target across redraws. Targeted light/black fades retain ASM's `$e4/$90/$40` and `$e4/$f8/$fc` palette-derived masked progression, including repeating/dual-battler variants and `incbgeffect` termination; this intentionally resolves the current TypeScript `FlashEffect` approximation in favor of cartridge behavior. The dedicated white-hold-white sequence uses the exact 17 palette-derived alpha stages and distinct low/high-nibble initial/step delays. Special animation parameter boundaries remain open generic-runtime work rather than move-name special cases.
- The generic animation compiler now retains Crystal's one-based `anim_incobj` and `anim_setobj` commands and reconstructs the fixed ten-slot animation-object table with first-free spawn allocation, natural static-frameset deletion, function-driven deinitialization, and `anim_clearobjs` teardown. Before every later object command, Rust now replays each active movement function through that exact command frame and releases any slot whose function reached its terminal `DeinitializeSprite` boundary; invisible dead objects therefore no longer consume slots and silently discard later spawns. Razor Leaf consumes the same retained slot state: all ten leaves use the source fixed-point directional scatter, sine height, `$40` flipped-half frameset entry, staggered one-based increments, four-update strike hold, and terminal 8×4 target steps.
- Leech Seed now follows its full three-stage object function rather than disappearing: the seed uses the packed high/low-nibble fixed-point X velocity, 33-step sine arc, alternating Y descent, and enemy-relative `fix_y` advance before switching at the exact 34th update to the planted frameset, waiting 64 updates, and entering the looping sprout frameset on update 99.
- Paralysis objects now choose the source normal/flipped frameset from bit 7, decode bits 4–6 as the shake delay, decode the low nibble as signed horizontal magnitude, and alternate sides on the exact delay-plus-one cadence. Both player- and enemy-side status bursts therefore remain visible at their exported positions.
- Metronome's hand now follows its continuous two-step angle advance with the source 8-pixel horizontal and 2-pixel vertical integer-sine amplitudes instead of being discarded as an unsupported object function.
- Battle-animation frameset playback now guards only control-command cycles and resets that guard whenever a frame/wait duration consumes time. The previous fixed `frames.len() * 4` traversal cap falsely rejected valid long-lived `oamend`/`oamrestart` loops after only a few cycles, causing persistent paralysis, Leech Seed sprouts, sparkles, and similar objects to vanish even though their exported framesets were valid.
- Amnesia's question-mark object now rotates through exported framesets 1/2/3 every eight updates, resets each new frameset cursor, and removes on update 32 as TypeScript/ASM do.
- Agility streaks now apply their exported one-time parameter X displacement; Growth/Swords Dance objects use the source one-step circular angle plus two-pixel-per-update ascent; and Safeguard/Protect objects orbit with the exact 24-pixel vertical and halved-horizontal integer-sine radii. These shared defensive/stat animations no longer disappear because their functions were unsupported.
- The remaining exported rapid-palette background effects now participate in the generic move-animation lifetime instead of being discarded at their command frame. `RAPID_FLASH` and `FLASH_MON_REPEATING` persist until their matching one-based `anim_incbgeffect` termination and display their two-frame `$e4/$6c` and `$e4/$fc/$e4/$00` cycles; `FADE_MON_FROM_WHITE` runs its finite `$00/$40/$90/$e4` two-frame steps as a targeted white-to-normal battler fade. This closes the TypeScript `RapidCyclePalsEffect` cases that previously allowed animations to finish early or remain visually unchanged.
- `BATTLE_BG_EFFECT_VIBRATE_MON` now executes its fixed ASM state machine: one setup update, 32 one-pixel horizontal displacement updates with direction toggled every two updates, and cleanup on the following frame. Rust previously evaluated `sin(nπ)` at integer frames, yielding zero displacement throughout, and treated the zero script duration as a two-frame lifetime; EnemyStatDown's target vibration was therefore entirely invisible.
- The two wobble background effects now use the cartridge's exact integer `BattleAnimSineWave` rather than host floating-point approximations with invented operands. `WOBBLE_MON` advances a radius-eight phase by four until its matching `anim_incbgeffect`, including immediate cleanup at that command; `WOBBLE_PLAYER` ignores its script operands, always targets the player, and advances a radius-six phase by two for exactly 32 updates. Rust previously used radii three/four, six/eight-frame fabricated lifetimes, and restarted `WOBBLE_MON` when its termination command arrived.
- `WOBBLE_SCREEN` likewise ignores all source operands and now writes the radius-six, step-two integer sine sequence to the screen X scroll for its exact 32 updates. The shared scanline compositor now treats its override bytes as `SCX` horizontal offsets rather than vertical source-row selectors, correcting Surf's existing wave axis as well. `WAVE_DEFORM_MON` uses that same horizontal scanline path over the source side-specific `$01..$36`/`$30..$5e` ranges, grows amplitude from 0 through 31, and after `anim_incbgeffect` decays 31 through 0 before cleanup; the former whole-battler six-frame vertical sine approximation has been removed.
- Screen X/Y shaking now executes `BattleBGEffects_GetShakeAmount`'s wrapping byte state exactly: the duration byte counts every update, the parameter's low nibble counts down, `swap/or` reloads it from the high nibble, and the signed displacement negates at each reload. Rust previously inferred an unrelated amplitude and frequency from the nibbles. Rollout no longer enters the Tackle horizontal-lunge approximation; its `$60,$1,$1` program produces the source one-pixel positive/zero vertical screen alternation for 96 updates and writes the simultaneous inverse displacement exclusively to live animation-object slot 1, matching `wAnimObject1YOffset` rather than shifting every object with the screen.
- Tackle and Body Slam now retain their setup and cleanup updates and the helper's source-visible `0,0,2,4,6,8,10,8,6,4,2,0` displacement sequence instead of an immediate eight-frame triangle. The unused Beta Pursuit routine uses the same jump table in the opposite initial direction. Vital Throw moves backward through that peak, pauses there until its source `anim_incbgeffect`, then runs the return helper and cleanup; Rust previously collapsed the pause-aware five-state routine into the same autonomous eight-frame lunge as Tackle.
- `BOUNCE_DOWN` now evaluates the cartridge integer cosine at phase `$20 + 2n`, adds its fixed `$11` bias, and continues for the script-owned 32/64/96 updates until `anim_incbgeffect` terminates it immediately. Its `SCY` override is represented per scanline over the exact enemy `$01..$36` or player `$2e..$5e` region, so leading rows become blank and the displaced battler clips at the region boundary. Rust previously ran a host-invented four-step 0↔8 whole-sprite triangle and could leak shifted art outside the source LCD region.
- Flail now writes the sum of the cartridge's radius-six step-two sine and radius-two step-eight sine to every scanline in its exact target battler region until `anim_incbgeffect`. Rust previously grouped it with Dig and Double Team as a whole-sprite vertical sine, interpreted unused source operands as amplitude/frequency, and ended it after six frames instead of the script's 32-frame program.
- Dig now drives the target-region `SCY` buffer through its four-step bursts: displacements `-3,-5,-7,-9`, a 16-update hold, then the next four odd negative offsets, continuing until the source script hides the user and terminates the effect. The scanline compositor clips the shifted rows within the exact side-specific LCD region. Rust previously treated Dig as the same short whole-sprite sine used for its unrelated Flail/Double Team approximation.
- Double Team now follows its six-state background routine: alternating scanlines expand from ±0 through ±15, the established split oscillates by the radius-two/step-four integer sine, the first `anim_incbgeffect` contracts it from ±16 through zero, and the second increment clears the LCD override. Rust previously reduced the complete 120-frame two-increment program to an unrelated six-frame vertical whole-battler sine.
- Acid Armor no longer fabricates a green full-screen palette tint. It initializes the source radius-eight/step-two `SCY` deformation over the user's scanline region, forces the bottom two entries to zero, then shifts the buffer downward one row per update while inserting `$90` blank rows and applying the cartridge's two tail-cleanup thresholds until `anim_incbgeffect` clears it. This reproduces the actual melting sprite program rather than merely coloring the scene.
- Withdraw no longer hides the user at its `anim_bgeffect` command. Its `$50` parameter is decoded as a 16-line displacement limit and one-line update speed; Rust now grows the target-region `SCY` displacement from one through fifteen, writes `$90` into the leading compressed rows and `~distance` into the remainder, holds the fully compressed image through the shield-object sequence, and clears at `anim_incbgeffect`. The battler remains renderable throughout the source compression instead of disappearing for the entire move.
- Haze smoke and Flame Wheel embers now share the source two-step circular angle, one-eighth vertical sine, and parameter-phase-gated downward drift, including removal when the signed drift reaches −24.
- Rain and Sandstorm objects now honor variants 0/1/2 as 2/8/4-pixel horizontal speeds after their source initialization update and wrap vertical fall to zero at `$70`; invalid exported variants surface as render errors instead of silently choosing motion.
- Heal Bell notes now select all three exported note framesets, rise for `$38` updates, oscillate at radius 24, and drift left when their source base row is even before deleting. Baton Pass now applies its absolute sine bounce and halves amplitude at each 32-angle boundary until motion settles.
- Lock-On/Mind Reader reticles now cycle exported framesets 1→2→3→4 at each four-update boundary through the source 40-update acquisition phase, retain the final frame through the following 16-update hold, and remove on update 58.
- Perish Song notes now advance angle by two, orbit at radius `$50`, use the source quarter-height sine, and descend one pixel per update. Sacred Fire uses the shared radius-24 orbit but advances downward by two on each four-angle boundary and removes at signed drift −48.
- Sludge bubbles now hold their base frameset for the source initialization plus twelve countdown updates, switch to the exported burst frameset on update 13 with a reset cursor, and rise one pixel per subsequent update.
- Solar Beam charge orbs now hold their spawn point for initialization, shrink from fixed-point radius `$28` by `$0080` per update, orbit at their parameter angle, and remove when the integer radius reaches zero. Dig's object motion now descends two pixels per update to 24, reverses at the source boundary, and removes when it returns to zero.
- String Shot objects now select exported strand framesets 1/2/3 from parameters 0/1/2, apply the source vertical flip only to variant 0 during OAM composition, and reject invalid variants instead of substituting unrelated strand art.
- Wrap now consumes its one-based object increment to advance Bind framesets 1→2 or 3→4 with a reset cursor. Metronome/Sketch sparkles use their two-angle horizontal oscillation and phase-gated 32-pixel fall; Encore/Belly Drum objects expand by two until radius 16; Swagger/Morning Sun decode angle and two-bit radius speed from the parameter; Petal Dance shares Spiral Descent; and Bonemerang holds initialization before entering its radius-48 offset orbit.
- Psych Up now uses its pre-incremented parameter angle with radius-24 quarter-height orbiting. Conversion expands one radius step through progress `$3f`, then contracts to removal with its continuously advancing angle. The generic `$b0` object now applies its packed high-nibble×`$11` and low-nibble fractional fixed-point X delta each update.
- Water Gun now moves two pixels toward the target and one pixel upward per update with its parameter-seeded sine, switches at source Y `$30` to splash frameset 2, clears enemy-relative X/Y flips at that boundary, grows the splash downward for 24 updates, and resets into deleting frameset 3.
- Spikes now use the same authoritative packed-nibble fixed-point throw, 33-step sine arc, alternating vertical descent, and enemy-relative `fix_y` advance as `step_thrown_to_target`, then remain planted at the exact landing position for the script-owned hold.
- Hidden Power now consumes the retained one-based slot increments after its 12×8-update charge loop: each orb first circles at radius 24 with quarter-height Y, holds its last orbital position on the increment update, then expands by eight pixels per update at the frozen source angle until radius `$80` removes it.
- Sky Attack now cycles gray/yellow/red/blue object palettes from its live parameter during the 64-update charge, retains the last palette across state increments with palette-aware art caching, advances four pixels to source X `$84` after the first `anim_incobj`, and advances from `$84` to removal at `$d0` after the second.
- Horn objects now retain their initialization update, travel two pixels per update to source X `$58`, hold the phase transition update, then apply the four-step 0/8/16/24 sine impact arc with half-height inverse Y before removing at amplitude `$20`.
- Sound objects now apply the enemy-side `(~param) + 3` remap before selecting exported note framesets 1–4, hold initialization, oscillate for eight updates with the source horizontal radius 16 and parameter-selected rising/level/falling diagonal, then remove on update nine.
- Confuse Ray objects now initialize from the parameter's low six bits, nibble-swap that parameter into orbital amplitude, advance the angle each update, drift right on even angles and upward on four-angle boundaries until source X `$80`, and retain their sine/cosine offsets thereafter.
- Curse nails now move two pixels left/down per update and delete once their pre-step X falls below `$30`. Beta Pursuit parameter zero rises from signed offset −20 by four through +4 and deletes on the following update; nonzero mode descends by four and holds at −40.
- Thief and Pay Day now share the source initialization hold, Y-derived sine amplitude, parameter-mask-controlled left drift, `$40` angle wrap back to `$20`, and amplitude halving at each wrap.
- Gust objects now use the exported 8/6/5/4/5/6/8/12/16 radius table, eight-step reverse angle, wrapped vertical parameter drift, and reset boundary at `$c2`; Whirlwind/Twister's retained slot increment additionally advances the object one pixel toward X `$88` and one pixel upward on even X steps before holding there.
- Strength and Seismic Toss objects now descend by fixed-point `$0080` until Y offset `$e0`, alternate the source signed two-pixel bounce on a four-update delay, consume their retained slot increment, then fly four pixels right/two pixels up per update and remove upon reaching X `$84`.
- Surf's object now initializes the SCY-owned phase, rises one pixel per update toward its parameter Y with radius-16 sine and wrapped 0–7 X offset, holds after crossing the target, consumes `anim_incobj`, then descends two pixels per update to removal at Y `$70`. `BATTLE_BG_EFFECT_SURF` now initializes the 64-entry radius-two/angle-two wave, rotates it once per active update, follows the object's live `$58`/dynamic `hLYOverrideStart` through fixed end line `$5e`, and resets when the object releases SCY ownership. Because Bevy battlers are independent textures rather than one Game Boy BG tilemap, the compositor slices their current normal/transformed/shiny/Substitute/minimized and target-overlay textures by source scanline and samples each strip at the matching SCY offset; the effect is not replaced with the unrelated flat Water overlay.
- Poison Gas and Attract objects now travel one pixel right toward X `$84`, rise on even X positions with radius-24 horizontal cosine, hold the transition update, then enter the shared one-eighth-height Spiral Descent and remove after descent `$28`.
- Kick objects now retain their source initialization update, advance two pixels right and one pixel up until X `$98`, and support the incremented radius-eight sine follow-through phase. Egg Bomb and Softboiled now retain their parameter-selected stages: both decay through the source 16- then 8-pixel vertical waves before advancing their own jumptable; Egg Bomb then performs the half-pixel ascent with 16-update pauses at each `$10` X boundary, holds at X `$88`, and lets the later script increment remove it. Softboiled approaches X `$4b`, automatically switches to the wobble frameset, and lets its later one-based slot increment switch to the cracked-bottom frame at Y offset four; the separately spawned top half uses the cracked-top frame and source held position.
- Generic `ENTER_MON` and `RETURN_MON` background effects now center-clip the targeted native battler art through Crystal/TypeScript's side-specific 2→4→6 or 3→5→7 tile-square sequence and the reverse collapse, with Return hiding the battler after its held small frame. `BATTLEROBJ_1ROW/2ROW` no longer reuse that resize model: they queue an independent fixed player-head or enemy-feet strip, clear exactly the corresponding one/two BG rows on the following update, keep that OAM strip outside later battler SCX/SCY transforms and visibility changes, restore the BG rows only when `SHOW_MON` redraws them, and retain the strip until the object-clear boundary. Their full effect lifetimes, the ordinary target light/black fade families, and Faint Attack's 17-palette white/wait/back cadence are retained by the compiled animation boundary instead of being dropped from its terminal-frame calculation.
- `REMOVE_MON` now follows `BattleBGEffect_RemoveMon`'s tile-copy jumptable instead of inventing a six-frame smooth slide: setup is followed by one whole-tile horizontal shift every four updates, right for the eight-tile enemy region and left for the nine-tile player region, with visibility ending on the final copied tile and the compiled lifetime retaining the longer player-side sequence. The shifted art is clipped to those fixed BG regions in both whole-sprite and scanline rendering, including the source layout's two-column player padding and one-column enemy padding, so pixels cannot slide into neighboring battle UI tiles.
- Psychic, Teleport, and Night Shade no longer use invented purple/blue/black fullscreen overlays. Their background effects now build the cartridge's 96-byte `DeformScreen` sine buffer (including the excluded start scanline), apply it through SCX for Psychic/Teleport and SCY for Night Shade, circularly rotate it with `BattleBGEffect_WavyScreenFX`, preserve Psychic's every-fourth-update gate, and clean up on the matching `anim_incbgeffect` command.
- The water-family effects no longer collapse to a generic blue overlay. Whirlpool now uses its 95-byte SCY sine buffer and one-position-per-update circular rotation until `anim_incbgeffect`. Start Water selects the ASM player/enemy scanline range, each Water pulse replays `DeformWater`'s expanding symmetric writes with the exact phase, amplitude transition, 16-update lifetime, shared-buffer overwrite/clear ordering, and off-visible `$5f` backup byte, and End Water disables the scanline override.
- The exported beta send-out parameter-1 path now gives `BETA_SEND_OUT_MON2` its actual 66-update SCX state machine instead of sharing a fabricated six-frame horizontal slide with `BETA_SEND_OUT_MON1`: setup clears the selected battler scanline range, the `$40` counter decrements through 64 `DeformScreen` updates, sine phase-step/radius fall together from eight to zero, and the final update disables the override.
- The source `BETA_SEND_OUT_MON1` unknown-send branch no longer uses Rust's fabricated six-frame horizontal slide. It now drives the selected battler scanlines through the exact two increment-controlled BGP passes: alternating lines advance through `$00/$40/$90/$e4`, the second pass advances the start line and fills the opposite parity plus the final scanline, and the terminal increment restores `$e4`. Per-line battler textures apply the BGP two-bit shade mapping rather than approximating these register values as alpha or tint overlays. Transparency is decided from the original BG pixel before palette lookup: transparent source color zero remains absent, while an opaque source shade mapped to color zero is drawn with the retained palette-zero RGB, matching Game Boy BG semantics.
- Global DMG palette effects now retain an authoritative BGP/OBP0/OBP1 register timeline. Flash Inverted/White honor the reload counter and finite flash count; White/Black Hues, Alternate Hues, gray/yellow full- and mid-OBJ cycling, and inverted BGP cycling replay their exact `dc` tables and `$fe/$ff` loop/end behavior; direct `anim_bgp`/`anim_obp*` writes compose in source event order. Non-default BGP values use indexed battler remapping, and the conflicting full-screen translucent approximations for these effects have been removed. The battle-animation exporter preserves each OAM piece's `OAM_PAL1` selector, and Rust remaps every opaque 2bpp object pixel through its selected live OBP register before compositing; source color zero alone remains transparent, while a nonzero color mapped to DMG shade zero remains visibly white. Applying the same BGP state to the remaining HUD/text background surfaces is still open compositor work.
- Palette-effect reload cadence and lifetime now read `BG_EFFECT_STRUCT_BATTLE_TURN`, the third `anim_bgeffect` operand, rather than misusing the initial `JT_INDEX` byte. Real source calls such as `CYCLE_OBPALS … $0, $2, $0` therefore hold each palette for three updates, finite flashes/hue tables remain alive through their exact final reload, and looping tables retain the script timeline instead of receiving fabricated four- or six-frame lifetimes. The obsolete gray/yellow translucent fullscreen overlay has been removed; these effects now act only through their authoritative OBP register writes.
- `ENTER_MON` and `RETURN_MON` now execute `BattleBGEffect_RunPicResizeScript` at its four-update entry cadence instead of treating the initial zero jump-table index as a one-frame delay. Player reveal holds 2/4/6-tile squares and enemy reveal 3/5/7 at ages 0/4/8, completing at 12; return holds the reverse sizes at the same boundaries and enters its hidden table entry at age 12 before terminating at 16. Rust previously completed the visible resize in three frames and hid a returning battler immediately afterward.
- Targeted battler palette programs now use indexed BGP remapping rather than translucent white/black silhouettes or fullscreen tints. `BGEffect_RapidCyclePals` preserves its setup update, low-nibble initial countdown, high-nibble-plus-one reload cadence, exact finite/repeating `$e4/$90/$40/$f8/$fc/$00` tables, target side, dual-battler black fade, and `anim_incbgeffect` cleanup. This covers the light/black families, light-dark cycling, repeating mon flash, Faint Attack's white hold/return, and fade-from-white without blending invented host colors over the canvas.
- `BGEffect_RapidCyclePals` `$ff` is now treated as a held end-of-table sentinel, not an automatic effect terminator. Finite light/black/white tables retain their last written BGP value and remain part of the compiled animation lifetime until the script's explicit `anim_incbgeffect` advances the effect into its restore/cleanup state. Rust previously restored `$e4` at table exhaustion and could truncate the retained animation before the source increment command.
- The compiled `BETA_SEND_OUT_MON1` lifetime now terminates at its second source `anim_incbgeffect`, which advances the two-pass scanline state into video cleanup. It no longer adds a fabricated six-frame lifetime after the final increment; the visual BGP state and animation ownership now release on the same command boundary.
- Toxic now follows `ResidualDamage`'s exact counter boundary: applying Toxic clears the target's count to zero; each afflicted battler's later residual phase increments the byte before damage, stores that incremented value, and repeats 16-bit addition that many times. A stored count of three therefore deals the fourth Toxic step, not the third. Incrementing 255 stores zero and executes 256 additions, preserving both the byte wrap and word wrap. Rust previously initialized the counter to one and multiplied before incrementing, repeating the first 1/16 step and leaving every later Toxic tick one stage behind.
- Successful move execution now increments `wPlayerTurnsTaken` and `wEnemyTurnsTaken` with byte wrapping. Rust previously saturated at 255, changing the source first-turn/X-item decisions after the counter's terminal value.
- Fury Cutter and Rollout now double the already-calculated post-STAB/type damage before variation, matching their effect-command position, instead of multiplying base move power before `DamageCalc`. Fury Cutter retains its raw wrapping byte counter rather than pinning it at the 16× cap; `$ff` therefore increments to zero and follows the source loop to saturated `$ffff` damage. Rollout likewise keeps its raw shared count distinct from the lock substatus: a miss clears only the lock, Substitute hits advance the count, the fifth hit leaves count five after ending the lock, and a later newly selected Rollout clears that stale count in `CheckRollout`. Hits redirected into Substitute also advance Fury Cutter's chain.
- Rampage and Bide now honor the command ordering of the same shared WRAM count byte. Rampage samples its 1–2 additional turns before the first accuracy check; on the terminal forced turn `CheckRampage` decrements and clears the lock, applies the source 2–3-turn confusion unless the user's own Safeguard is active, and then lets `DoTurn` immediately decrement/process that confusion, including replacing the final attack with self-damage. Active Bide runs `StoreEnergy` before obedience/status: an intermediate count prints storage and ends the effect without ticking Sleep or other `DoTurn` gates, while a terminal count clears and prepares the doubled release before confusion/status can intercept it. Rust previously started/ended Rampage after damage with generic 2–5-turn confusion and advanced Bide only after all action-prevention checks.
- Future Sight countdowns now belong to the attacking side, matching `wPlayerFutureSightCount`/`wEnemyFutureSightCount`, while their eventual hit still targets the opponent. Rust previously stored each queue on the target side; a single queued attack appeared to work because the record redundantly retained its source, but simultaneous player/enemy attacks ticked in reversed serial-clock order and persisted under the wrong side field. A dual-countdown regression covers ordinary player-first resolution and both target mappings.
- MultiHit, Double Hit, Twineedle, Triple Kick, and Beat Up now execute `checkhit` only before their first strike. `BattleCommand_EndLoop` scans backward to the `critical` command, so later strikes reroll critical and damage variation but never accuracy; Rust previously rerolled accuracy for every non-Double-Hit loop. Twineedle's `effectchance` also sits before that loop-back target and is now sampled exactly once before its first strike. In particular, a first hit that breaks Substitute cannot manufacture a second poison roll. Scripted-byte regressions cover both boundaries.
- Player recharge, charged/Fly/Dig, Rampage, and Rollout states now replace the requested menu action before validation, turn ordering, Pursuit interception, and execution, matching `CheckPlayerLockedIn`/`ParsePlayerAction`. A switch, item, or run request therefore cannot escape a committed turn or make Pursuit treat that battler as departing, and second-turn command jumps bypass obedience where the source skips `checkobedience`. Player Bide and Encore remain deliberately outside that pre-menu lock, so switching is allowed and clears those volatile states; enemy Bide is deliberately asymmetric because `CheckEnemyLockedIn` and `AI_SwitchOrTryItem` include it, blocking trainer switches/items and forcing the stored move. Rust previously treated most non-move requests as real menu actions until execution, special-cased only airborne switching, and incorrectly allowed a trainer to switch out of Bide.
- Trainer switches and items retain the move chosen by `AIChooseMove` for `DetermineMoveOrder`, then run `AI_SwitchOrTryItem` after ordering but before either ordinary move, matching both `Battle_EnemyFirst` and `Battle_PlayerFirst`. Their decision RNG follows equal-speed/Quick Claw RNG on the same stream. A trainer switch invokes the selected player Pursuit once against the outgoing enemy before recall without retroactively changing the already-determined order; the later player action is consumed just like the source writes `CANNOT_MOVE`. Rust previously assigned trainer actions synthetic menu priority 10, sampled their decision before ordering, or executed them too late at the enemy's nominal move position.
- Enemy move selection now enters the core after `HandleBerserkGene` and before ordering, so scoring observes the consumed item, updated Attack stage, source confusion state, live transformed/Conversion types, status, and active-battler turn counters instead of a stale party snapshot. `AI_Basic` now uses the complete `AI_Redundant` table and status-only list, including the source's enemy-side Mean Look check and documented Nightmare/Future Sight bugs. `AI_Setup` uses the exact per-move 128/30 Random thresholds and the correct enemy-vs-player turn counters; `AI_Types` is gated by its class flag and uses the source immune/super/not-very-effective `+10/-1/+1` deltas, including the different-type damaging-move exception; `AI_Offensive` adds exactly two only to zero-power moves. Scoring now runs as complete passes in source flag order. `AI_Opportunist` preserves the half/quarter-HP boundaries and single 50% roll before discouraging the exact stall table; `AI_Cautious` samples each residual move after turn one with the exact 230 cutoff and whole-layer early-return bug; `AI_Status` implements the explicit Poison-type check and ordinary type-immunity checks. `AI_Aggressive` and `AI_Risky` now share an exact `AIDamageCalc` path: it retains the prior `wCriticalHit` byte across turns, uses maximum unvaried damage, live screens/badges/transformed types and held type boosts, and routes Super Fang/static/level/Psywave through the separate constant-damage behavior. Aggressive preserves last-slot damage ties and reckless-effect exceptions; Risky preserves the max-HP exclusion, 200 cutoff, and strict damage-greater-than-HP KO test. `AI_Smart` now executes in its source layer position and covers all 80 source dispatcher entries, including player-used-move analysis, per-slot switch scoring, Priority damage, Hidden Power, and every HP/status/stat/RNG handler. It also preserves the documented Sunny Day, Mean Look, Conversion2, and `CheckTypeMatchup` bugs: switch-related handlers repeatedly use the currently loaded battle move type, and first-turn Conversion2 can leave `hBattleTurn` on the garbage-derived neutral player type for later slots. The core now keeps Crystal's `LAST_MOVE` and `LAST_COUNTER_MOVE` histories distinct, including the charge-turn update boundary, so Encore and counter/copy/PP handlers consult the same register as ASM. Rust previously ran selection before start-of-turn items, applied invented type weights even when `AI_TYPES` was disabled, ignored `AI_SETUP`, treated Offensive as a power bonus, reduced Basic to four status effects, and substituted unrelated accuracy/power/status heuristics for later layers.
- Sleep Talk now reaches its own source effect-command gate when the user is awake instead of being intercepted by a generic pre-effect `NoEffect` return. This preserves the effect-specific failure event/text boundary. A selected move still consumes no PP, matching `ResetTurn`; the former regression contradicted its own “without extra PP spend” premise by expecting the selected move's five PP to become zero.
- Direct confusion regression state now distinguishes application from the target's later action in the same turn. Confuse Ray's event records the source 2-5-turn roll; when the slower target subsequently reaches `CheckConfused`, it consumes one counter immediately, so the settled turn state is 1-4 rather than the unconsumed application range.
- Double Team contraction now consumes its first `anim_incbgeffect` exactly as state 3 does, decrementing `$10` to `$0f` on that same frame instead of holding the maximum displacement for one extra update. Bounce Down and Dig now replay `BGEffect_DisplaceLYOverridesBackup` rather than applying a uniform vertical translation: the leading displaced scanlines receive the source `$90` blanking sentinel and every retained scanline receives the bitwise-complement SCY sample, with their existing cosine and burst/pause state timing preserved.
- Surf's SCY wave now respects the engine's real per-frame ordering and buffer geometry. The background effect's setup frame leaves LY overrides zero because the Surf object has not enabled SCY yet; later copies use the object's prior-update `hLYOverrideStart`, rotate exactly once per active BG update, and index the 64-byte `wSurfWaveBGEffect` ring modulo `$40`. Rust previously rotated on setup, sampled the object's newly updated boundary one frame early, and continued sine phase by absolute scanline instead of wrapping the wave buffer.
- `SHAKE_SCREEN_X`, `SHAKE_SCREEN_Y`, and `WOBBLE_SCREEN` no longer consume animation lifetime invisibly or move only the battlers. Rust applies TypeScript's raw numeric turn amplitude (or packed-parameter default), low-nibble shake frequency, alternating sign, axis, sine wobble, and duration as a shared offset over every `BattleCommandMarker`: canvas, battlers, HUD, source text/windows, and animation objects now move together. Per-entity applied-offset tracking prevents high-frequency host redraws from accumulating the displacement between Game Boy frames and initializes newly composed entities from zero.
- Ordinary battlers, Substitute, Minimize, Transform, send-out growth, capture collapse, and palette-overlay copies no longer receive Rust's invented translucent rectangular ground shadow. TypeScript blits only the battler surface at this boundary and Crystal supplies no separate shadow object/tile command; the extra rectangle visibly dirtied every battle canvas and scaled with effects that should affect only the battler pixels.
- Battle trainer portraits, the gendered player backpic, enemy frontpics, and player Pokémon backpics now use their exported source dimensions at the LCD's exact four-pixel host scale and TypeScript/ASM's top-left anchors: enemy `(12,0)`, player `(2,6)`. Rust previously forced every Pokémon into an invented `2.2×1.8` or `2.4×1.9`-tile rectangle, every trainer to 2.25× native size, and approximate center coordinates; this shrank/distorted common battle art, placed the player backpic roughly 112 host pixels too low, and misaligned species whose frontpic is 5×5 rather than the maximum 7×7. Send-out scale multiplies the native frame around its full-size source center; capture and background pic-resize effects center-clip native pixels without resampling. Substitute and Minimize retain their exact 16×16 source size.
- Opening party-condition balls now sit inside the complete source trainer HUD frames instead of floating over an absent border. Rust retains the complete party frames through the trainer/backpic exit animation, then clears balls and party corners at that explicit boundary instead of keeping them under every remaining send-out/`Go!` page; wild and tutorial openings use their corresponding initial-message boundary. `DrawPlayerPartyIconHUDBorder` substitutes EXP-source tile `$5c` for the ordinary player HP corner `$77` only while the six balls are present. The frame begins at source coordinate `(18,10)`; the deleted extra `$73` at `(18,9)` had been hidden by the HP end tile during combat but protruded above every opening party display.
- The ordinary player battle HUD now anchors the nickname at source tile `(10,7)` and both level/status at `(14,8)`, with the existing genderless one-tile level adjustment and gender at `(17,8)`. Rust had placed every player nickname one tile left and every level two tiles right at `(16,8)`, colliding with the gender column and visibly disagreeing with `PrintPlayerHUD`; enemy anchors were already source-exact.
- Ordinary and Bug Contest battle command cursors now occupy the first inner window tiles at `(9,13)` and `(3,13)`, leaving their source borders at x=8 and x=2 intact; labels begin one tile later. Rust had started each cursor-prefixed string on the left border itself, shifting both menu columns left and overwriting the frame throughout every action-selection turn.
- Selected-move PP information keeps the visible `PP` label at `(1,11)` while following `MoveInfoBox.PrintPP` for the numeric field: current PP begins at `(5,11)`, the slash is fixed at `(7,11)`, and maximum PP ends at tile 9. Disabled rows continue replacing the complete detail body, and the explicit terminal-row placeholder remains scoped to the projected cancel surface.
- Battle HP palettes now use `GetHPPal`'s exact compare boundaries: 24–48 filled pixels are green, 10–23 are yellow, and 0–9 are red. Rust inherited TypeScript's inclusive threshold checks, leaving exactly 24 pixels yellow and exactly 10 pixels red; palette-transition sound/low-health presentation therefore also occurred one pixel late at each boundary.
- Ordinary player/enemy HP bars now advance one pixel every two LCD frames, matching the two `DelayFrame` calls in `HPBarAnim_BGMapUpdate`. Rust inherited TypeScript's countdown value of two but consumed it only on later updates, producing three-frame pixel spacing and making every damage/healing tween 50% slower than the cartridge.
- HP palette transitions no longer emit TypeScript's fabricated `SFX_MENU` clicks. ASM's HP animation only recalculates/applies the palette at those boundaries; Crystal's audible low-health behavior is the separate player-only danger alarm controlled by `CheckDanger`, not one-shot sounds for either battler at both palette thresholds.
- Animated battle HP bars now choose green/yellow/red from the currently drawn tween width, matching `HPBarAnim_PaletteUpdate` passing `wCurHPBarPixels` to `SetHPPal`. Rust previously colored the bar from the already-committed final HP while still drawing its old width, making damage jump immediately to yellow/red before the draining pixels crossed those thresholds (the current TypeScript renderer has the same approximation, so this follows ASM authority).
- HP fill length now retains `ComputeHPBarPixels`' high-maximum truncation: when maximum HP exceeds 255, both the `current × 48` product and the maximum are shifted right twice before the cartridge's byte-divisor operation. Rust previously used mathematically direct host division, which can choose a different filled pixel for high-HP late-game battlers even though both paths agree at ordinary low levels.
