- Player numeric HP now belongs to the retained HP tween instead of reading the already-committed battle snapshot. Each visible bar-pixel step advances through the corresponding HP values, and reaching the target pixel snaps to the exact final HP, matching `HPBarAnim_UpdateHPRemaining`; switching snaps all retained HP fields together. Rust previously displayed final damage/healing immediately beside a bar that was still visibly draining or filling.
- Battle dialogue now computes the complete 18×4 page layout before letter reveal and then reveals through those retained line breaks. Rust previously re-wrapped the partial prefix every LCD frame, allowing an already-visible word to jump down a row when later letters crossed the width boundary; source text printing never repositions glyphs that were already written.
- Retained EXP animation endpoints now use `CalcExpBar`'s remaining-to-next-level arithmetic: `64 - floor(remaining × 64 / level span)`. Rust's static HUD already used that source/TypeScript formula, but its tween helper used `floor(gained × 64 / span)`; integer rounding makes those differ by one pixel, causing the bar to shift when an EXP animation began.
- The six battle-intro party Poké Balls now apply the Game Boy OAM hardware offsets before entering Rust's ordinary screen-coordinate renderer: player icons begin at LCD `(88,80)` and advance right, while enemy icons begin at `(64,16)` and advance left. They use required `PAL_BATTLE_OB_YELLOW` colors rather than invented grayscale. Rust previously placed both rows 8 pixels right and 16 pixels down by treating the raw OAM register coordinates as LCD coordinates, then discarded their source palette.
- Battle dialogue, main-command, FIGHT, Pack, party, and prompt window fills now share the same native 20×18 LCD origin as their border tiles and bitmap text. Rust's fill-only `BATTLE_PLAYFIELD_TOP` shifted every battle interior 12 source pixels upward while borders/text stayed at the authoritative coordinates, producing exposed and overlapping window regions.
- Dedicated capture-ball and send-out smoke compositions now render at the shell's exact four-host-pixels-per-source-pixel scale and use Game Boy OAM `x−8/y−16` placement. Rust previously rendered both at half size and passed their register coordinates through a tile-center helper, displacing the ball and poof relative to the battler and generic animation objects.
- Scripted `pokepic` scenes now reproduce `PokepicMenuHeader` at tiles `(6,4)..(14,13)` and place the required grayscale 7×7 first frontpic frame at `(7,5)`, padding 5×5 art by one tile left/top and 6×6 art by one tile left as TypeScript does. Rust previously replaced this starter-selection/story surface with an invented dark 4.4-tile card, arbitrary 1.55× colored art, and non-source Pokédex/type labels.
- Target light/black fades are no longer substituted with a fullscreen tint. Rust derives and caches a silhouette from the exact active battler texture (including current frontpic frame, transformed/shiny palette, or Substitute), then alpha-composites only inside that mask. The ordinary `$e4→$90→$40` light and `$e4→$f8→$fc` black programs, their `$fe` repeating variants and `anim_incbgeffect` termination, the both-battlers black variant, and Faint Attack's `$e4/$90/$40`, eleven-white-step, `$40/$90/$e4` return sequence use the source parameter high-nibble cadence.
- Battler-art opcodes are no longer discarded by the compiled animation timeline. `anim_transform` and `anim_minimize` load temporary actor tiles without changing the visible battler; the later `anim_updateactorpic` performs the source VRAM copy and reveals the transformed or minimized picture. `anim_minimizeopp`, `anim_raisesub`, and `anim_dropsub` write the side-correct visible actor tiles directly at their command frame. These states require and render the shipped `gfx/battle/minimize.png` and compose with retained offsets, visibility, shiny palettes, animated frontpics, and target masks rather than bypassing the generic battle renderer.
- The static exported-command audit now accounts for all 36 opcodes used by `animations.json`. `anim_bgp`, `anim_obp0`, `anim_obp1`, and `anim_resetobp0` retain their ordered register values on the animation event stream; gfx declarations remain intentionally data-loading no-ops because each rendered object resolves its authoritative bundle gfx directly; battler-gfx declarations remain no-ops as in TypeScript because their visible row state belongs to the background effect; Beat Up/Poké Ball selection stays in the battle/capture boundary; and keep-sprites stays lifecycle metadata rather than inventing a visual command.
- Enemy-side relative battle-animation objects now mirror their base X coordinate and then add the function's X offset, and compute FixY from the base Y plus the function's Y offset. Rust previously mirrored/subtracted the already-animated coordinates, reversing both motion offsets for enemy projectiles and effects. The shared transform retains Leech Seed/Spikes' evolving FixY and applies TypeScript's exact one-tile enemy FixY correction only to Kinesis, Recover, Softboiled, and Milk Drink.
- Trainer and Pokémon send-out presentation now preserves the source actions between text pages instead of treating acknowledgement as an instantaneous sprite swap. After the trainer challenge, the enemy portrait shifts right eight source pixels with the exact eight-step/two-delay-frame `SlideBattlePicOut` cadence before the send-out page appears. The initial player backpic similarly shifts left for nine steps after `Go! <MON>!`; replacement sends skip that trainer-only slide. Normal enemy and player sends then run `BattleAnim_SendOutMon`'s four-frame Ball-poof hold followed by `ENTER_MON`'s centered 2→4→6 or 3→5→7 square reveal during its 32-frame boundary, with `SFX_BALL_POOF`, hidden commands/text, and locked input. The poof is composed from the shipped `smoke.2bpp` tiles using source OAM sets $00/$07/$08/$09`, including their flips, tile offsets, three-frame cadence, and expanded final frame. Replacement cries now wait until the boundary completes, and battle-entry cry scheduling is gated across both slide and send-out phases. Shiny enemies and party members extend that retained boundary through the source 64-frame shiny branch: the generic `BATTLE_ANIM_OBJ_SHINY` renderer emits all eight radius-16 sparkles at four-frame phase offsets, `SFX_SHINE` fires at every spawn, the opening inverted flash is shown, and deferred cries/frontpic animation wait until the sparkle sequence ends.
- Battle Pokémon rendering no longer applies the old shared 2.25× multiplier or forced side rectangles. Normal battlers use exported native dimensions at the LCD's four-pixel host scale; Substitute and Minimize retain their dedicated 16×16 source dimensions. True send-out/capture effects scale about the native center, while `ENTER_MON` and related pic-resize effects crop without resampling.
- Enemy frontpics now execute the same exported per-species `frame` / `setrepeat` / `dorepeat` / `endanim` program used by TypeScript after wild appearance and enemy send-out. The interpreter applies TypeScript's exact `duration + floor(duration * speed / 16)` timing (normal speed for wild/static encounters and slow speed 4 for trainer sends), retains repeat-pointer semantics, and reads the requested vertical frame from the shipped multi-frame PNG instead of permanently cropping row zero. Animation frames have their own sprite-cache identity, reset on enemy replacement/battle exit, and do not incorrectly lock the Go/text boundary while they play.
- Battle front/back sprites now select the shiny palette from Crystal's exact DV predicate (Defense/Speed/Special 10 and Attack 2/3/6/7/10/11/14/15). The renderer previously hard-coded `shiny=false` for both sides even though the palette loader and shiny assets already existed, so Lake of Rage Gyarados and every legitimately shiny party/wild Pokémon displayed normal colors. The additional send-out sparkle objects remain part of animation-command rendering.
- Ordinary faint events now queue Crystal/TypeScript's 24-frame `BattleAnim_FaintMon` boundary with `SFX_FAINT` and the 20-frame downward `BATTLE_BG_EFFECT_FAINT_MON` movement. Its progress-zero hold, two-update/four-pixel descent cadence, and terminal visibility boundary match TypeScript. The staged scene retains the living battler while that animation runs, then commits the zero-HP scene so the sprite disappears at the effect boundary instead of vanishing immediately when the event batch is projected.
- Successful capture presentation now plays `SFX_CAUGHT_MON` exactly when the completed ball animation exposes `Text_BallCaught`, and first-time registrations play `SFX_SLOT_MACHINE_START` when acknowledging that page exposes `NewDexDataText`. Rust previously displayed both source text pages silently even though their embedded text commands own those sounds.
- Capture balls now use the exported `BATTLE_ANIM_FRAMESET_POKE_BALL_1` through `_5` and their exact OAM sets throughout flight, opening, flattened capture, bounce, closed hold, and wobble phases. Rust's deleted specialized cache had only two fabricated 16×16 composites, hid the retained flattened ball for 24 frames, alternated open art during unrelated phases, and could not represent OAM sets `$0c/$0d`. The shared object compositor now keeps tile offsets relative to each object's declared base frameset when an animation function overrides its frameset, matching the source VRAM contract instead of rebasing the override to tile zero.
- A successful final shake now honors `anim_keepsprites`: the closed ball and hidden target remain on the battle canvas through `Text_BallCaught` and `NewDexDataText`, then the retained object is removed at the source `ClearSprites` boundary before nickname handling. Rust previously cleared the capture animation on the click frame, making the ball disappear and the captured Pokémon reappear beneath both success pages.
- Retained terminal battle scenes now compose capture objects, poofs, and the capture-driven target clipping/visibility state just like active battle scenes. Tutorial and Bug Contest capture paths commit their battle result before the animation finishes; Rust previously switched render branches at that commit and silently dropped the entire remaining ball/collapse presentation even though its retained pre-result battle scene was still displayed.
### Magnet Train presentation

- Rust now retains the `MagnetTrain` graphics special instead of draining it as a no-op. It blocks script continuation, reads `wScriptVar` for the Goldenrod/Saffron direction, uses the ASM/TypeScript positions and three 128-frame waits, and scrolls the 47/48/49-pixel screen bands from the native station tilemap assets. `SetMagnetTrainPals` is reproduced per source tile: rows 0–3 and 14–17 use `PAL_BG_GREEN`, rows 4–13 use `PAL_BG_GRAY`, and window tiles 7–12 on row 8 use `PAL_BG_YELLOW`, all through the station tileset's current time-of-day palette bank rather than the raw grayscale PNG. Its `PlayMusic2` boundary stops only music, consumes `DelayFrame`, and starts `MUSIC_MAGNET_TRAIN` on the following frame. The first-run jumptable now initializes the gender-selected Chris/Kris OAM layer at source X/Y, loads the exact standing/down-walk frames with `PAL_OW_RED`/`PAL_OW_BLUE`, advances the four-entry 8-count frameset including the final X flip, applies the movement phases' one- and two-pixel global X offsets, and composites `OAM_PRIO` behind BG colors 1–3 while remaining visible through color zero. The final arrival frame retains `SFX_TRAIN_ARRIVED` before resuming the map script.
- Ruins wall inscriptions no longer disappear through the generic runtime-visual drain. `DisplayUnownWords` validates the four source constants, retains the TypeScript/ASM-sized centered window over the chamber, displays ESCAPE/LIGHT/WATER/HO-OH, locks unrelated controls, and resumes the compiled script only after A/B with `SFX_READ_TEXT_2`.
- Retained healing-machine presentation now accepts the exact symbolic `HEALMACHINE_POKECENTER`, `HEALMACHINE_ELMS_LAB`, and `HEALMACHINE_HALL_OF_FAME` values emitted by compiled `setval`, as well as their 0–2 compiled values. The previous numeric-only parse could abort Elm's heal and the Hall of Fame sequence before showing their source animation. All eight Game Boy controls are consumed for the retained sequence so an animation frame cannot also advance dialogue, open a menu, or move the player.
- `Diploma` no longer disappears through the generic visual-special drain. Rust composes ASM's 20×18 `page1.tilemap` from `diploma.png` with the source palette, overlays the player name and certificate copy at `PlaceDiplomaOnScreen` coordinates, reproduces the 16-frame lower-right blinking cursor, blocks unrelated input, and returns to the Game Freak script only after A or B.
- `PrintDiploma` no longer disappears through that generic drain either. It retains the composed diploma beneath the Game Boy Printer connection-status window, exposes source `Printer Error 2` because no printer transport exists, ignores A/Select, and only B cancels printing, restores the map, and resumes the compiled graphic-artist script. The previous path silently reported completion without displaying the diploma or attempting the printer program.
- Credits now preserve ASM/TypeScript skip ownership. The first Hall of Fame record remains unskippable; a repeat championship detects the pre-existing record after core inserts the newest team and enables the credits skip bit; Red's `credits` opcode enables skipping from the Hall-of-Fame status/count/history state. Rust previously hard-coded `allow_skip=false` for both routes.
- The Pokémon Center Hall of Fame PC now opens at entry zero, matching core/TypeScript's newest-first history insertion, and exposes TypeScript's `HALL OF FAME #01` viewer with six numbered team slots. Left/Up and Right/Down wrap across every retained championship with the menu sound; A/B returns to the PC hub, while Start/Select are consumed. Rust previously used `.last()` and then a static debug-like summary of only one record, showing the oldest retained championship while labeling it as current.
- Surf now retains the land scene through the use textbox, switches to the authoritative surf sprite/map scene only when that text closes, and interpolates the committed player position through ASM/TypeScript's one 16-frame `slow_step` onto the facing water tile while input remains locked. Rust previously committed the core destination invisibly beneath the retained text and then snapped directly onto the water with no `SurfStartStep` presentation.
- Selecting CONTINUE no longer loads the save directly from the title-menu row. Rust now opens TypeScript/ASM's native 16×10 Continue data screen first, with PLAYER, total BADGES, conditional Pokédex-owned count, and three-digit game TIME at `DisplayNormalContinueData` coordinates. A confirms the already validated exact save, B returns to the title menu, and directions do not move the hidden title cursor beneath it. Host save paths, frame numbers, pack IDs, versions, and hashes remain diagnostic-only rather than becoming game-screen Continue data.
- Ordinary player motion now consumes core's authoritative movement-speed multiplier: walking/surfing crosses a tile in eight LCD frames while bike/skate crosses it in four, matching TypeScript's `WALK_FRAMES` and fast-step cadence. Turning holds for TypeScript's four frames before a held direction starts its step, and the renderer divides interpolation by the actual step duration instead of hard-coding eight frames (which also fixes Surf's 16-frame `slow_step` appearing stuck for its first half).
- Continuous player walking now preserves TypeScript's four-step held sprite cycle across tile landings: action, stand, horizontally mirrored action, stand. Rust previously cleared its stride phase whenever interpolation reached zero, so every tile restarted on the same action frame and the same foot appeared to slide—most visibly while walking up or down. Horizontal directions retain their source directional mirroring while only the vertical action frame alternates feet.
- Autonomous NPCs, trainer approaches, and compiled object movement now retain that same four-step stride independently per object and reset their phase when changing facing. Rust previously drove every pedestrian and story actor from one global bit, cleared it when each interpolation ended, and therefore both reused the same foot and let an unrelated NPC's movement choose the next object's pose. Vertical NPC action frames alternate horizontal mirroring exactly like TypeScript; stationary frames still interpolate position on the intervening stride steps.
- Ordinary step warps now retain the landed source-map frame through `MAPSETUP_DOOR`'s eight-frame white fade-out, expose the already-loaded destination only beneath the fully white palette, and retain input ownership through the matching eight-frame fade-in. Scene scripts and map callbacks resume after the fade-in rather than emitting invisible text, emotes, or audio under white. Their source collision permission is preserved on the authoritative warp trigger and selects TypeScript/ASM's door, warp-panel, or exit-building sound at the landing boundary. Map connections continue through their separate seamless setup path without an invented fade. Rust previously released the destination immediately after the walking interpolation landed, omitted the original transition sound/boundary, and allowed held movement to enter the new map during it.
- Compiled `warp` / `warpfacing` transitions now retain `MAPSETUP_WARP`'s distinct presentation: the source disappears at the LCD-disable boundary and the loaded destination fades in from white over eight frames, with arrival scripts and callbacks deferred until the palette is restored. Rust previously snapped directly to the destination and could begin its script work on that same frame.
- Map-name signs now execute the cartridge's shared `InitMapNameSign` authority for every setup path that reaches it directly or through `RefreshMapSprites`, including connections, door/script/spawn warps, Fly/field travel, blackout recovery, Continue, and reloads. Core persists exact `wCurLandmark`, `wPrevLandmark`, `wMapNameSignFlags`, and `wLandmarkSignTimer` bytes; NewGame seeds New Bark, `FinishContinueFunction`'s bit-1 suppression is one-shot, gates become `$ff`, the five special landmarks remain silent, and ordinary changes arm 60. `PlaceMapNameSign` uses the source pre-decrement values: old 60 is the setup hold, old 59 initializes text and exposes fixed `WY=$70`, old 1 leaves the timer-zero frame visible, and the following old-zero pass hides at `WY=$90`. The retained renderer is invalidated at those exact show/hide boundaries, so an idle player cannot skip the sign while ordinary countdown frames still avoid rebuilding the map.
- Autonomous NPCs now retain their pre-commit tile and visibly interpolate into the destination over eight LCD frames. Rust previously committed the NPC tile immediately and only changed its walk-cycle image, making wandering characters teleport one tile at a time. Scripted trainer approaches retain their distinct 16-frame `slow_step` cadence.
- Autonomous movement no longer fires every eligible object on one fabricated global 16-frame beat. Each object retains its source `OBJECT_STEP_DURATION`: ordinary X/Y/XY walkers select the raw `hRandomAdd` direction bits, own an independent eight-frame stride, and perform the separate `$00..$7f` idle-duration Random call only when that stride lands; blocked steps roll the slow wait immediately. Sleep bytes use cartridge wrapping (`$00 → $ff`) and return to the movement function on the frame after expiry rather than accelerating the next action by one frame. Slow and fast random spinners use the source direction bits and `$7f/$1f` duration masks, including fast-spin same-facing XOR, while clockwise/counterclockwise actors retain their initial 16-frame sleep and independent 16-frame turns. Production supplies shared gameplay RNG whenever any autonomous behavior is present instead of only on the old synchronized random-object tick. Bevy retains origin, remaining/total ticks, collision ownership, and walk phase per object, so a second NPC can start without snapping or restarting one already in flight.
- While a committed player step is still visibly landing, Rust now accepts only directional continuation input. A interactions, Start, Select/registered items, and contextual field moves cannot dispatch from the destination tile early and snap the presentation into a textbox or menu before the sprite arrives.
- A direction pressed during an active step is now buffered until landing. Rust's atomic core previously accepted a changed direction immediately because its held-repeat counter only throttled the original direction, allowing a second tile commit while the first tile was still being drawn. Held straight movement and mid-step turns now both begin their next step on the landing boundary, as TypeScript does.
- An authoritative player or autonomous-object movement result now ends the current host-timer batch immediately. When several LCD ticks accumulated between Bevy updates, Rust previously replayed movement through tile-atomic core before installing the first result's visible cadence; a single rendered update could commit multiple player/NPC tiles, turn and step without Crystal's four-frame facing hold, or make pedestrians snap past intermediate positions. Player walks, NPC walks, turns, and bumps now each become a hard presentation boundary before another core movement is accepted.
- Ordinary in-flight player interpolation now continues feeding empty authoritative overworld frames so autonomous NPCs and world timers progress concurrently with the eight/four-frame walk, matching TypeScript's single shared update loop. Rust previously stopped the complete overworld authority while drawing every atomic player step, making pedestrians effectively freeze during continuous walking. Ice, currents, and downhill forced movement retain the stricter landing gate because an empty joypad frame owns their next atomic step; pending warps, scripts, trainers, encounters, and step events remain frozen behind their existing arrival boundary.
- Overworld direction arbitration now mirrors TypeScript's ordered held-direction map. A newly pressed direction owns one queued step, after which movement resumes from the oldest direction still held; only that selected direction reaches the authoritative joypad mask. Rust previously forwarded every physically held arrow in fixed Up/Down/Left/Right order, so ordinary perpendicular cornering or opposite holds could reach core as a conflicting D-pad mask and abort play instead of turning fluidly.
- Ice, directional water currents, and downhill bike/skate coasting now chain through core's forced-movement authority only at visible landing boundaries. The Bevy loop no longer feeds empty in-flight frames that let atomic forced movement race several tiles ahead of presentation; current direction changes bypass the voluntary turn hold, downhill idle movement forces Down, and downhill movement in any other direction uses `STEP_WALK` speed rather than bike speed, matching TypeScript/ASM.
- Blocked ground, bike, and skate steps now emit `SFX_BUMP` on the collision frame, including object and invalid-edge collisions. Rust previously changed facing/returned a blocked outcome silently; Surf retains TypeScript's distinct silent blocked-water behavior.
- Held wall/object collisions now preserve ASM `BumpSound` ownership: the one-frame bump movement remains immediately retryable, but `SFX_BUMP` is queued only when no transient sound is active or already pending. Rust previously enqueued a bump on every blocked host frame; its single-transient mixer stopped the preceding cue for each replacement, producing chatter or a sound that continually restarted instead of Crystal's `CheckSFX`-gated cadence.
- Compiled `applymovement` no longer presents an actor only at the authority's already-committed final tile. Rust reconstructs each verified movement command into a retained blocking presentation queue: normal, slow, bike-speed, slide, and two-tile jump steps use TypeScript's 8/16/4-frame profiles; jump height indexes the exact 16-entry ASM table at the corresponding 1/2/4-pixel rate; head/bump turns retain one frame while slow/walk/waterfall turning steps retain 16/8/4; exact and parameterized sleeps plus `step_wait_end`, Dig, shake, Rock Smash, and return-Dig effects retain their declared duration. Multiple sequential programs and different actors queue without overwriting one another, player movement scrolls the camera through the same retained scene, object movement uses per-command duration rather than the trainer-only 16-frame assumption, and script continuation remains locked until the final phase lands. Tree shake uses TypeScript's exact repeating `[0,-1,1,-1,1,0]` source-pixel offset sequence for all 24 frames and its movement pose. Teleport follows ASM rather than TypeScript's divergent frozen 24/64-frame placeholders: TeleportFrom spins for 16 frames and enters its 16-frame amplitude-$60 rise at the same-tick `Sine($11, $60) - $60 == -1` sample; TeleportTo retains the initializer/wait frame plus 16 wait frames at -96 px, descends through the inverse table for 16, and performs the final 16-frame spin. Counterclockwise facing changes every four frames. `skyfall` is no longer drained as a zero-motion effect after `MAPSETUP_FALL`: its 16-frame setup preserves the incoming sprite Y offset (zero for an ordinary PIT tile or $60 after `skyfall_top`), enters the first `Sine(1, $60) - $60` fall sample on the setup countdown's terminal object tick, completes the exact 16-frame amplitude-$60 descent, and alternates the step pose before `SFX_STRENGTH`/landing earthquake continue. SkyfallTop retains the player's direction while advancing `SetFacingSkyfall`'s two-tick action-frame/mirror cadence, then preserves its terminal $60 sprite offset after `applymovement` returns; TeleportFrom likewise remains at -$60 until its following disappear/warp commands.
- Player/object visibility is now part of the retained overworld snapshot and scripted-movement boundary. Rust previously ignored `player_hidden` entirely in its renderer and captured only the already-final visible-object list, so Dig/removal sequences could hide an actor before its preceding movement appeared or keep the player drawn through a hidden phase. Movement outcomes now retain their authoritative pre-program visibility, queued programs restore it alongside position/facing, and `hide_object`, `remove_object`, `show_object`, Dig, and return-Dig change the retained scene at their own compiled command boundary.
- Dig/Escape Rope movement now visibly performs the source 32-frame counterclockwise spin from the actor's current facing before `hide_object`, and restores visibility before the matching 32-frame return spin. Rust previously rendered both declared durations as motionless standing holds, then toggled visibility, despite core having already accepted the complete `.DigOut`/`.DigReturn` programs.
- Compiled `step_shake` now spawns the same parameterized screen-shake boundary as `Script_earthquake`: the top two bits select the 1/2/4/8-source-pixel displacement and the lower six bits own its lifetime while the paired sleep blocks the movement program. Rust previously treated the complete encoded byte as a motionless hold, producing neither the source amplitude nor the correct duration split.
- Compiled `rock_smash` movement now alternates the actor's stand-action and ordinary frame from the remaining-duration parity on every retained tick, matching `StepFunction_RockSmash`. Rust previously held one standing image for all ten source frames even when the field effect and sound were visible around it.
- Beat Up now owns one move-animation boundary per authoritative `BeatUpParticipant` event instead of playing only one parameter-zero animation behind the initial “used” line. The active battler uses the current-mon branch; each other eligible party member takes the nonzero branch, returns the prior actor, applies `anim_beatup` at its source command frame, renders that participant's species and shiny palette for the tackle/hit sequence, and restores the authoritative active battler after the animation. Participant attack text remains the trigger page for its corresponding animation.
- Charge and semi-invulnerable setup turns now pass animation parameter 1, so SolarBeam, Razor Wind, Skull Bash, Sky Attack, Fly, and Dig run their ASM preparation branch instead of displaying the damaging animation one turn early.
- Present now derives its animation parameter from the authoritative turn result: damage gifts retain parameters 0–2's shared explosion branch, healing gifts pass parameter 3 and render the recovery branch, and a full-HP healing failure follows `AnimateFailedMove` without falsely exploding. Rust previously launched every Present with parameter zero before projecting the already-known heal/failure event.
- Curse now selects its animation from the authoritative effect result: a successful non-Ghost stat change passes parameter 1 for the black-fade stat animation, a successful Ghost curse retains parameter 0 for the nail/self-damage animation, and either source failure skips the move animation through `AnimateFailedMove`. Rust previously rendered the Ghost branch for every Curse, including maxed-stat and protected-target failures.
- Successful Roar and Whirlwind results now pass animation parameter 1, retaining their ordinary sound/wind sequence and then removing the forced-out target before the replacement scene appears. `ForceSwitchFailed` follows ASM's failed-move delay without playing either successful branch. Rust previously passed zero for both outcomes, so a successful target remained visibly present through the switch boundary.
- Selfdestruct and Explosion now pass ASM's unconditional parameter 1 once `SelfdestructDamage` proves their effect command executed. The full repeated explosion hides the user before the faint scene, including when the attack misses its target; Rust previously either played parameter zero's short impact or skipped the animation entirely on a miss, leaving the already-fainted user visibly standing.
- Completed multi-hit turns now queue one compiled move animation per authoritative landed hit instead of showing only the first. Ordinary multi-hit/Double Hit/Poison Multi Hit animations follow `BattleCommand_MoveAnimNoSub`'s parameter toggle (`1,0,1…`), while Triple Kick uses its `KickCounter` sequence (`0,1,2`) for the three distinct impact positions. Consecutive animations share the acknowledged used-move page; after each hit with a retained damage scene, Rust retargets and completes the exact one-pixel/two-frame HP tween before starting the next animation. Chains with no intermediate HP mutation continue immediately, and the final result scene remains hidden until the complete sequence settles.
- Bide now follows `StoreEnergy`/`UnleashEnergy`: the initial turn passes parameter 1 for the energy aura, intermediate turns show only the storing-energy text and do not fabricate another used-move page or animation, and release retains parameter 0 for the Mega Punch strike. Rust previously played the release punch during every Bide phase.
- Metronome and Sleep Talk now retain their setup animation and then queue the authoritative selected move's compiled animation; Mirror Move's dummy base animation likewise flows directly into its copied move. The queue follows nested recorded selections (for example Sleep Talk→Metronome→selected move) with cycle protection. Called animations reuse the selected effect's typed outcome for Present/Curse/forced-switch/Selfdestruct/Bide/setup parameters and exact multi-hit sequences, while a called effect that reached its source failure boundary skips the fabricated success animation but retains `AnimateFailedMove`'s 40-frame delay and Substitute choreography. Rust previously animated only the caller and applied the selected move invisibly.
- `DestinyBondActivated` now chains `BattleAnim_DestinyBond` parameter 1 behind the bonded Pokémon's faint animation, with animation context switched so `BG_EFFECT_TARGET` removes the attacker before its own faint scene. Rust previously applied the second faint and displayed the result text without the source activation animation.
- Future Sight no longer plays its attack animation on the queuing turn: `FutureSightQueued` retains only the used/foresaw text boundary, matching the early `EndMoveEffect`. When `FutureSightLanded` appears three turns later, its hit text now triggers the compiled parameter-zero animation in the original source side's battle context before the retained delayed-damage scene settles. Rust previously inverted those visible boundaries.
- Jump Kick and Hi Jump Kick misses now retain ASM's post-`CrashedText` parameter-1 animation: the impact object and sound target the user before crash HP settles. Rust's generic miss filter previously removed the complete crash animation even though `BattleCommand_JumpKick` explicitly loads it after reporting the crash.
- Fly/Dig semi-invulnerability is now an authoritative render-snapshot field rather than a transient side effect of whichever animation is currently at the queue front. Event-scene reconstruction reverses the already-committed `AirborneStarted/Ended` state and reapplies it in source order, so setup begins visible, the compiled hide/warp animation controls disappearance, the user remains absent through the intervening battle boundary and opponent animation, and the second-turn result releases visibility at `AirborneEnded`. HUDs remain present. Rust previously redrew the battler as soon as the setup animation object was popped.
- Fly/Dig misses without a Substitute preserve the hidden user beneath the miss textbox and apply the retained `AirborneEnded` scene only when that page is acknowledged. With a Substitute, their explicit `moveanimnosub; raisesub` command sequence instead retains the 40-frame miss delay, runs only parameter-2 RaiseSub, and exposes the restored doll beneath failure text. This matches `BattleCommand_FailureText`/`AppearUserRaiseSub`; the unused Fly/Dig parameter-2 move-animation branch is deliberately not fabricated.
- Command-owned defensive/status effects now suppress their success animation when their typed source command fails validation: Substitute, Transform, Mimic, Sketch, Conversion/Conversion2, Metronome/Sleep Talk/Mirror Move, Teleport, Bide, healing, Future Sight, Mist, Focus Energy, Spikes, Foresight, Perish Song, Protect, Endure, Safeguard, Nightmare, Spite, Disable, Encore, Reflect, Light Screen, and Attract. This is deliberately limited to scripts whose command owns animation after its failure gate; secondary-effect failures on ordinary attacks still retain the attack animation. Rust previously treated any `MoveUsed` event as proof that these success animations ran.
- OHKO failures and unsuccessful zero-power stat moves now follow the same `AnimateFailedMove` boundary: their failure text appears without first playing the move's success animation. A stat move is suppressed only when the resolved event batch contains no successful stage change for that side/move, so mixed outcomes such as multi-stat effects retain the animation when any source command actually applied.
- Grass rustle now follows the player's interpolated draw position throughout the active step, matching TypeScript's target-bound `GrassRustleInstance`. Rust previously anchored it immediately at the committed destination tile, so the grass moved a full tile ahead while the player was still crossing into it—especially conspicuous during an eight-frame walk.
- A direction pressed during an in-flight tile step is now retained separately from held-step throttling and forwarded alone on the landing boundary. Rust previously replaced it from a fixed directional scan and then forwarded conflicting held directions, making cornering depend on key-order rather than the newest queued turn.
- Seamless map connections now keep sampling the physical D-pad while the retained crossing step finishes and preserve a newly queued corner through connection setup along with held arbitration and foot phase. Rust previously returned at the pending arrival boundary before input synchronization, so a perpendicular press during the seam was lost even though holding straight across the same boundary continued correctly.
- Ledge jumps now retain their queued landing direction for the complete 16-frame `STEP_LEDGE` presentation. Rust previously treated only the overlapping eight-frame ordinary walk timer as in flight; during the jump's second half it entered idle arbitration, consumed the queued corner into an input-cleared frame, and made responsive landing turns disappear.
- A-button object interactions now play `SFX_READ_TEXT_2` at `TryObjectEvent`'s pre-dispatch boundary for ordinary NPCs, item balls, trainers, Strength boulders, and smashable rocks. Background and collision events remain silent unless their own scripts request audio. Rust previously dispatched every visible object interaction without ASM/TypeScript's talk cue.
- A-button dispatch now rejects an autonomous object for the complete duration of that object's retained walk interpolation, matching `CheckFacingObject`'s `OBJECT_WALKING == STANDING` gate. This includes the frame on which Rust atomically commits the NPC destination and creates its visible walk. Rust previously exposed only committed object tiles to core interaction lookup, allowing the player to grab and run an NPC script while the character was visibly still stepping.
- Autonomous NPC walk origins now remain collision-owned until their retained interpolation lands, with ordinary `SFX_BUMP` gating when the player pushes into them. This mirrors ASM's simultaneous `OBJECT_MAP_*` destination and `OBJECT_LAST_MAP_*` origin occupancy and TypeScript's moving pixel footprint. Rust previously exposed only the atomically committed destination to core collision, allowing the player to walk through the half-visible trailing side of a moving NPC.
- Autonomous objects now persist each vacated origin as the source `OBJECT_LAST_MAP_*` collision coordinate until that object's independent eight-frame stride lands. Both player movement and later NPC scheduler frames treat the visible origin and committed destination as occupied, while same-frame walkers also reserve newly vacated tiles immediately. Rust previously checked only committed destinations (and briefly only one scheduler batch), so independently timed pedestrians could overlap at the trailing edge of a visible stride.
- Retained player and autonomous-object origins now release at the shell's exact visual landing boundary as well as carrying their deterministic core-frame expiry. Ice, current, and downhill chains deliberately pause core ticks while each atomic move is interpolated; relying only on the core frame counter left invisible old origins collision-owned across later forced tiles after their sprites had already landed.
- Downhill bike/skate steering now applies `TryStep`'s non-downward `STEP_WALK` override to retained-origin collision timing as well as the reported visual speed. Rust already rendered these sideways/uphill steps over eight frames, but the core expiry was still calculated from the actor's ordinary four-frame bike mode, allowing an autonomous object to claim the player's visibly occupied origin halfway through the stride.
- Tile-forced movement now implements `CheckTile`'s full direction table: currents, both directional-walk ranges, and the source `DOOR`/`DOOR_79`/`STAIRCASE`/`CAVE` permissions continue in their authored direction without an extra input. Downhill remains distinct and passes through the ordinary four-frame `CheckTurning` boundary before coasting down; Rust previously omitted forced entrance/walk permissions and incorrectly made downhill snap into its first step without turning.
- Autonomous first strides now interpolate from each visible object's resolved map-event tile. The Bevy change detector previously snapshotted only `object_runtime_tiles`; that map has no entry until an NPC first commits movement, so every pedestrian's first step after loading a map bypassed the eight-frame presentation and snapped directly to its destination. Invalid initial object coordinates now fail at the same authoritative boundary instead of being silently absent from detection.
- Strength boulder pushes now interpolate the object for the authoritative 16-frame slow step while the player remains on the origin tile after the source bump. Object-tile change detection runs for every authoritative overworld tick, not only ticks labeled as autonomous movement, and Bevy derives each object's visible timer from the core step duration rather than truncating every object to eight frames. The boulder's trailing tile remains independently collision-owned through the exact landing boundary.
- Every `MovementFunction_Strength` member (`SPRITEMOVEDATA_STRENGTH_BOULDER`, `BIGDOLLASYM`, and `BIGDOLL`) now spawns ASM's `BoulderDustGFX`; `BIGDOLLSYM` correctly remains excluded because it uses BigStanding despite carrying the palette flag. Dust lifetime is derived from the live object step with the source `(OBJECT_STEP_DURATION + 1) * 2` byte formula, yielding 34 visible frames for a 16-frame push instead of the former hardcoded 18. The renderer repeats each source 8x8 tile across the four dust quadrants, alternates tiles every two frames, uses `PAL_OW_EMOTE`, and applies the direction-specific native-pixel offsets from `MovementFunction_BoulderDust`.
- Position-only overworld rendering now yields to full OAM composition while a ledge jump, grass rustle, or Strength dust effect is active. Those effects are created on the same authoritative tick that changes player/object coordinates; the old fast path moved retained player/NPC transforms and returned before spawning the new shadow/rustle/dust sprite, dropping the effect's first visible frame.
- Player walk origins now remain authoritative collision coordinates for autonomous NPC movement until the exact walk/bike/ledge frame expiry. Core records the last runtime tile and an absolute expiry when a step commits, map transitions clear both, and wanderers exclude that tile before random candidate selection instead of selecting it and losing an otherwise valid movement turn. Rust previously let NPC scheduling inspect only the atomically committed player destination, so an NPC could enter the player's still-visible origin during interpolation and overlap the moving sprite; ASM and TypeScript reserve both current and last coordinates.
- Overworld sprite depth now uses TypeScript/ASM's complete render ordering key: runtime Y, then X, then inverse original object slot, with the player treated as the sentinel largest slot. The render snapshot carries source slots separately so hiding an earlier event object cannot renumber every later sprite's OAM priority, and the same key is reapplied during retained interpolation updates. Rust previously encoded only destination Y and added a fixed player bias, making the player incorrectly cover every same-row NPC and leaving equal-depth object ordering to Bevy rather than Crystal's OAM priority.
- The Rust tileset cache now builds a palette-zero-transparent counterpart for every rendered 8×8 tile, preserving the selected time-of-day palette for nonzero colors. A second retained viewport applies TypeScript's exact priority-collision catalog and its half-tile clipped upper edge for floor-over-wall/priority foreground faces, scrolls with the base map, and draws above ordinary player/NPC sprites. Tall/long grass, counters, bookshelves, incense burners, mart shelves, PCs, and foreground bottoms therefore occlude sprites instead of remaining permanently behind them. Rust previously flattened every map tile into one opaque background below all objects.
- Item balls now follow TypeScript's second priority pass: source object slots classified as `OBJECTTYPE_ITEMBALL` render above the foreground plane unless their exact runtime collision quadrant is one of the grass permissions, in which case grass continues to cover them. The retained position updater preserves the same layer while objects or the camera interpolate. Rust previously had no foreground pass and therefore no equivalent of the item-ball `overhead` exception.
- Cave wild-battle wave strips now sample and shift the retained transparent priority viewport alongside the base map at the same source rows and horizontal phase. Rust previously redrew opaque base-map strips above the live scene during the distortion, causing newly restored grass/counter/foreground occlusion to disappear until battle entry completed.
- Connected-map rendering now builds TypeScript/ASM's composite coordinate bounds instead of clamping the camera to the current map. Every viewport tile outside the base rectangle resolves through connection segments in source order (later overlaps win), translates the declared block offset and direction into the target map, and uses that map's own blocks, border, tileset, time-of-day palette, collision priority, and transparent foreground art. All referenced neighbor art is loaded before composition and participates in the retained visual cache key. Rust previously painted the current map's border block at every edge, so approaching or crossing a seamless connection visibly hid the neighboring route/town until after the atomic map swap.
- Ambient viewport invalidation now merges and deduplicates animation schedules from the current map and every visible connection tileset. Neighbor water, flowers, fountains, scrolling cave tiles, and forest-restless phases therefore continue updating before the player crosses the seam; Rust previously watched only the base tileset, leaving connected-map animated tiles frozen despite composing their art.
- Battle exit, load, and navigation resets now clear held-direction repeat and the queued corner along with the visible walk. Rust previously retained those shell-only input timers across scene restoration, allowing the first direction after a battle or reload to be suppressed or inherited from the prior map.
- The 48-pixel battle HP bar now retains red through pixel 10 and yellow through pixel 24, matching TypeScript's inclusive 21% and 50% thresholds. Rust used exclusive comparisons, changing both the visible palette and downward threshold sound one pixel too early.
- Battle text now reveals at the selected FAST/MID/SLOW letter cadence. A first A press completes the active page, only a subsequent press dismisses it, and the prompt arrow remains hidden until printing finishes. Rust previously rendered every battle page and arrow immediately, making ordinary combat dialogue visibly instantaneous.
- Completed battle and field dialogue now blink the lower-right `▼` prompt from LCD/VBlank bit 4, giving the source 16-frames-off/16-frames-on cadence at tile `(18,17)`. Field dialogue participates in the retained LCD invalidation while active; Rust previously drew both arrows continuously after reveal completion.
- Acknowledging a fully printed battle or field prompt now queues `SFX_READ_TEXT_2` at `PromptButton`'s input boundary. The first press that merely completes typewriter reveal remains silent; Rust previously dismissed the blinking prompt without the cartridge's acknowledgement cue.
- Script `waitbutton` remains distinct from `promptbutton`: it waits for A/B without loading the blinking cursor or playing `SFX_READ_TEXT_2`. Rust's shared pending-wait projection previously displayed the same prompt arrow for both command types; acknowledgement audio now likewise consults the retained source command.
- Missed moves and command-owned failed effects now retain the acknowledged `used` page through Crystal's exact 40-frame `BattleCommand_MoveDelay` before exposing failure text. If the acting Pokémon has a Substitute, the retained boundary composes the exported parameter-1 drop animation, the 40-frame delay, and parameter-2 raise animation in source order. Rust previously skipped directly to the miss/failure page and left the doll permanently drawn through the command.
- Successful move presentation now composes Crystal's `BattleCommand_LowerSub → move animation → BattleCommand_RaiseSub` wrapper whenever the user has an active Substitute. The wrapper reuses the exported Substitute parameter-1/2 programs in one retained timeline so result/HP settlement still occurs only after the doll is restored. Multi-hit scripts lower once before the first hit and raise once after the last, retaining the exposed Pokémon during each between-hit HP tween. Charge releases skip the redundant lower phase exactly as `CheckUserIsCharging` requires; first-turn Fly/Dig lowers but deliberately omits RaiseSub while the user disappears, and their release branch animates then raises. Called-move chains apply the same wrapper at each actual animation command. Rust previously rendered the Substitute doll throughout every successful attack animation.
- Battle status presentation now consumes the exported `BattleAnim_Psn`, `Brn`, `Frz`, `Par`, `Confused`, and `Slp` scripts at their ASM command boundaries. Newly inflicted major statuses and confusion chain after the initiating move (including called moves) and before result text; an active confusion check animates after its textbox; poison/burn residual text retains the pre-damage HUD through its animation and settles HP only afterward. Sleep preserves even the cartridge's asymmetric turn order: the player animation precedes `FastAsleepText`, while the enemy text precedes its animation. Rust previously narrated all of these states with static text and could expose residual HP loss before the cartridge's status effect.
- Turn checks and quarter-HP residual effects now retain their dedicated exported boundaries. Attraction prints the in-love page, runs `BattleAnim_InLove`, and only then exposes the authoritative immobilization result; Nightmare and Ghost Curse both run `BattleAnim_InNightmare` against the afflicted side while retaining its pre-damage HUD, then settle the quarter-HP loss before their result text. Rust previously reduced all three source sequences to static text over already-mutated battle state.
- Leech Seed residual presentation now runs exported `BattleAnim_Sap` in the seeder's switched-turn context while retaining the seeded target's pre-damage scene. Its target HP tween completes first, the source's distinct restoration tween follows when healing is possible, and only then does the sap textbox begin revealing; a faint page queued between the typed damage/drain events inherits the healed source scene. Rust previously displayed static sap text over a scene in which both HP mutations had already committed together.
- Continuing sandstorm presentation now chains Crystal's complete per-battler sequence: the weather page acknowledges into exported `BattleAnim_InSandstorm` using the source's temporarily switched animation turn, then the affected battler's HP tween settles and its hit page becomes readable before the next eligible battler animates. An expiring weather counter retains only the ending page. Rust previously displayed the weather/hit narration over already-applied damage with no storm animation.
- Continuing partial traps now replay the authoritative Bind/Wrap/Fire Spin/Whirlpool move animation in the trapper's switched-turn context between the preceding battle page and the trapped target's 1/16-HP tween/hurt page. Crystal's hidden Fly/Dig target branch still takes damage but deliberately skips that replay, and the terminal trap turn retains release text without an attack animation. Rust previously reduced every residual trap turn to static text over committed HP.
- Spikes entry damage now retains the newly sent-out battler's full-HP HUD beneath `hurt by SPIKES!`, then commits the authoritative 1/8-HP scene and bar tween when that page closes, before any queued faint presentation. This follows `SpikesDamage`'s text-before-`SubtractHPFromTarget` order; Rust previously associated the text page with already-reduced HP.
- Confusion self-damage now chains exported `BattleAnim_HitConfusion` after the ordinary confused/chick boundary (or the disobedience page), then exposes the HP tween and `hurt itself!` text. Rust previously retained only the chick animation and jumped directly to post-hit state, omitting the cartridge's dedicated impact object and `SFX_POUND`.
- Berserk Gene activation now preserves its source-visible event chain: item activation text, sharply-raised Attack text, exported `BattleAnim_Confused`, then the became-confused page when the holder was not already confused. Existing-confusion activation correctly omits the latter animation/page. Rust's typed item/stat events previously left the special confusion animation disconnected because no ordinary `MoveUsed` trigger exists at this start-of-turn boundary.
- Consumable held recovery now plays `ItemRecoveryAnim` through the exported parameter-zero Recover program before HP-berry, status-berry, confusion-heal, or MysteryBerry result state/text. Each activation chains from the actual preceding battle page, so multiple end-of-turn holders remain serialized. Leftovers deliberately bypasses that animation and retains its direct HP-tween-then-text source path. Rust previously projected every held recovery as text/state only.
- Pre-text battle animations now inherit the actual preceding page as their queue trigger instead of being marked globally started. This preserves source order when weather, poison/burn, Leech Seed, Nightmare, Curse, trapping, and held recovery all coexist in one event batch: no later animation can overtake an earlier unread result page. Leech Seed uses distinct target-damage, source-restoration, and terminal boundaries; Nightmare/Curse use an animation-to-HP terminal boundary so their result text cannot reveal before damage settles.
- A, B, and Start now share the TypeScript battle-dialogue advance path: each first completes an unfinished page and only a later press acknowledges it. Rust previously ignored B over battle text and let Start fall through toward the field menu path.
- Ordinary field dialogue accepts A or B for typewriter completion and prompt acknowledgement, matching `JoyTextDelay`, `JoyWaitAorB`, and `PromptButton`; embedded YES/NO and selection surfaces retain their own B-cancel behavior first. Start remains consumed for the lifetime of the textbox and cannot open a menu beneath an NPC/story interaction. This deliberately follows ASM where TypeScript's generic field `DialogueWindow` is A-only.
- Held directional input now follows TypeScript's surface-specific cadence: battle cursors use an eight-frame initial delay, ordinary shared menus use twelve, and both repeat every four frames. Rust previously reacted only to `just_pressed`, forcing a separate physical key press for every move, party, Pack, PC, and other shared cursor row. A newly pressed direction takes ordered ownership while other held directions remain non-diagonal input.
- Party STATS browsing now follows `StatsScreen_JoypadAction`: Up/Down changes the displayed party member without wrapping at the first or last slot, including when STATS was opened from the battle party menu. The retained battle party cursor follows the displayed member so B/A exit returns to that same row; Rust previously ignored vertical input in battle STATS and wrapped ordinary party summaries across the ends.
- Tile-forced overworld movement now selects the ASM step function independently of player mode. Currents, directional `WALK_*` tiles, doors, staircases, and caves use `STEP_WALK`'s eight visible frames even on a bike/skate, while `ICE`/`ICE_2B` uses `STEP_ICE`'s four-frame slide even in normal mode. Retained origin collision lasts for the corresponding full 8/4-frame presentation; Rust previously inherited the actor mode's speed, making normal ice half-speed and bike-forced land/warp movement double-speed.
- Surf traversal now follows `CheckSurfPerms` for the complete `$30..$3f` current family: all sixteen entries are `WATER_TILE` from every approach, and only the following `CheckTile` pass forces direction from the low two bits. Rust previously blocked upward or sideways entry onto the named waterfall collisions and `CURRENT_DOWN`, skipping the cartridge's visible enter-then-force sequence. Exhaustive core coverage checks every permission/direction pair, while the integrated runtime test enters `COLL_WATERFALL` sideways and is forced Down on the next empty-input frame.
- Whirlpool collision now follows the same enter-then-`CheckTile` boundary instead of acting as an invented impassable wall. Both `$24` and `$2c` accept the Surf step; the next player-movement pass queues the exported `Script_ForcedMovement` global root, whose facing branch runs `step_dig 16`, moves one tile back through `turn_in` in the opposite direction, runs the second `step_dig 16`, and leaves the player facing away through `turn_head`. Export and Rust materialization tests certify all nine reachable labels, and an integrated runtime regression proves the right-facing enter/queue/return lifecycle without a host-side movement approximation.
- Failed RUN attempts now retain the RUN command cursor exactly like `wBattleMenuCursorPosition`. Trainer refusal and unsuccessful wild escape return to RUN rather than silently selecting FIGHT, preventing an escape retry from becoming an accidental attack.
- Entering FIGHT now clears accumulated escape attempts at the `BattleMenu_Fight` boundary, before move selection and therefore even if the move screen is canceled. Rust previously retained the growing RUN bonus across FIGHT visits, diverging from both ASM and TypeScript's normal committed-move path.
- Battle switch refusal pages now preserve ASM's explicit line structure for `<MON> / is already out.`, `<MON> / can't be recalled!`, `An EGG can't / battle!`, and `There's no will to / battle!`. The trapped path requires the authoritative active party entry instead of fabricating `POKEMON` when battle identity is corrupt.
- Player Guard Spec use now presents only `UseItemText`, matching `GuardSpecEffect`. Rust previously appended TypeScript's extra `<MON> became shrouded in MIST!` page after the ordinary item-use page, adding a non-ASM acknowledgement and delaying the enemy response.
- Battle healing/status/PP/X-item result pages now use `PrintPartyMenuActionText`/item-effect wording and explicit lines: revitalized, exact recovered HP delta, poison/paralysis/burn/freeze/sleep/confusion phrases, `PP was restored.`, and the stat-up page. Dire Hit no longer invents a second “getting pumped” page after `UseItemText`. Result presentation requires the selected/active party target instead of substituting `POKéMON`.
- Battle item narration now follows each ASM effect's entry point instead of universally prepending `UseItemText`. Potion/Revive/status/Full Restore/PP paths show their party-action result directly; X items, Dire Hit, Guard Spec, escape items, and pure confusion-heal berries retain the generic player-used page where their source routines actually call it. This removes an extra acknowledgement before most healing effects and enemy responses.
- Confusion recovery now distinguishes the two source routines: Full Heal-style party action text says `<MON> came / to its senses.`, while a pure confusion berry follows `BitterBerryEffect` with `<MON>'s / confused no more!` after its generic item-use page.
- Battle party-item and PP-move targeting now apply `UseItem_SelectMon`'s Egg gate before opening a move list or performing any core mutation, displaying the exact `That can't be used / on an EGG.` page while retaining the selected target for retry. Rust previously had no Egg rejection in these paths, allowing item payload logic to treat an Egg as an ordinary party Pokémon.
- Elixir and Max Elixir metadata (`pp_restore_scope = ALL`) now routes through the battle party-Pokémon target surface and confirms through the PP mutation with no move slot, matching `RestorePPEffect`'s branch before `MoveSelectionScreen`. Rust previously classified only single-move PP items; after opening no target at all, an ALL-scope item would otherwise reach the ordinary Pokémon-effect API, which cannot execute `RESTORE_PP`.
- Cursor-prefixed battle command strings now begin on the cursor tile rather than the ASM-derived label tile. This removes the one-tile right drift from the normal and Bug-Catching Contest command menus, move names, and battle YES/NO choices while retaining TypeScript's exact label origins.
- The player battle HUD now uses `PrintPlayerHUD`'s distinct nickname `(10,7)`, level/status `(14,8)`, and gender `(17,8)` origins; genderless battlers retain Crystal's one-tile level adjustment. Rust previously placed the nickname at x9 and the level at x16, colliding with the gender column.
- Selected battle cursors now alternate between solid and outline glyphs every 16 Game Boy frames, matching TypeScript's `MENU_CURSOR_FRAMES` cadence across main commands, moves, YES/NO prompts, and battle submenus instead of displaying a permanently solid cursor.
- Battle render caching now includes an eight-frame LCD animation phase while a battle exists. The shared visual checksum intentionally removes `frame_counter`, which previously allowed otherwise unchanged battle scenes to retain a frozen cursor and frozen party icon indefinitely; eight frames covers the fastest party-icon cycle and the cursor's 16-frame alternation without imposing per-frame overworld rebuilds.
- The retained overworld now has a presentation-time LCD clock independent of semantic save revisions. Johto/Modern Johto/Kanto/Park/Forest flower tile `$03` uses the two CGB source frames at the TypeScript 60-frame cadence, and supported overworld water tile `$14` uses all four `water.2bpp` frames with the source `((timer / 11) >> 1) & 3` cadence. Park fountain `$5f` follows `[0,1,2,3,2,3,4,0]`; Elite Four lava tiles `$5b/$38` retain their two-frame offset; restless Ilex Forest tree halves `$0c/$0f` animate only under `ENGINE_FOREST_IS_RESTLESS`; and all ten Tower pillar destinations use the five-frame rise/fall pattern in the ASM one-destination-per-frame command order. Cave/Dark Cave compose the four-frame water source with the 19-command horizontal scroll and rotate waterfall `$40` downward three rows at command 16; Ice Path applies the same command buffer to visible tiles `$b5/$b1`. Animation frames use each destination tile's time-of-day palette and invalidate the retained viewport only at actual phase offsets.
- Map rendering now derives the same effective environment palette as TypeScript/ASM instead of consuming the exported map-table token literally. `PALETTE_AUTO` routes and towns follow the live `morn`/`day`/`nite` clock; `INDOOR` and `GATE` maps force the dedicated `indoor` group despite their `PALETTE_DAY` declaration; and `PALETTE_DARK` selects `dark` until Flash switches it to `nite`. The resolved value owns the base viewport, connected-map art and cache identity, NPC/player sprites, grass, and overworld effects, eliminating outdoor-color interiors and palette seams at connection edges. Time labels normalize TypeScript's complete aliases (`morning`, `night`, `darkness`, and `indoors`) before lookup. Map setup now also executes `ResetFlashIfOutOfCave` semantics by clearing `STATUSFLAGS_FLASH` only on entry to a route or town, so illumination survives indoor/cave transitions but does not leak into every later dark cave.
- Options retains its separate TypeScript cadence of fifteen frames before four-frame repeats for both row movement and value changes; confirm/cancel clears that ownership. It previously bypassed the shared repeat work and still required discrete presses.
- Menu direction arbitration now accepts a newly pressed arrow while another arrow remains held, gives that press TypeScript's Up/Down/Left/Right priority, and transfers repeat ownership with the complete initial delay. Rust previously required exactly one physically held direction, so rolling from one direction into another canceled battle command, move, party, Pack, and ordinary menu movement until the first key was released.
- Tile-arrival consequences now wait for the retained movement to land. Warps/connections, coordinate scripts, trainer sight, wild-battle entry, poison blackout, Repel expiry, Egg hatching, and other visible step events no longer replace the overworld on the same host frame that core atomically commits the destination. The exact triggering core frame is retained and dispatched after the eight/four-frame step or the ledge sequence, so its script/battle payload cannot be overwritten by an intervening idle frame.
- A deferred wild encounter stages its battle scene and transition at frame zero while keeping both visually and audibly dormant. This prevents the already-committed battle snapshot, white battle canvas, HUD, transition palette, or battle music from leaking beneath the remaining walk frames; map music continues until the landing boundary starts the transition.
- Door/warp steps now retain the source-map snapshot and its triggering tile through the visible walk, despite core having already installed the destination session atomically. The destination map is released only at landing. Map connections translate their interpolation origin into destination-map coordinates and discard the old map's unrelated camera-origin numbers instead of flinging the player or viewport across the new map.
- Seamless map connections now retain the live held-direction arbitration and four-step player stride phase across arrival. Rust previously ran the broad door/load navigation reset for `MAPSETUP_CONNECTION`, clearing the edge-driven held queue and making a continuously held direction stop at route boundaries until the key was released and pressed again. Physical key state is reconciled on every unlocked frame, so a release that occurred during the retained landing boundary cannot leave a stale direction latched; true warps and travel still reset input completely.
- Trainer sight detection no longer applies Rust's invented terrain/object line-of-sight scan. Crystal's `_CheckTrainerBattle` and TypeScript require only a visible unbeaten trainer facing an aligned player within the declared sight range; neither checks intervening collision permissions or objects. Restoring that rule prevents ordinary and progression-relevant trainer encounters from being silently suppressed by the extra Rust condition.
- Wild encounter surface resolution no longer recognizes only `$18` tall grass. Rust now uses `CheckGrassCollision`'s encounter catalog (`$08,$14,$18,$28,$48..$4c`) and, like `CanEncounterWildMon`/TypeScript, permits ordinary non-ice land throughout `CAVE` and `DUNGEON` environments. This restores random battles on long/cut/legacy grass permissions and, critically, on ordinary cave floors; Sweet Scent uses the same environment-aware surface instead of incorrectly reporting that nothing is present in caves. Visible grass-rustle classification also includes TypeScript's complete `$48..$4c` grass family rather than silently omitting those tiles.
- Ledge camera scrolling now follows TypeScript's same two-source-pixels-per-frame jump progress and applies the identical offset to the map, player, and shadow instead of committing the landing viewport before the jumping sprite. The retained player uses its movement pose through the jump and performs the sixteenth two-pixel landing update when the jump state clears.
- Ledge actors now also follow `UpdateJumpPosition`'s exact sixteen-frame vertical offsets (`-4,-6,-8,-10,-11,-12,-12,-12,-11,-10,-9,-8,-6,-4,0,0` source pixels) above the independently grounded shadow. Full redraws and retained updates share the same sixteen-frame position, camera, actor, NPC, and shadow coordinate space. The actor retains the ordinarily primed two-frame stride (including vertical foot mirroring) throughout all sixteen frames instead of forcing every jump onto one walking image. Rust previously interpolated the player and camera across the ledge but left the actor at ground height and could rebuild it at the already-committed destination—visually reducing the jump to a fast two-tile walk over a detached shadow.
- Scripted `slow_jump_step`, `jump_step`, and `fast_jump_step` now create the same grounded tracking shadow as ASM `JumpStep` for player, NPC, and follower actors. Each retained shadow records its actor identity and follows that actor's independent live stride timer, so a fast follower shadow disappears independently even if a slower leader remains in flight (and conversely); the renderer no longer limits `SpawnShadow` behavior to collision-driven player ledge jumps.
- Overworld emotes now implement `StepFunction_TrackingObject` positioning instead of anchoring to the target's already-committed map tile. Player, NPC, and follower targets use their independent retained origin, source movement duration, current subframe, and shared camera displacement, so the emote remains attached throughout a stride and a scrolling player step. The emote copies only the target's live base `OBJECT_SPRITE_X/Y` and then applies its own `-2 * TILE_WIDTH` offset, deliberately excluding the target's jump-height/Y-offset field just like ASM; an unloaded target produces no emote because the source tracking object deletes itself when the tracked sprite slot is empty.
- Grass rustle now composes the exact two-piece `FacingGrass1/2` OAM instead of drawing one 8×8 tile and flipping the entire single-tile image. The shared 18×9 source canvas contains the normal and X-flipped copies at `(0,8)/(8,8)` for frame 1 and `(-1,9)/(9,9)` for frame 2, preserving the one-pixel outward/downward shake. Its tracking position uses the player's grounded base sprite coordinates, so the landing rustle stays on the grass during the raised second half of a ledge jump rather than inheriting `OBJECT_SPRITE_Y_OFFSET` from `UpdateJumpPosition`.
- Jump shadows now preserve `FacingShadow`'s complete 16×8 source OAM footprint: the normal 8×8 `$fc` tile at x=0 and its X-flipped copy at x=8. Rust previously overlapped the two pieces by four source pixels into a 12×8 image, making every ledge and scripted-jump shadow one quarter too narrow. The renderer now requires the authoritative one-tile 8×8 source asset and fails explicitly for any other shape. The focused shadow regressions pass; the complete Bevy validation passes 776 library tests with two intentional performance probes ignored plus all seven binary tests, and `cargo check --workspace` passes with only the pre-existing unused browser-save helper warning.
- Shadow tracking now applies `MovementFunction_Shadow`'s direction-dependent `OBJECT_SPRITE_Y_OFFSET`: 14 source pixels for Up/Down and 12 for Left/Right. Full redraw, the optimized walk updater, and retained ledge updates derive the live jump direction and share the same source-pixel positioning helper. Rust previously bottom-aligned every shadow at an effective offset of 8, placing vertical shadows six source pixels too high and horizontal shadows four too high; the TypeScript overlap/bottom-alignment helper has the same divergence and is not treated as authoritative over ASM. The complete Bevy suite passes 777 library tests with two intentional performance probes ignored plus all seven binary tests.
- Shadow lifetime now matches the source object-update order. `JumpStep` advances the actor before the new temporary object initializes; `MovementFunction_Shadow` therefore reads the already-decremented half-step duration, and `StepFunction_TrackingObject` reaches zero and deletes the shadow before the actor's final landing frame is drawn. Ledge frame 15 and scripted-jump `remaining == 1` now render the grounded actor without a shadow. Actor-owned visibility also removes the correct retained leader/follower shadow on optimized updates instead of leaving an unowned entity until a later full redraw. The complete Bevy suite passes 778 library tests with two intentional performance probes ignored plus all seven binary tests.
- Overworld screen shake now follows `MovementFunction_ScreenShake`/`StepFunction_ScreenShake` instead of cycling the camera right, left, up, and down. The packed high bits still select source intensity `1/2/4/8`, but every active displacement is vertical: each update removes the previous vector, decrements the low-six-bit counter, applies `+intensity` for an even remaining counter or `-intensity` for an odd one, and restores zero before deleting on the final update. Bevy projects the Game Boy's downward SCY axis onto its upward world Y axis with the required sign inversion. The following generated `step_sleep` phase remains stationary. The complete Bevy suite passes 779 library tests with two intentional performance probes ignored plus all seven binary tests.
- Ledge authority now reports the fixed `STEP_LEDGE` speed in bike and skate modes instead of inheriting `STEP_BIKE`'s 2× multiplier. The renderer already held every ledge for sixteen frames, but the mismatched core speed released the player's origin collision after eight and could let an NPC overlap the second half of a bike jump. `bike_and_skate_ledge_jumps_keep_fixed_ledge_speed` explicitly covers both accelerated modes, and the complete 1,968-test core suite passes.
### Battle FIGHT move-info PP label

- Restored the missing `PP` label at native tile `(1, 11)` for valid moves while retaining Crystal `MoveInfoBox.PrintPP`'s explicit current/max field at `(5, 11)` (`/` at x7 and max PP at x8). The TypeScript grouped helper currently places that field one tile left of the ASM routine, so Rust follows the cartridge coordinate here.

### No-text-scroll presentation

- Field and battle typewriters now reveal the current page immediately when the retained `no_text_scroll` option is active, matching TypeScript's zero-delay text path. Rust previously persisted the option but still applied the ordinary FAST/MID/SLOW cadence.

### FIGHT selection confirmation

- A-confirming a move or the FIGHT menu's `CANCEL` row now emits `SFX_READ_TEXT_2` at `ParsePlayerAction`'s post-`MoveSelectionScreen` `PlayClickSFX` boundary. Cursor movement remains silent, and this does not restore the removed HP-palette threshold clicks.
- A-confirming FIGHT, POKéMON, PACK, or RUN on the main 2×2 battle menu now emits that menu's own generic confirmation click. The click is gated to the main surface so choosing a move or party entry cannot play it twice.

### Battle party action order

- Restored `BattleMonMenu`'s `SWITCH / STATS / CANCEL` order in both the visible submenu and its action dispatch. A concurrent refactor had reversed the first two entries, making the default confirmation open STATS even though the screen's source contract puts SWITCH first.
- Ordinary switch refusal now follows `TryPlayerSwitch` order: already-active, active battler trapped, then selected-member fitness. Eggs receive the distinct source `An EGG can't battle!` page, while a fainted selection retains `There's no will to battle!`; Rust previously merged both and could report reserve fitness before the active battler's recall failure. Baton Pass, trainer Shift selection, and mandatory faint replacement retain their separate trap rules.
- The faint-replacement YES/NO textbox now uses `BattleText_UseNextMon`'s original `Use next #MON?` casing and Pokémon control token. Rust previously exposed an all-caps shell phrase with the plain word `POKEMON`.

### Player switch send-out text

- Player replacements now select Crystal's HP-sensitive `Go!`, `Do it!`, `Go for it,`, or `Your foe's weak! Get'm,` line from the opposing battler's remaining-HP percentage. The same centralized line owns display, send-out animation detection, and deferred cry release; Rust previously fabricated `Go!` for every replacement regardless of battle state.
- All four source lines now share the same pre-send render boundary: the incoming battler and player HUD remain hidden beneath the text, then appear only through the send-out animation. The renderer previously recognized only the literal `Go!` prefix even after the other source lines were restored.
- Ordinary player switches now retain the enemy HP captured at the prior player send-out and use the damage percentage to select Crystal's `that's enough`, plain `come back`, `OK`, or `good` withdrawal line. That page retains the outgoing battler, then owns the source 50-frame pause and exported `BattleAnim_ReturnMon` collapse before the incoming send-out page; forced switches and Baton Pass keep their separate presentation.
- Trainer Shift replacements now preserve Crystal's complete enemy-first order: the opposing trainer's replacement message, hidden incoming battler, send-out animation, and cry complete while the old player battler remains visible; the player's HP-sensitive withdrawal page and 50-frame `ReturnMon` animation follow; only then does the selected player replacement page reveal its staged scene and send-out animation. Deferred send-out cries are retained in message order instead of one pending slot overwriting the other.
- The Shift offer now preserves `BattleText_EnemyIsAboutToUseWillPlayerChangeMon`'s actual trainer name and authored line structure: `<ENEMY>` / `is about to use` / the incoming nickname, followed on the final YES/NO page by `Will <PLAYER>` / `change #MON?`. Rust previously substituted the generic word `Enemy` and dynamically wrapped both sentences, so identity and LCD line breaks diverged from the source.
- Trainer intros and every later enemy send-out now retain their authored textbox lines (`<ENEMY>` / `wants to battle!` and `<ENEMY>` / `sent out` / nickname); wild appearances likewise keep `Wild <nickname>` / `appeared!`. Send-out animation recognition consumes the structured form directly, so exact source layout no longer depends on sentence rewrapping or a space-delimited trigger phrase.
- Player and enemy faint pages now force `BattleText_MonFainted`/`BattleText_EnemyMonFainted`'s nickname line followed by `fainted!`. The retained post-faint scene trigger uses that same structured page, preventing exact LCD layout from desynchronizing faint animation and settlement.
- Pay Day settlement now renders `BattleText_PlayerPickedUpPayDayMoney` with the actual player name on the first line and `¥<amount>!` on the second. Rust previously discarded the provided name and inherited TypeScript's generic single-line `You picked up …` wording; trainer prize money remains the distinct `got ¥… / for winning!` text.

### KO settlement surface

- Defeated-battler settlement no longer exposes shell operations such as `CLAIM $…`, `CLAIM REWARD`, `NEXT POKEMON`, or `FINISH BATTLE` as a selectable LCD menu. Faint animation, EXP/level/evolution presentation, Shift choice, trainer replacement, and battle exit retain their existing automatic/source-owned boundaries; while the enemy HP is zero, no fabricated command window is drawn between them.

### Capture refusal text

- Full capture storage follows the referenced ASM path `PokeBallEffect → Ball_BoxIsFullMessage → _BallBoxFullText`, including its three-line `The #MON BOX / is full. That / can't be used now.` page. TypeScript currently emits the shorter `The BOX is full!`, but that matching disassembly string is explicitly unreferenced; Rust therefore keeps the cartridge path and canonical Pokémon bitmap token.
