### PP-item and party-picker presentation

- Single-move PP items now select the source prompt from item metadata: PP Up displays `Raise the PP of / which move?`, while Ether, Max Ether, and MysteryBerry display `Restore the PP of / which move?`. PP Up completion names the selected move and prints `<MOVE>'s PP / increased.`; ordinary restoration retains `PP was restored.` Rust previously used one shortened restore prompt and one restoration result for both source routines.
- The battle party picker now uses `Choose a #MON.` for the ordinary `BattleMenu_PKMN` surface, `Which <PK><MN>?` for mandatory faint replacement through `PickPartyMonInBattle`, and `Use on which <PK><MN>?` for item targeting, matching each `wPartyMenuActionText` boundary. Rust previously collapsed the ordinary and mandatory switch prompts into one phrase.
- PACK action submenus now use the cartridge's framed seven-tile window with height `2 × item count + 1` in both overworld and battle rendering. Rust previously painted an unframed white rectangle one tile taller than the matching `MenuHeader_*` coordinates.
- Trainer RUN refusal now retains `BattleText_TheresNoEscapeFromTrainerBattle`'s authored three lines: `No! There's no / running from a / trainer battle!`. Rust previously passed TypeScript's single sentence through a generic wrapper, making source line structure incidental.
- Zero-shake capture failure now renders `_BallBrokeFreeText`'s generic `Oh no! The #MON / broke free!` instead of substituting the opposing nickname. Blocked-ball and unusable-item pages likewise retain their authored `The trainer / blocked the BALL!` and `It won't have any / effect.` lines rather than depending on generic wrapping.
- Successful standard captures now render `AskGiveNicknameText` in the retained battle textbox with the caught Pokémon name on its authored second line and the standard `(14,7)..(19,11)` YES/NO box. Rust previously reused the new-player preset-name screen headed `NAME`; although its hidden state contained YES/NO, the actual nickname question was absent from the LCD.
- Capture nickname state now seeds that prompt and the naming screen from `wCurPartySpecies`'s canonical species name, matching `GetPokemonName`, rather than copying the active battle nickname. This matters for any authored/static encounter whose displayed battle nickname differs from its caught species identity.
- Battle reward pages now preserve `Text_Gained + ExpPointsText`, `GrewToLevelText`, and `LearnedMoveText` layout: `<MON> gained / <amount> EXP. Points!`, `<MON> grew to / level <n>!`, and `<MON> learned / <MOVE>!`. EXP tweens, level-stat panels, and fanfare triggers use the same structured strings, preventing exact text restoration from disconnecting their retained presentation boundaries.
- Pending move learning now opens with `AskForgetMoveText`'s `<MON> is / trying to learn / <MOVE>.` lines, and the replacement screen uses `Which move should / be forgotten?` instead of an overlong single line. Both ordinary battle rewards and evolution-deferred learning share the structured intro.
- Move-learning `ForgetMove` and `StopLearningMoveText` decisions now use the shared `YesNoBox` border `(14,7)..(19,11)`. Rust previously placed a six-tile-tall approximation at y8, shifting both cursor rows down and extending the box into the battle textbox.
- Move-learning resolution text now preserves `_MoveCantForgetHMText`, `Text_MoveForgetCount`, `_MoveForgotText`, `_LearnedMoveText`, and `_DidNotLearnMoveText` wording and line structure, including Crystal's ellipsis glyph and the period after the forgotten move. Rust previously compressed these into prose, changed that period to an exclamation mark, and used three ASCII dots; the same punctuation fix now covers the field Move Tutor path.
- Ordinary battle command text now retains `DisplayUsedMoveText`, `HasNoPPLeftText`, `AttackMissedText`, `EvadedText`, and `DoesntAffectText` line structure. In particular every move page is `<USER> / used <MOVE>!`; Rust previously constructed a single sentence and relied on width-dependent wrapping, which also made animation trigger identity vary from the cartridge-visible page.
- Major-status application now distinguishes `WasPoisonedText` from `BadlyPoisonedText` and restores the authored sleep, burn, freeze, paralysis, and disobedience-nap lines. Toxic no longer reports ordinary poison, and paralysis now retains `<TARGET>'s / paralyzed! Maybe / it can't attack!` instead of TypeScript's shortened “was paralyzed” sentence. Failed/immune status pages likewise use explicit `It didn't/doesn't affect / <TARGET>!` lines.
- Stat-stage messages now place `<USER/TARGET>'s` on the first line and the stat/result on the second, matching `Text_BattleEffectActivate` and `Text_BattleFoeEffectActivate`. Reflect and Light Screen application now report `DEFENSE rose!` and `SPCL.DEF rose!` respectively; Rust previously invented “raised REFLECT!” and “raised LIGHT SCREEN!” pages that do not match their command text.
- Safeguard, Reflect/Light Screen expiry, Mist, Leech Seed, Curse, Nightmare, Spikes, Future Sight, and Substitute pages now retain their ASM possessives, punctuation, and explicit line structure. This fixes content differences such as `protected by MIST.` (period), `<USER>'s / hurt by the CURSE!`, and `<TARGET> / was hit by FUTURE / SIGHT!`, not merely cosmetic wrapping.
- Trap release, confusion, Attract, Disable, and Encore pages now use their exact command text. Confusion recovery is `<USER>'s / confused no more!` and self-damage is generic `It hurt itself in / its confusion!`; Rust previously invented “snapped out of confusion” and nickname-owned self-hit prose. Attempting a disabled selection now correctly displays `The move is / DISABLED!`.
- Trap application/damage, Protect, Spikes, Future Sight, Perish Song, Focus Energy, Foresight, Nightmare, Psych Up, Transform, Mimic, Sketch, Conversion, stat reset, Lock-On, Endure, and Bide pages now retain their source-defined two/three-line forms. This also restores canonical control tokens such as `Both #MON will / faint in 3 turns!` and paragraph structure for copied stat changes.
- Recovery pages now distinguish `BattleText_TargetRecoveredWithItem` (Leftovers/HP berries: `<TARGET> / recovered with / <ITEM>.`), `RecoveredUsingText` (status berries), MysteryBerry PP recovery, and confusion-berry recovery. Rest and ordinary healing likewise retain their authored lines; Rust previously mixed incompatible “regained,” “recovered with,” and “recovered using a” routines.
- The general `BattleItemEffect` formatter no longer reintroduces Guard Spec's fabricated Mist page or Dire Hit's fabricated “getting pumped” page after their direct item-use paths. X-item stat results retain their source-owned page with the same possessive/stat line structure as move-driven stage changes.
- Sleep/recharge turns, Jump Kick crash recoil, two-turn move charge/airborne announcements, and weather start/continuation/end boundaries now use their authored battle-text lines. This restores such visible details as Razor Wind's `made a whirlwind!`, uppercase `SANDSTORM`, and Crystal's explicit two/three-line pacing instead of generic prose wrapping.
- Held-item theft/activation, blocked recalls, held-item escape, Teleport, Roar, Whirlwind, and ordinary flee pages now preserve their distinct source text and line boundaries. Whirlwind once again prints the forcing move name after `was blown away!`, while an enemy's ordinary departure uses `Enemy <MON> / fled!` instead of the player's safe-escape wording.
- Destiny Bond, Spite, forced-switch arrival, full paralysis, flinch, frozen-turn, and wake-up pages now retain their exact ASM possessives and line structure. Full paralysis is once again `<USER>'s / fully paralyzed!`, and all associated visible-boundary strings stay identical to the page placed in the battle queue.
- Sacred Ash now displays `_UseSacredAshText` with the actual player name and `#MON / were all healed!` wording after the whole-party mutation. Rust previously invented the generic `Your POKéMON were revived!`, losing both trainer identity and the cartridge's broader healed result.
- Stat-stage ceiling/floor pages now place the battler possessive, stat, and `rise/drop anymore!` clauses on their three authored lines. Curse application preserves its paragraph break between self-damage and targeting, and disobedience idle outcomes restore Crystal's line breaks and `loafing around.` punctuation.
- Trainer item use now identifies the trainer as the actor and the active enemy Pokémon as the target (`<ENEMY> / used <ITEM> / on <MON>!`), matching `_EnemyUsedOnText`; Rust previously claimed the enemy Pokémon used the item itself and omitted the target. Present healing and both infatuation pages likewise now use `RegainedHealthText`, `InLoveWithText`, and `InfatuationText` instead of invented summaries.
- Remaining disobedience event branches, defrosting, and Heal Bell now retain their source-owned line boundaries. Heal Bell preserves its deliberately blank second line rather than collapsing the sound cue into a normal one-line page.

### Forced overworld movement fast path

- Empty-input optimization now yields to every source-owned forced movement state before taking the idle shortcut. On maps without autonomous NPCs, Rust previously returned early before ice slides, water currents, directional walk tiles, door/cave continuation, or downhill bike coasting could run; those surfaces could therefore stop or strand the player solely because no wandering object happened to be present. The fast path remains available only when neither tile nor downhill movement is pending.

### Battle paragraph paging

- `para`-separated Curse, Psych Up, and move-forgetting text now enters the retained queue as separate visible pages. Rust's battle box intentionally renders at most four lines, but these routines had been stored as five-to-seven-line strings, silently truncating the Curse/Psych Up target and most of `Poof! … forgot … And…`. Every source paragraph is now acknowledged and displayed before the next page.

### Move Tutor confirmation playability

- Every field Move Tutor delete/stop confirmation now owns the `pc:confirmation` cursor surface used by rendering, directional input, A acceptance, and B cancellation. Several branches created the unrelated `yes-no` surface, which made the renderer default to NO and caused A to reject the cursor instead of resolving the prompt. Field and PC-style notice renderers now use all six interior rows of their eight-tile windows, and the full-moves prompt fits those rows with the selected Pokémon and move, four-move limit, and deletion question visible.
- Day-Care withdrawal, Name Rater, and Move Deleter decision text now fits the same six-row retained confirmation window with the fee/purpose and actual question visible beside YES/NO. Their former paragraph strings exceeded the renderer's hard limit, so the decision cursor appeared while `Will you pay?`, `rate names?`, or `forget?` was silently below the LCD surface.
- Field/PC notice wrapping now uses the window's actual sixteen-tile interior rather than its eighteen-tile border width. Long lines can no longer draw through the right frame or unexpectedly consume a hidden seventh row; confirmation copy is explicitly laid out within the same six-by-sixteen content surface.

### YES/NO default selection

- ASM `home/menu.asm::YesNoMenuHeader` uses default option `1`, the first entry (`YES`), and TypeScript's zero-based `YesNoPrompt` equivalently initializes `selection = 0`.
- Rust PC, Day-Care, trade, Buena, Move Deleter, mailbox, toss, and release confirmations had instead initialized zero-based option `1` (`NO`). Their initial cursor now selects `YES`; pressing B still resolves to `NO`, matching the original interaction.

### Happiness standard-script target

- ASM `GetFirstPokemonHappiness` and TypeScript skip leading Eggs before selecting the Pokémon whose happiness is evaluated. Rust's handwritten `HappinessCheckScript` dispatcher instead used the first occupied slot, so an Egg lead could show the wrong happiness response.
- The dispatcher now requires and selects the first non-Egg party member, retains its nickname/species registers and exact happiness script value, then chooses the source `<50`, `<150`, or high-happiness text branch.
- Egg identity is derived only from the typed Egg state/species. A non-Egg Pokémon legitimately nicknamed `EGG` remains eligible, matching ASM and TypeScript.

### Egg identity versus nickname

- Rust repeatedly treated the display nickname `EGG` as authoritative Egg identity. A normal Pokémon renamed `EGG` could consequently be omitted from healing and blackout viability, refused release/items/TMs/held items/field HP transfer, lose ordinary party actions, and be treated as unable to battle.
- Production overworld, party, PC, item, healing, blackout, and special-routine paths now use only the typed Egg flag/species boundary. Nicknames no longer alter gameplay classification.

### Dynamic warp backup identity

- ASM reads `wBackupWarpNumber` directly for a `-1` dynamic destination; elevator selection, `warpmod`, or the prior-warp backup path must have populated it. Rust instead converted a missing/zero backup index to warp 1, silently routing malformed elevator or link-room state through an unrelated doorway.
- Dynamic return warps now require a saved nonzero index. Ordinary warp bookkeeping preserves the backup-warp register instead of manufacturing index 1 while updating the backup map, matching the source register separation.

### New-game money initialization

- ASM `InitializeGame` writes `START_MONEY` to the player's wallet and `MOM_MONEY` to `wMomItemTriggerBalance` before play begins. Rust reset both fields to zero and never restored the compiled constants, so the first trainer-battle reload could treat the zero Mom threshold as an exact random-purchase trigger and create an impossible pending purchase against zero savings.
- Both production spawn boot and the location-test boot now initialize these fields from required pack-owned currency constants. Verification requires `START_MONEY` and requires `MOM_MONEY` to match the ASM-derived Mom purchase increment, preventing a malformed pack from reaching gameplay with contradictory thresholds.

### Egg hatch species presentation

- TypeScript and Rust core both guarantee a concrete `hatched_species` whenever `egg_hatched` is true. The Bevy shell nevertheless substituted generic `POKEMON` for a missing payload, which could request a nonexistent cry and display invented hatch text.
- Visible hatch presentation now requires the exact species emitted by the authoritative step event.

### Required visible Pokémon identity

- Move-learning/evolution progression previously substituted `POKEMON` if its authoritative party slot disappeared, and successful Day-Care withdrawal substituted `POKéMON` if the result omitted its species.
- Both successful boundaries now require the concrete party/species identity before constructing visible text or scheduling a cry; malformed state cannot produce plausible but false narration.

### Roaming encounter level authority

- A successful roaming wild-battle start carries the encounter's resolved species/level. Rust fell back to the saved roamer level if that resolved record was absent, allowing combat stats and visible level to be constructed from a different boundary.
- Roaming battle materialization now requires the resolved encounter and uses its exact level; incomplete encounter state fails before mutation.

### Blackout paragraph visibility

- `_WhitedOutText` separates the exhausted-party sentence and `<PLAYER> whited out!` with ASM `para`. Rust retained both paragraphs in one five-line battle string even though its battle textbox renders at most four lines, hiding `out!` at the recovery boundary.
- Blackout now queues the two source paragraphs as separately acknowledged pages before the white fade and recovery commit.
# Wild encounter entry cooldown and global suppression

- Persisted Crystal's `wWildEncounterCooldown` in Rust game state instead of rolling immediately after map entry.
- Every map entry now arms the five-step cooldown. Like `CheckWildEncounterCooldown`, Rust decrements before terrain/RNG and permits the fifth completed step when the counter reaches zero.
- Battle deactivation also arms five steps because Crystal returns every battle through `ReloadMap -> EnterMap` before field control or a suspended script resumes; Rust's retained visual reload does not reconstruct the core map session.
- Map entry callbacks now follow the callback dispatchers reached by each exact `MapSetupScript_*`, in ASM command order instead of map declaration order. Warp/door/fall/train/fly/teleport/bad-warp setups run NEWMAP, CMDQUEUE, TILES, SPRITES, OBJECTS; connections omit SPRITES; continue omits NEWMAP and OBJECTS; link return omits OBJECTS; submenu runs only TILES; and reload runs TILES then SPRITES while preserving the live object buffer and current scene. The source pack currently authors no SPRITES callbacks, but the dispatcher remains part of the setup contract. Callback bodies execute once in the authoritative setup mutation; Bevy no longer queues and interprets the same bodies a second time merely to reach the following scene. Current-map reload, link-return, and bad-warp setups use the journaled schema-v46 callback mutation, while transitions retain their atomic setup execution. Unsupported callback opcodes now abort with their exact callback, target script, command index, and map instead of being silently skipped; a compiled-corpus test executes the complete WARP callback plan on all 89 callback-bearing maps. Callback-local `scall` frames unwind only to the callback's entry depth, and setup restores the suspended interpreter's pre-existing call stack, next-script target, and end marker instead of consuming them at `endcallback`. The legacy macro spelling `reloadmappart`, which expands to the separate `refreshmap` opcode in source, is no longer accepted as an invented third reload command.
- Same-map reload now preserves the compiled script's next-command cursor across its tiles callback and resumes it afterward. Rust previously cleared navigation without a return cursor, which could drop the commands after `reloadmapafterbattle`—including Gym badge awards, event flags, and required post-battle dialogue.
- Compiled control transfers now preserve the asset layer's resolved target `ScriptLocation`, including its owning map, instead of rebuilding the next cursor from the caller's stale origin. This matters when a source script continues after `warpfacing`: Battle Tower's `Script_BeatenAllTrainers2` runs with `BattleTowerBattleRoom` as its caller origin after `BattleTower1F` is live, then jumps to the 1F-owned `Script_GivePlayerHisPrize`. The authoritative state already selected 1F, but the runner replaced that owner with the battle room and could resolve subsequent map-local commands against the wrong module. A source-pack regression now pins both the returned cursor and saved next-script location to `BattleTower1F`.
- Battle/map reload now runs `MAPCALLBACK_TILES` beneath the white screen, presents `FadeInFromWhite` for eight LCD frames, and defers only the suspended script continuation until the fade completes. This preserves `LoadBlockData -> tiles callback -> FadeInFromWhite -> script resume` source order; Rust previously snapped directly to the lit map or ran reload work on the wrong side of the fade.
- Plain random encounters now enter that same reload presentation after victory, capture, or escape. They do not own a compiled map-script cursor in Rust, but ASM always returns through `WildBattleScript -> reloadmapafterbattle`; the desktop path previously bypassed it entirely.
- That reload is armed only after retained victory, reward, Pokédex, nickname, and PC-storage pages have drained. Arming the input-locking fade while a terminal battle page remained would prevent A from acknowledging the page and strand the battle exit; the final acknowledgement now transfers ownership to the reload fade.
- The reload fade completion path now performs its tiles-callback/return restoration and suspended-script continuation as two explicit boundaries. This keeps callback ownership legible during the concurrent shell refactor and avoids coupling two mutable runtime transitions inside one combinator expression.
- Restored `STATUSFLAGS_NO_WILD_ENCOUNTERS_F` gating after the cooldown check and before any encounter RNG consumption.
- Added the missing zero-argument `wildoff` and `wildon` runtime opcodes so compiled scripts can set and clear that exact WRAM status bit instead of silently lacking the source control surface.
- Corrected wild-level variance to surfing encounters only. Rust and TypeScript had both applied the source +0–4 roll to grass and kept water levels fixed, but `ChooseWildEncounter` calls the variance roll only when `CheckOnWater` succeeds; this also restores the levels used by Repel and encounter-table reachability checks.
- Grass slot selection no longer consumes a fabricated level RNG byte. Random encounters and Sweet Scent now draw the post-slot level byte only on water, preserving Crystal's gameplay RNG order into battle and later overworld events.
- Enemy item-use narration now requires the active trainer identity instead of displaying the fabricated name `Enemy` if a structurally impossible wild/item event reaches presentation.
- Battle Pack-target and switch-party renderers no longer print host diagnostics such as `INVALID PARTY ...` into the cartridge textbox. Valid battle state must supply the authoritative party/cursor; malformed state fails at that boundary instead of fabricating game-screen copy.
- Dark cave palettes no longer suppress the complete three-sweep battle-transition flash. TypeScript and `DoBattleTransition` run the same transition program regardless of map illumination; ASM's flashing-reduction hooks are platform hooks, not a `PALETTE_DARK` branch.
- Encounter-rate, rejected-percent, and surfing-level samples now use one raw `Random` subtraction byte rather than Rust's range sampler; the latter can consume multiple cartridge RNG calls for a nominal 256 range. Sweet Scent uses the same rejected-byte slot loop as `ChooseWildEncounter`.
- Normal land encounters now consume `CheckEncounterRoamMon`'s random byte even when roaming slots are inactive or absent. Rust previously skipped that source call unless three materialized candidates existed, shifting ordinary slot selection and all later gameplay RNG before the beasts were released.
- Bug Contest steps now use their separate 20%/40% rate selected by long-grass collision, sample `hRandomAdd` like ASM, choose a contest slot by rejecting subtraction bytes at 200 then halving, and derive the level from the next `hRandomAdd` remainder. Rust previously used the map encounter rate and generic range sampling.

### Overworld movement cadence static parity

- Rust retains the same normal eight-frame tile cadence and four-frame facing-turn hold as TypeScript main, with bike/skate travel halving the visible step duration to four frames. Held directions are buffered only across the current visible landing and are cleared at script, trainer, encounter, and warp boundaries.
- Wall and object bumps remain one-frame retryable, matching ASM `movement_step_bump`'s duration byte of `1`; they are not incorrectly stretched to a full walking step. Ledge jumps retain the full sixteen-frame input-owned boundary, and map connections continue sampling the live D-pad so corners pressed at a seamless map edge are not dropped.
- The standard and Bug Contest battle command windows were statically checked against `engine/battle/menu.asm`: normal coordinates remain `8,12–19,17` with two rows, two columns, and spacing six; Contest remains `2,12–19,17` with spacing twelve and the live Park Ball count.

### Contextual field-obstacle feedback

- Pressing A toward Cut, Whirlpool, or Waterfall collision now always consumes the interaction and shows the source response. Eligible parties receive the original YES/NO prompt; missing moves, badges, Surf state, or upward Waterfall facing receive `CanCutText`, `MayPassWhirlpoolText`, or `HugeWaterfallText` respectively.
- Rust previously combined obstacle detection with eligibility and silently returned to overworld input on failure. That made valid source obstacles feel noninteractive and omitted the exact guidance Crystal uses to teach their required progression gates. Surf remains intentionally silent when its own eligibility checks fail, matching `TrySurfOW::.quit`.

### Bicycle textbox sprite boundary

- Bicycle mode and music may change before the “got on/off” message, but the visible player sprite remains the pre-toggle sprite until that textbox is acknowledged. This matches `Script_GetOnBike`/`Script_GetOffBike`, where `loadvar VAR_MOVEMENT` precedes the text and `UpdatePlayerSprite` follows `closetext`.
- Rust previously rendered directly from the already-mutated snapshot, making the bike or walking sprite appear underneath the message one source boundary early.
- Dig and Escape Rope field travel now preserves the departure scene, plays the ASM-sized 32-frame `step_dig` spin before hiding and changing maps, then plays `SFX_WARP_FROM` and the destination `show_object`/32-frame `return_dig` sequence before returning control. Previously both paths jumped directly from their use text to the destination map.
- Teleport now keeps its 60-frame source textbox pause, then runs the exact exported movement timing: 16 spin + 16 rise before the map load and 17 wait + 16 descent + 16 final spin after arrival, with the warp sounds on their ASM boundaries. Previously the 60-frame pause ended by cutting directly to the destination.
- B-canceling FIGHT or the battle party-selection submenu now plays `SFX_READ_TEXT_2` at the return boundary. This matches `MoveSelectionScreen -> ParsePlayerAction::PlayClickSFX` and the ordinary menu-button path while leaving B on the `STATICMENU_DISABLE_B` main command grid silent; Rust previously closed these active submenus with no audible acknowledgement.
- The battle party action popup now owns the same click for A selection and B cancellation. Its SWITCH/STATS/CANCEL surface previously accepted every button silently, making a core combat navigation surface feel disconnected even though the cursor result changed.
- Shell-owned field and PC text now explicitly capture A and B even when the core snapshot has no `ui.window_open` bit. Their dismissal routines already owned field-move animations, travel arrival, queued notices, PC results, and prompt continuation, but the input predicates previously recognized only core-owned text state; direct Bicycle, Dig/Teleport, item, or PC notices could therefore display while their acknowledgement was misrouted away from the visible textbox.
- Retained battle messages, active EXP presentation, and level-stat pages likewise capture A/B independently of `snapshot.battle`. Victory, capture, reward, and storage narration deliberately survives the core battle commit; routing solely from the active-battle flag could leave those terminal pages visible with no button reaching their existing reveal/dismiss handlers.
- Those retained text/reward surfaces now also capture D-pad, Start, and Select after the core battle or UI window has ended. D-pad and Select remain inert while visible text owns the LCD; Start retains the established battle-dialogue advance behavior. Previously terminal battle pages and shell-only field/PC notices could coexist with an overworld input frame underneath them because only the active core battle/window flags blocked the remaining controls.
- Overworld object rendering no longer substitutes `Direction::Down` when both live facing state and the compiled movement-data facing are absent. The pack verifier already requires a source-valid initial direction; malformed runtime state now reaches the existing render-error boundary instead of putting a plausibly drawn but incorrectly rotated NPC on screen.
- Retained autonomous-object walks now require both their remaining-frame and total-frame timers. The scheduler creates these alongside the retained origin; the renderer previously defaulted missing values to `0/1`, silently turning malformed eight-frame movement into an apparent one-frame snap.
- Object interpolation now preserves a retained origin just outside the 20×18 LCD. The static-position helper intentionally rejects offscreen tiles, but using it for a moving origin made an NPC entering at the viewport edge substitute its destination and pop in fully landed; retained movement now keeps the offscreen OAM coordinate through the complete stride.
- Viewport culling now keeps a moving object while either its retained origin or committed destination is visible. Rust previously culled an NPC walking outward as soon as its atomic destination crossed the LCD boundary, dropping the sprite before the eight-frame exit stride finished.
- The battle compositor now retains `battle_message_scene` while an EXP tween or level-stat page is the sole active reward boundary, not only while a text queue is nonempty. Input already remained battle-owned at those boundaries; the renderer could nevertheless fall back to the committed overworld after battle teardown, displaying EXP/stat progression over the wrong scene.
- A retained battle message/reward boundary now requires its retained battle scene. Missing scene state reaches the visible render-error boundary rather than silently substituting the field snapshot and making post-battle presentation appear to succeed on the wrong background.
- Multi-level EXP presentation now recognizes the actual retained level-up page format, `<MON> grew to\nlevel N!`, when restarting the next bar segment. The continuation gate searched for a nonexistent single-line `grew to level` string, so a gain spanning multiple levels could stop after its first full bar and let later level/stat pages overtake the remaining EXP animation.
- Initial and subsequent EXP bar targets are now mandatory at their staging/continuation boundaries. Both queues are constructed from authoritative reward segments; substituting pixel zero for an impossible empty queue could fabricate another full-bar animation and conceal broken reward ordering.
- D-pad input during an active EXP tween or level-stat page is now consumed without dispatching to the hidden battle cursor. The retained reward surface already blocked overworld input, but the shared direction dispatcher could still move the next command selection underneath the overlay, making the menu reappear on an option the player never visibly chose.
### Battle animation input and frame ownership

- Fixed the Rust shell allowing the authoritative battle frame loop to keep
  advancing during enemy frontpic animation and active HP-bar tweening. These
  presentation routines now own their complete Game Boy frame, matching the
  blocking ASM/TypeScript sequencing.
- Fixed the separate visible-control path accepting A/B/Start/Select or D-pad
  input beneath frontpic, capture, move, send-out, trainer-exit, and HP-bar
  animations. The shell now clears UI repeat state and ignores controls until
  the noninteractive animation releases the screen, so hidden battle cursors
  cannot drift and buffered host input cannot leak into the next menu.

### Seamless connection player interpolation

- Fixed retained player positioning requiring the reconstructed source tile
  of a connection stride to already be inside the destination viewport. That
  source is legitimately one tile offscreen after the authoritative map swap;
  Rust now converts it without static visibility clamping and interpolates the
  player and camera through the complete arrival stride, matching TypeScript's
  composite-map pixel movement and Crystal's uninterrupted connection scroll.
- Fixed the retained in-place NPC update path counting and positioning only
  destination-visible objects. NPCs walking out of the viewport no longer
  trigger a complete world rebuild on every stride frame, and NPCs entering
  from or leaving toward an offscreen tile retain the exact origin instead of
  silently snapping to their destination. Missing/zero per-object timing is
  rejected consistently with the full renderer rather than replaced by 0/1.
- Strength boulder dust now shares the boulder's mandatory per-object timing
  and unbounded retained origin. Its own tracking-object lifetime uses the
  source doubled-plus-two duration, so it remains for the exact 18 frames
  after a 16-frame boulder landing. The effect no longer fabricates 0/1-frame
  timing, snaps to the committed destination when its source is just outside
  the viewport, or disappears before an outward stride has fully landed.
- Animated player and object sprites now require their source action frame.
  Rust previously substituted the standing frame when action art was missing,
  making a malformed pack look like characters sliding while walking. Missing
  two-frame movement art is now surfaced at the visible render boundary.
- Fishing's bite presentation now follows the source movement program: four
  eight-frame `fish_got_bite` commands bob the player and rod by one source
  pixel on alternating frames, the shock emote appears only afterward for the
  40-frame pause, and facing Up retains its extra `step_sleep 1`. Rust
  previously showed and slowly bounced the shock icon throughout the tug while
  leaving the player stationary.
- Battle command, text, party, move, Pack, stat, and yes/no windows now require
  the selected source window-frame art. Rust previously swallowed frame-load
  failures and left interactive screens as borderless fill rectangles, hiding
  both menu geometry and malformed pack data. The failure now reaches the
  existing visible render-error boundary; party-menu structural errors also
  propagate instead of panicking from the renderer.
- Enemy frontpic animation now suppresses the queued battle text/menu layer,
  just like send-out and move animation. Rust previously exposed the next
  `Go!`/command page as soon as ball poof ended even while the species-specific
  frontpic program still owned the LCD; input was locked but the future page
  was visibly leaking underneath the animation.
- Player battler and HUD rendering now require the battle's authoritative
  active party index to resolve to a real party slot during enemy send-out and
  ordinary command play. Rust previously omitted the player sprite and/or HUD
  silently, leaving an enemy-only battle screen interactive. Inconsistent
  identity now reaches the visible render-error boundary instead.
- Opening, moving, and cancel-detecting the battle move cursor now use one
  checked menu-size boundary tied to the authoritative active party slot. Rust
  previously fabricated `command slots + CANCEL` when that identity was
  missing, allowing input to navigate a move menu that visible rendering could
  not construct. The boundary also rejects disagreement between rendered moves
  and selectable command slots instead of letting cursor indices address two
  different lists.
- Noninteractive field animation frames now own the shell hotkey path as well
  as the authoritative overworld tick. Fishing hook/pause frames, Dig/Teleport
  travel, Flash, and the post-text Cut/Whirlpool/Headbutt effects can no longer
  open menus or dispatch A/B over an animation. The preceding field-move use
  textbox remains interactive until it closes.
- The shared cursor-repeat dispatcher now observes the same field-animation
  lock, including Fly and Waterfall, and battle transitions explicitly join
  the noninteractive battle lock. This closes the separate keyboard path that
  could move or confirm a concealed cursor while the visible LCD was owned by
  a transition or field animation.
- Trainer approach, walk-warp, healing-machine, Magnet Train, and non-text
  blackout phases now use that same exclusive presentation boundary. Their
  frame systems were already blocking overworld ticks, but the parallel menu
  dispatcher could still service stale shell cursors underneath them.
- Battle dialogue, core field dialogue, and shell-owned field/PC notices now
  share Crystal's per-letter acceleration boundary. Holding A, B, or the
  desktop Start/confirm key clears the current delay and prints at one
  character per LCD frame; it no longer reveals an entire page atomically.
  Incomplete field/PC notices can no longer be dismissed, queued notice pages
  retain their own reveal identity, and the prompt arrow appears only after
  the visible page has finished printing.
- Shop welcome and result notices now use that printer too, including the
  source-style blinking completion arrow. These commonly visited overworld
  textboxes previously appeared fully populated in one frame and could be
  dismissed before any text-speed cadence existed.
- Cut, Whirlpool, Headbutt, and Rock Smash use text now continue into their
  source effects automatically when printing completes; Rust previously added
  a nonexistent acknowledgement press between `writetext` and the effect.
  A no-encounter follow-up is staged only after the retained effect finishes,
  while a pending field battle still enters at that same effect boundary.
- Teleport's 60-frame source pause now starts after its sentence finishes
  printing instead of counting down underneath it. Field-move `waitbutton`
  surfaces (including Surf, Waterfall, Dig/Escape Rope, and pending encounter
  notices) no longer borrow the blinking `promptbutton` arrow.
- Whirlpool now releases the retained obstacle scene when its use text closes,
  matching `DisappearWhirlpool`: the replacement block is visible beneath the
  effect instead of the removed whirlpool reappearing for all 32 frames.
- Strength now enters exported `Script_StrengthFromMenu` instead of a
  hand-authored three-phase notice. `refreshmap`, `UpdateTimePals`, the exact
  `SetStrengthFlag` callasm, both `writetext` commands, `readmem
  wStrengthSpecies`, `cry 0`, `pause 3`, and `closetext` all execute through the
  common interpreter in source order. Menu dispatch only records
  `wCurPartyMon`; the engine flag, species byte, and nickname buffers remain
  untouched until `SetStrengthFlag` runs.
- Ordinary Surf collision now treats `CURRENT_DOWN` as part of the waterfall
  family, matching TypeScript's `isWaterfallCollision` boundary. Rust had
  admitted that permission as ordinary water, allowing an invalid entry and a
  subsequent forced-current step where Crystal requires the waterfall path.
- Surf can now enter the waterfall collision family while moving down, while
  continuing to block upward and sideways entry outside the Waterfall field
  move. Rust previously blocked descent too, making valid one-way waterfall
  traversal impassable despite TypeScript and ASM explicitly permitting it.
- Sweet Scent now retains the source `waitbutton` presentation across both
  pages. Its use line and the queued “nothing here” result no longer acquire a
  blinking `promptbutton` arrow merely because the latter was dequeued; the
  wait style is carried as explicit runtime state rather than inferred from
  rendered text.
- Sweet Scent menu use now queues the exported
  `.SweetScent@SweetScentFromMenu` root. The exact source-certified
  `SweetScentEncounter` callasm owns encounter RNG after `UseSweetScentText`
  and `waitbutton`; `randomwildmon` and `startbattle` remain later script
  commands. The deleted eager path could roll and activate battle at menu
  dispatch, before any of those source boundaries.
- Diploma, Magnet Train, healing-machine, and ordinary dialog construction no
  longer discard asset or dialog-state errors. A failed full-screen surface
  now reaches the existing visible render-error boundary instead of vanishing
  while its underlying gameplay input remains live.
- Every production battle-entry path now requires a valid battle snapshot and
  active player party identity through a checked result. Rust previously
  swallowed snapshot failure and panicked on a missing active slot after
  clearing the prior screen; failures now reach the visible runtime-error
  boundary instead of leaving a blank or aborted transition.
- Canceling a battle item-target surface now propagates target-close failure.
  Rust previously discarded that error and returned success, allowing stale
  party/move targeting UI to remain visible under the restored battle menu.
- Battle-transition selection now requires the current map and its source
  environment. Missing data previously defaulted to an outdoor transition,
  silently choosing the wrong cave/field wipe instead of exposing a malformed
  runtime pack.
- Player grass rustle now uses the occupied collision byte and ASM's complete
  `CheckSuperTallGrassTile`/`CheckGrassTile` union (`$10,$14,$18,$1c,$20,$28`).
  The former TypeScript-derived `$14/$18/$1c` subset omitted three valid
  temporary-object paths. Lifetime is `OBJECT_STEP_DURATION - 2` as observed
  after `MovementFunction_ShakingGrass` and the same-frame tracking step: six
  visible frames for walking and two for bike-speed movement.
- Grass encounter classification now uses `CheckGrassCollision.blocks`
  exactly (`$08,$14,$18,$28,$01,$48..$4c`). The TypeScript-derived `$10`
  and `$1c` additions were removed because the cartridge does not classify
  those permissions as grass encounter surfaces; `$01` remains owned by the
  separate water-surface path.
- Wild encounter surface resolution now follows the TypeScript reference directly: an occupied water collision resolves to the water table without depending on a potentially stale Surf movement-mode flag.
- Cave-wide land encounters now follow ASM's exported environment byte exactly: both `CAVE` and `DUNGEON` permit ordinary non-ice land, while a cave-looking tileset alone does not. This removes the TypeScript-derived tileset heuristic that suppressed tower/facility `DUNGEON` encounters such as Tin Tower and could grant cave-floor behavior to an `INDOOR` map such as Seafoam Gym.
- Card Flip, Slot Machine, and Unown puzzle rendering now propagate required-art decoding failures through the visible runtime error boundary instead of retaining an input-owning blank screen.
- Visible battle entry now retains TypeScript's full transition state timing: determine/load/setup frames, three complete 25-frame palette sweeps, the intervening NEXT/SETUP states, and exact 15-frame wave, 9-frame zoom, 61-frame spin, or 21-frame scatter outro plus FINISH delays. Each outro is reconstructed from the state visible before that frame's mutation, so its first geometry update is no longer drawn one frame early. Rust previously flashed immediately and compressed these paths to 83-117 frames, making battles appear substantially too early.
- Battle-music and entry-cry synchronization now report snapshot failures through the visible runtime boundary. They previously returned silently, allowing a battle to retain map music or omit cries while continuing with an unexplained partial presentation.
- Start no longer accelerates or dismisses field/battle text. Rust treated Enter/Start as a third confirmation input for both `PrintLetterDelay` and battle messages, while ASM and TypeScript accept only held A/B for acceleration and A/B at `PromptButton` boundaries.
- Completed field-dialogue page advances now play exactly one `SFX_READ_TEXT_2` before revealing the next page, matching ASM paragraph/continue commands' `PromptButton` call. Rust previously reserved the click for the terminal pending-text wait, making multi-page dialogue advance silently.
- Dismissing an arrow-bearing field notice now plays the same PromptButton click for either A or B. Arrowless `waitbutton` notices remain silent, preserving Strength/Sweet Scent and other source waits that do not call `PromptButton`.
- PC notices plus shop welcome/follow-up text now play one PromptButton click on either A or B before mutation. These surfaces already drew the 16-frame blinking arrow but previously dismissed silently, unlike their ASM dialogue boundary.
- Dispatching an object interaction now commits that object's facing toward the player before the resulting script snapshot is exposed. Rust previously played the talk cue and opened the script while leaving the NPC turned away; TypeScript turns the selected object first, and the retained scene now reflects that same interaction boundary.
- Restored the acknowledgement cue for every successful overworld A-button
  target. Rust had only sounded object interactions, leaving signs, hidden
  items, standard collision scripts, and contextual collision prompts silent;
  ASM routes background events through `PlayTalkObject` and every successful
  tile-collision event through `PlayClickSFX`, while TypeScript follows the
  same one-cue-before-dispatch boundary.
- Player OBJ priority now remains keyed to the visible step's origin tile
  until landing. Rust's tile-atomic authority previously sorted the still-
  interpolating sprite at its destination immediately, causing overlap with
  NPCs and priority edges to snap at step start; TypeScript retains the origin
  player coordinates through the eight-frame stride. Two-tile ledge jumps use
  the takeoff tile for their first stride and the intermediate tile for their
  second, matching the source's chained movement steps; the ledge shadow uses
  that same priority boundary instead of separating onto the final plane.
- Battle HUDs, battle command/text surfaces, field dialogue/notices and menus,
  and map-entry signs now require the shared bitmap font plus its space and
  replacement glyph before drawing.
  Font decode or structural failures previously produced empty/zero-sized
  glyph streams while leaving the input-owning screen active; they now reach
  the existing visible render-error boundary.
- Direct and counter-mediated A-button object interactions now reject an NPC
  while its retained eight-frame walk is still in flight. Rust previously
  resolved the tile-atomic destination immediately and could rotate/start the
  NPC's script before its sprite landed; TypeScript's `walking` guard and
  ASM's `CheckFacingObject` reject that interaction boundary.
- Trainer sightline acquisition uses the same retained-walk gate. A trainer
  can no longer notice the player from its atomically committed destination
  while its sprite is still crossing from the prior tile, matching
  TypeScript's trainer eligibility check before the emote/approach sequence.
- Move-forget and battle PP-item target prompts now render their two text rows
  at separate tile coordinates. Rust previously passed embedded newlines to a
  single-line glyph spawner, which displayed a replacement glyph and ran the
  second sentence horizontally off-layout on these input-owning screens.
- Battle and party HUD status labels now normalize the TypeScript spellings,
  Rust canonical spellings, short persisted tokens, and letter case to the
  five Crystal HUD tokens (`PSN/SLP/PAR/BRN/FRZ`); party confusion likewise
  resolves to `CNF`. Previously a lowercase or short-token afflicted Pokémon
  was rendered as healthy (or showed its level in battle).
- Player grass rustle tests the exact occupied collision quadrant rather than
  asking whether any quadrant of the destination metatile contains grass.
  Mixed grass/path blocks therefore follow the tile-local source collision
  byte without TypeScript's whole-metatile heuristic.
- Grass on a ledge landing now begins at the eight-frame midpoint of the
  sixteen-frame jump and retains the source six-frame tracking-object life,
  ending two updates before the landing step completes. Rust previously
  started the final tile's rustle at takeoff and then retained it for the
  complete second half; ASM models the jump as two chained steps and applies
  both grass-object decrements when the landing step starts.
# Bicycle transition timing and split pack-path parity

- Made both visible Bicycle-use routes retain the pre-toggle overworld scene beneath the acknowledgement text. Crystal changes `VAR_MOVEMENT` before the text but does not run `UpdatePlayerSprite` until after it closes; the refactored generic pack path had exposed the new sprite one boundary early.
- Queue `MUSIC_BICYCLE` immediately on mounting, before opening the acknowledgement. The normal current-music synchronizer intentionally pauses for field text, but ASM `BikeFunction` starts bicycle music before `Script_GetOnBike` displays its text. Dismount still resumes map music after the text closes through the normal synchronizer, matching `FinishGettingOffBike`.

# Exact walking-warp sound classification

- Corrected visible walking-warps to follow `GetWarpSFX`'s exact collision-byte comparisons: only `COLL_DOOR` (`0x71`) plays `SFX_ENTER_DOOR`, `COLL_WARP_PANEL` plays `SFX_WARP_TO`, and other ordinary warp collisions fall through to `SFX_EXIT_BUILDING`. Pit collisions dispatch `FallIntoMapScript` without `warpsound`, so they now remain silent at this boundary. Rust previously grouped alternate doors, stairs, and cave entrances with `COLL_DOOR`; TypeScript currently carries the same source divergence.
- Pit arrivals now continue after the white map fade through `FallIntoMapScript`'s visible sequence: `SFX_KINESIS`, the 16-frame skyfall setup at the inherited sprite offset, the 16-frame falling motion, `SFX_STRENGTH`, and `earthquake 16`. Destination scene scripts and callbacks remain deferred until that retained movement completes instead of regaining control immediately after a generic door warp.

# Direct forced-tile movement

- Current (`0x3*`) and directional walk (`0x4*`/`0x5*`) collisions now use a dedicated direct step matching `CheckTile`'s `.continue_walk -> .DoStep` path. They no longer enter ordinary terrain/object collision, Strength-push, ledge, or blocked-edge checks. Ice remains on `TryStep` and therefore keeps normal collision behavior. This prevents source-valid conveyors and water currents from sticking on destination collision logic that Crystal never invokes.

# Non-wrapping battle command grid

- The visible 2x2 FIGHT/POKéMON/PACK/RUN cursor now stops at each row and column edge. Rust previously used XOR for both axes, making an edge press wrap to the opposite entry. `BattleMenuHeader` uses `STATICMENU_CURSOR | STATICMENU_DISABLE_B` without `STATICMENU_WRAP`, so only an in-bounds directional press changes selection.
- The selected-party SWITCH/STATS/CANCEL submenu is likewise bounded. Its `BattleMonMenu` data uses `STATICMENU_CURSOR | STATICMENU_NO_TOP_SPACING` without `STATICMENU_WRAP`; Rust previously sent it through the generic wrapping list helper, so UP on SWITCH selected CANCEL and DOWN on CANCEL selected SWITCH.
- Physical B now plays the single source menu click when leaving either MoveSelectionScreen or the battle party list. Selecting the move list's explicit CANCEL row still plays exactly one click through ParsePlayerAction; that route now closes directly rather than reusing the physical-B handler and double-playing the cue.
- Party Stats controls now remain locked while a queued or playing Pokémon cry owns `StatsScreenWaitCry`. Page cycling, party-member changes, and A/B exit previously accepted input immediately and could change or close the visible screen underneath its opening cry.

# Battle textbox scrolling and baselines

- Battle prose now retains its complete 18-character layout instead of silently
  truncating after four rows. The visible window uses Crystal's two text
  baselines at `TEXTBOX_INNERY` and `TEXTBOX_INNERY + 2`; longer messages are
  split into two-line pages, and a completed page requires A/B before the next
  pair begins with the normal read-text click. This preserves the flattened
  runtime equivalent of ASM `_ContText` (prompt, two `TextScroll` calls, then
  continue at the lower baseline). Rust previously drew four tightly stacked
  rows at 13–16, discarded later text, and a single acknowledgement dismissed
  the entire message.
- Battle-entry party-ball status icons now use the same normalized status-byte
  interpretation as the battle HUD. A serialized healthy token such as `OK`
  (or a differently cased no-status token) no longer renders the afflicted
  ball merely because it is not the exact Rust string `NONE`; fainted still
  takes precedence, matching `DrawPartyHUD`.
- The catching tutorial now hides the Dude backpic only while the Poké Ball
  throw animation owns the player-side OAM, then restores it afterward. Rust
  previously respawned Dude unconditionally throughout capture, drawing the
  throw through the trainer sprite; TypeScript's `throwPokeballActive` path and
  the tutorial battle animation both suppress that backpic during the throw.
- The battle PP-item move-target screen now requires the exact selected party
  slot, move cursor, usable-item cursor, and carried item record. Invalid
  retained input state previously made this active screen disappear silently
  or guessed the restore/raise prompt; it now reaches the visible render-error
  boundary like the surrounding PACK and move-learning surfaces.
- The active FIGHT move list now requires its authoritative active-party slot,
  exact cursor, and selected move metadata. Rust previously left an
  input-owning blank screen when the retained slot/cursor state was incomplete
  and fabricated `INVALID MOVE` when exported metadata was absent; both cases
  now reach the visible render-error boundary.
- The ordinary overworld PACK now requires its exact pocket cursor, selected
  item metadata, item-action list, and action cursor. Those invalid retained
  states previously blanked the entire input-owning PACK or mislabeled a
  missing item as CANCEL's description; they now reach the same visible
  render-error boundary used by the rest of the field shell.
- The field FLY map now requires an exact destination cursor and a Pokégear
  landmark for every selectable destination. Rust previously fabricated `NO
  DESTINATION` for invalid retained input and silently omitted missing map
  markers while leaving travel selectable; both now reach the visible field
  render-error boundary.
- The field Pokédex now renders the exact selected species and detail page.
  Rust previously clamped invalid cursors/pages, silently omitted missing
  entry data, and fabricated `INVALID DEX ENTRY`; incomplete authoritative
  state now reaches the visible field render-error boundary instead.
- Scripted post-capture Pokédex entries now propagate that same exact-state
  renderer error; this secondary call site previously discarded it.
- Kurt's required Apricorn picker now requires its exact cursor, selected
  quantity item, and window-frame art. Rust previously let this story-critical
  selector disappear silently or remain interactive as a frameless substitute;
  incomplete state now reaches the visible field render-error boundary.
- Follower movement now retains Crystal's queued next step in the overworld
  session across separate `applymovement` programs. The follower consumes the
  prior queued direction and stride before the leader's current step replaces
  it, and the visible script-movement renderer starts from that same retained
  command. Rust previously reconstructed a one-tile direction from geometry at
  every program boundary, losing jump/mixed-speed continuity and sometimes
  choosing a different turn than TypeScript's persistent follower queue.
- The retained follower command now includes its exact visible duration as
  well as direction and stride: 4-frame fast, 8-frame normal, 16-frame slow,
  with jump commands doubling those durations. A queued mixed-speed command
  crossing an `applymovement` boundary no longer reverts to an eight-frame
  animation.
- Queued follower commands now retain jump and standing-frame semantics too.
  Follower jumps receive their own source-shaped vertical arc while their
  leader continues independently, and jump/slide followers use the standing
  sprite frame instead of being rendered as flat ordinary walks.
- Ordinary leader movement now primes an absent follower queue by moving one
  runtime stride toward the leader instead of snapping the follower directly
  onto the leader's previous tile. This preserves the source's initial
  one-command lag when `follow` begins with separated objects.
- Starting `follow` now records an NPC follower's exact pre-step runtime tile,
  allowing the generic object-diff renderer to retain and interpolate the
  first eight-frame stride. Previously a follower absent from the runtime-tile
  map could snap on its first ordinary overworld step before later steps became
  smooth.
- Pokégear rendering now requires the selected card to be unlocked and keeps
  map, phone, radio-frequency, and radio-transcript cursors exact. Rust
  previously clamped several of these only while drawing, ignored a missing
  map landmark, and substituted a raw ASM label for missing radio text, so the
  displayed option could differ from the action input would execute.
- Field and battle party-summary screens now require the exact party slot and
  page plus authoritative species typing, held-item data, and move metadata.
  Rust previously clamped invalid retained selection state and leaked
  `INVALID TYPE`, `INVALID ITEM`, or `PP INVALID` substitutions, allowing the
  displayed summary to disagree with the selected Pokémon; failures now reach
  the appropriate visible render-error boundary.
- The field move-reorder screen now requires its exact party slot, move cursor,
  reorder origin, move metadata, and ASM description. It previously vanished
  silently for bad selection state and fabricated invalid type, zero power, or
  placeholder-description content while retaining input ownership.
- Main party selection and its action/give/take submenus now require exact
  cursors and action derivation instead of clamping or disappearing silently.
- START and OPTIONS now require exact cursors plus complete window-frame art.
  Bug Contest START status also requires the caught Pokémon's recorded level
  instead of displaying a fabricated level zero. These input-owning windows
  now fail through the visible field boundary as a single complete surface.
- Shop rendering now requires exact top/item cursors, authoritative item
  records, carried sell quantities, descriptions, prices, and a nonzero
  quantity prompt. Rust previously repaired these states with cursor zero,
  quantity zero, price zero, or blank text while purchases remained active.
- Bill's PC storage, summaries, release/action prompts, item storage, and box
  selection now require exact boxes, slots, pages, cursors, and catalog data.
  Rust previously blanked missing storage screens or substituted cursor zero,
  empty descriptions, unnamed `BOX` records, and missing prompt cursors while
  those PC surfaces still owned input.
- Input and confirmation now preserve that same strict retained-state boundary
  instead of repairing it after a surface has opened. START, party actions and
  summaries, field/battle Pack rows and actions, battle Pack party targets,
  Fly destinations, scripted shop buy/sell lists, and Bill's PC box/MOVE
  selections all reject a missing, wrong-surface, or out-of-range live cursor.
  Source initialization remains at the actual constructor: in particular,
  `InitPartyMenuWithCancel` resets an invalid saved `wPartyMenuCursor` to the
  first Pokemon, while later navigation and confirmation never clamp it. Pack
  pocket and Mail-composer tests now enter through those constructors rather
  than relying on navigation to fabricate cursor zero. Compiled `verticalmenu`
  movement and confirmation likewise require the exact retained source surface
  instead of rebasing a wrong surface to the first menu in the snapshot. The
  obsolete generic `script_command_cursor` elevator branch is removed: the
  retained elevator cursor is now the only input owner, so no second controller
  can manufacture a one-row menu or move unrelated host selection state. The
  elevator cursor also retains ownership when its compiled surface is invalid;
  its renderer, navigation, and A now report that exact mismatch instead of
  drawing a blank/`ELEVATOR EMPTY` surface, clearing the cursor, logging `NO
  ELEVATOR`, or falling through to the overworld beneath the still-retained
  script boundary. The
  complete Bevy library suite passes with 650 tests and two performance probes
  ignored.
- Both full and incremental PLAYER'S PC dialog paths now require the exact
  action cursor instead of independently substituting action zero, preventing
  retained-dialog redraws from changing the visibly selected command.
- Battle party item targeting now requires the exact party/CANCEL row instead
  of clamping the retained cursor, and an open SWITCH/STATS/CANCEL submenu now
  requires its exact action cursor rather than disappearing silently.
- Every visible battle PACK row now requires its catalog item record before
  drawing. Missing nonselected item metadata previously leaked an `INVALID
  ITEM` label into an otherwise active bag even though only the selected row's
  description had been validated.
- Battle move-learning and move-forget screens now require metadata for the
  pending move and every displayed existing move. An open YES/NO prompt also
  requires its exact cursor instead of silently disappearing.
- Battle main-action, faint replacement, and trainer-shift entry derivation
  now propagates invalid cursors or missing trainer-party state to the visible
  render boundary instead of creating selectable `INVALID ...` menu rows.
- The main battle command renderer now propagates a missing active party slot
  through the visible battle error boundary instead of panicking while
  constructing the `What will ... do?` prompt.
- Poké Ball flight rendering now reports an unexpectedly deinitialized throw
  object through the visible battle boundary instead of panicking and
  terminating play.
- Battle HUDs now distinguish recognized no-token states from malformed status
  data. Empty/`OK`/`NONE`/`HEALTHY` and confusion still display the level,
  matching TypeScript's battle HUD mapping; both underscore- and space-form
  bad poison render `PSN`. A genuinely unknown status no longer silently makes
  an afflicted battler look healthy.
- A final source-only battle HUD layout comparison confirms Rust uses the same
  ASM/TypeScript tile coordinates for enemy and player names, level-or-status,
  gender, HP bars, player HP digits, and the player EXP bar. The EXP fill also
  advances right-to-left with the same eight full/partial tile convention as
  TypeScript and `FillInExpBar`.
- The shipped TypeScript movement corpus was compared directly with Rust's
  verified movement opcode inventory. Every opcode actually emitted by the
  corpus (`step`, `big_step`, `jump_step`, `turn_head`, facing lock/unlock,
  and `step_end`) has a Rust runtime and visible-animation path. Rust's broader
  teleport timing was retained intentionally: its 32-frame `teleport_from`
  and 49-frame `teleport_to` sequencing matches the initialization and action
  ticks in `map_objects.asm`, while the TypeScript movement task currently
  shortens/lengthens those sequences.
- Every move effect referenced by the shipped TypeScript move catalog is
  present in Rust's supported battle-effect inventory. This source comparison
  found no catalog move capable of reaching `UnsupportedMoveEffect` merely by
  being selected during ordinary play.
- Global player-event scripts are now exported from
  `engine/overworld/events.asm` and its linked trainer, whiteout, breeding, and
  player-object sources through the exact `PlayerEventScriptPointers` roots.
  Pack verification requires the exact ordered 11-entry `dba` ABI, including
  the terminal duplicate `InvalidEventScript`; reordered, duplicated, missing,
  or substituted event slots are rejected before runtime materialization.
  Every known root must also match an exact ASM body certificate, including the
  interpreter-owned invalid, connection, door-warp, pitfall, and direction
  programs; interpreter-owned roots are materialized alongside standard and
  phone scripts. `deactivatefacing` uses its one-frame byte counter and
  releases all objects at the delay boundary; `ChangeDirectionScript` then
  executes the separately certified exact three-instruction
  `EnableWildEncounters` CPU body synchronously, including the inverse mapping
  between `PLAYEREVENTS_WILD_ENCOUNTERS` and Rust's
  `STATUSFLAGS_NO_WILD_ENCOUNTERS_F`. Missing, stale, or silently divergent
  roots and called bodies now fail pack verification.

# PC, item, and Pokémon record parity (2026-08-12)

- PC boxes are now enforced as ASM counted lists: occupied entries must be
  contiguous, removal shifts the tail left, and insertion shifts the tail
  right. `MovePcPokemonWithoutMail` inserts before the selected destination
  and applies `CheckTrivialMove`'s same-box cursor adjustment instead of
  swapping two slots. All four source jump-table paths (box-to-box,
  party-to-box, box-to-party, and party-to-party) are staged atomically, apply
  the source blackout/mail restrictions, and restore deposited move PP after
  PP Ups. The visible move surface can cycle between PARTY and every box,
  exposes occupied source rows plus exactly one insertion position, and sends
  those locations through the same authoritative command. Evidence:
  `engine/pokemon/bills_pc.asm::MovePKMNWithoutMail_InsertMon` and
  `engine/pokemon/move_mon_wo_mail.asm`.
- The item, ball, key-item, and player-PC item pockets now preserve their ASM
  list order instead of sorting by item ID. Receipt fills matching stacks in
  list order and appends a duplicate stack when required; removal operates on
  the selected stack. Select-driven switching now reorders stacks and performs
  Crystal's same-item 99-count combine/overflow behavior. TM/HM remains a
  fixed indexed count table with no Select reorder path, matching its separate
  `TMHMPocket` menu. Evidence: `home/items.asm::PutItemInPocket`,
  `engine/items/switch_items.asm::SwitchItemsInBag`, and
  `engine/items/pack.asm::Pack_InterpretJoypad`.
- Key-item receipt and tossing now operate on one list entry regardless of the
  generic quantity argument, matching their one-byte pocket records. Player-PC
  deposit, withdraw, and toss carry the selected stack index and preflight the
  complete mutation, so duplicate stacks and failures cannot mutate a
  different row or leave a half-applied transfer.
- TM/HM receipt is now atomic at the 99-copy boundary. An addition whose sum
  would reach 100 or overflow is rejected without changing the saved count;
  Rust previously saturated the count to 99 and reported success. Evidence:
  `engine/items/items.asm::ReceiveTMHM`.
- Persisted caught data now models the actual two source bytes: six-bit caught
  level plus two-bit time of day, and one-bit original-trainer gender plus
  seven-bit landmark. The invented caught-ball byte was removed; ordinary
  capture records the live time and player gender, NPC gifts/trades retain the
  zero caught level/time and gift landmark, and Poké Seer displays the retained
  Morning/Day/Night value. Evidence:
  `constants/pokemon_data_constants.asm`,
  `engine/pokemon/caught_data.asm`, and `engine/events/poke_seer.asm`.
- `addval` now stores the low byte of the sum in the script accumulator.
  Rust previously retained signed and over-255 integer results, so subtracting
  from zero produced `-1` instead of `$ff` and incrementing `$ff` produced 256
  instead of zero. This matches `Script_addval`'s byte-sized `wScriptVar` write
  and the real underground switch scripts that use `addval -1`, `-2`, and
  `-3`. A focused regression covers underflow and overflow.
- Blue Card points now use the saved `blue_card_balance` byte as the single
  authoritative `wBlueCardBalance` value. `writevar VAR_BLUECARDBALANCE`
  commits that byte, `readvar` derives from it, the Blue Card item displays it,
  and Buena prize redemption updates its exact runtime mirror. Rust previously
  let the generic script-variable map diverge from the saved field, so Buena's
  real `readvar`/`addval 1`/`writevar` sequence could display a point that was
  neither spendable nor visible in the trainer snapshot. Saved mismatches and
  values above the source 30-point cap are now rejected. A source-backed
  RadioTower2F regression executes the exact three-command award sequence.
- Live `readvar` now derives every source-used typed overworld value that was
  previously absent from the generic variable map: caught Pokédex count,
  current map group/environment, selected-box free space, Bug Contest minutes,
  pending special phone-call byte, and Kenji's break timer. Rust previously errored
  with `UnsetVariable` on these real scripts; in particular, talking to a
  registered Kenji reached `VAR_KENJI_BREAK` and aborted before his time/event
  branches. Source-backed regressions execute the exact Celadon designer,
  Route 29 tutorial, National Park gate, Mom/Elm phone, and Route 45 commands.
  Phone constants are now exported into the numeric script catalog, so the
  symbolic `VAR_CALLERID` written by incoming/outgoing call setup compares as
  the exact `wCurCaller` byte in generic callee scripts; a regression routes
  Kenji through `PhoneScript_AnswerPhone_Male`. Special-call catalog rows also
  retain their exact source byte IDs instead of only their names. Fly from Bike
  is locked to the source `PLAYER_NORMAL` destination mode.
- The Bug Contest post-battle global script is now exported and materialized
  from the source-declared `GlobalScriptRoots` list. Rust previously ignored
  that metadata, so `BugCatchingContestBattleScript` was absent at runtime;
  even if reached, `readmem wParkBallsRemaining` had no mirror of the typed
  contest counter. The exact source command now reads the live Park Ball byte
  and branches to `BugCatchingContestOutOfBallsScript` when it reaches zero.
- Sparse generic script memory now preserves the Game Boy's cleared-WRAM
  semantics: `readmem` returns byte zero before an address's first write.
  Rust previously raised `UnsetMemory`, aborting source flows whose zero state
  is intentional, including Ilex Forest's initial Farfetch'd position and the
  first Moomoo Berry counter read. Typed WRAM mirrors are populated before this
  read, so live counters such as Park Balls remain authoritative.
- Interactive service specials now preserve `wScriptVar` unless their ASM
  routine explicitly writes it. Name Rater, Poké Seer, Move Deleter, Mom's
  bank, the Pokémon Center PC, Oak's PC rating, the overworld Town Map, and the
  Unown Printer, and Link Record display no longer publish host-only success
  values; Move Deleter, Poké Seer, Mom's bank, and Link Record also drop their
  source-free diagnostic variables, and
  the bank no longer leaves a fabricated menu owner after its synchronous
  routine returns. Move Tutor retains its real ABI instead: a successful learn
  writes `FALSE` (`0`), while cancellation/no learn writes `$ff`. Rust
  previously wrote `1` on success and `0` otherwise, inverting Goldenrod's
  `ifequal FALSE, .TeachMove` continuation and potentially charging on the
  wrong branch. Evidence: `engine/events/specials.asm::NameRater`,
  `engine/events/poke_seer.asm`, `engine/events/move_deleter.asm`,
  `engine/events/mom.asm::BankOfMom`, `engine/events/pokecenter_pc.asm`,
  `engine/events/prof_oaks_pc.asm`, the menu wrappers in `specials.asm`, and
  `engine/events/move_tutor.asm::MoveTutor`.
- The shared visible-special adapter now preserves `wScriptVar` and records
  only its typed graphics event. It previously wrote a host-only
  `_visual_special` variable and forced the accumulator to `1` after every
  Battle Tower fade, player-sprite update, heal-machine animation, Surf start
  step, sprite reload, decoration toggle, Magnet Train ride, Diploma path, and
  Unown-word display. Several of those routines consume `wScriptVar` as their
  exact input selector, and none writes a result byte in ASM. Evidence:
  `engine/events/heal_machine_anim.asm::HealMachineAnim`,
  `engine/events/magnet_train.asm::MagnetTrain`,
  `engine/events/unown_walls.asm::DisplayUnownWords`, and each directly called
  routine named by the special pointer table.
- `WarpToSpawnPoint` now does only its source operation: it clears the Safari
  and Bug Contest timer status bits, synchronizes the typed contest timer, and
  returns without reading spawn data, changing `wScriptVar`, publishing a
  destination, or mutating the overworld. `newloadmap` preserves and consumes
  the destination staged by `warpcheck`, field travel, whiteout, or another
  preceding source operation; the visible shell prioritizes that lifecycle
  boundary over the coexisting warp request and runs destination setup only
  after transition. Non-`newloadmap` load commands continue to discard stale
  warp requests. Whiteout's collapsed runtime command resolves `GetWhiteoutSpawn`
  and commits the transition at its embedded `newloadmap` boundary. Evidence:
  `engine/overworld/events.asm::WarpToSpawnPoint`,
  `engine/events/whiteout.asm::Script_Whiteout`, and all eleven source
  `newloadmap` call sites.

# Modal script continuation parity

- Acknowledging `promptbutton` now resumes through the visible one-command
  executor instead of the headless bulk script runner.
  The bulk path could execute the next modal `special` before its cartridge UI
  supplied input; the main-story `CopScript` therefore attempted `NameRival`
  without `_rival_name` and stopped immediately after the officer's first
  textbox. The visible path now opens the naming screen, commits the selected
  (or source-default `SILVER`) name, resumes at `ElmsLabOfficerText2`, executes
  the officer's departure, and commits `SCENE_ELMSLAB_NOOP`. This preserves
  `engine/overworld/scripting.asm`'s synchronous prompt semantics and
  `engine/events/specials.asm::NameRival`, where `_NamingScreen` returns before
  ScriptEvents advances.
- `givepoke` no longer opens the Rust-only one-option gift selector or consumes
  an extra A press. Reaching Cyndaquil's `givepoke` command now enters the
  authored nickname decision synchronously and only then resumes Elm's
  continuation. Storage now happens before that decision, matching
  `TryAddMonToParty`/`SendMonIntoBox`; an accepted nickname updates the exact
  returned party or box slot, while full party plus selected box returns 2
  without opening the nickname screen. A successful box destination now
  resolves the exported `_WasSentToBillsPCText` with the final nickname and
  blocks script continuation on that prompt, matching `GivePoke`'s post-name
  `PrintText`. The regressions begin on the exact compiled command and require
  the stored Pokémon, nickname prompt, synchronized box nickname metadata, PC
  notice, and full-storage return behavior at their source boundaries.
  Five-argument custom gifts now resolve both ASM `db` name labels directly:
  Kenya is stored as `KENYA` with OT `RANDY`, skips the player nickname prompt,
  and uses the exported `RANDY_OT_ID` in party storage. If that custom gift is
  sent to the selected box, the runtime records the two additional `Random`
  calls and assigns their source-derived 16-bit OT ID; a full selected box
  returns 2 without consuming those calls. This follows the custom-identity
  branches in `engine/pokemon/move_mon.asm::GivePoke` without map- or
  species-specific policy. The following `givepokemail GiftSpearowMail` also
  preserves the ASM `next` line boundary in saved mail; mail validation now
  permits that exact newline while continuing to reject other control bytes.
  Gift caught data now follows the same split: ordinary gifts record their
  authored level, current time, player gender, and exact map landmark; eggs
  substitute exported `CAUGHT_EGG_LEVEL`; custom gifts record level/time zero,
  exported `LANDMARK_GIFT`, and the gender byte immediately following the OT
  name in ASM. The typed request carries this metadata into either party or PC
  storage, matching `SetCaughtData`, `SetBoxMonCaughtData`,
  `SetEggMonCaughtData`, and `SetGiftMonCaughtData`.
  This matches `Script_givepoke`, whose `GivePoke`/nickname work completes
  synchronously before ScriptEvents returns.
- Party and mailbox `READ` now enter a retained 160x144 Mail LCD instead of
  showing Rust-only notice text. All ten stationery types rebuild their exact
  `mail_2.asm` tile loads, palette row, border and decoration tilemap from the
  canonical `.1bpp` assets, including Surf/Lite Blue's cross-`INCBIN` loads and
  Blue Sky's packed Dragonite/Sentret graphics. Message and author placement,
  Portrait Mail's species frontpic, A/B dismissal, modal input ownership,
  deterministic hashing, fullscreen retention, and navigation reset behavior
  now follow `ReadAnyMail`; START's Game Boy Printer path remains unavailable.
- Scripted elevators now preserve the full `Script_elevator`/`Elevator`
  boundary. Opening the menu clears `wScriptVar`; A belongs to the visible
  floor selector before the retained textbox and script cursor, while B clears
  the menu and resumes the authored false branch. `.FindCurrentFloor` requires
  a floor matching `wBackupMapGroup/Number`; an unmatched backup returns false
  without opening a menu. Selecting `wElevatorOriginFloor` likewise returns
  false without queuing a warp, playing `SFX_ELEVATOR`, or running the ride
  branch. Other floors alone set `wScriptVar` true and enter the destination
  warp path. Source-backed Celadon elevator regressions cover all four paths.

# Scripted wild battle setup parity

- `loadwildmon` is now an ordinary executable compiled-script mutation instead
  of metadata consumed only by the later `startbattle` boundary. It writes the
  exact `Script_loadwildmon` bytes: `wBattleScriptFlags=$80`,
  `wTempWildMonSpecies`, and `wCurPartyLevel`. Species ids are checked against
  the exported Pokémon catalog and the level must fit `GetScriptByte`.
- All 18 compiled source positions are classified and the canonical packs were
  regenerated. Fixed `startbattle` dispatch now rejects a skipped or mismatched
  setup instead of synthesizing the encounter from static metadata. A real-pack
  Route 36 regression executes `WateredWeirdTreeScript` at its authored
  `loadwildmon SUDOWOODO, 20` position and verifies the next cursor and all
  three WRAM values.

# Executable script-body and dynamic-call parity

- Pack verification now walks every linearly reachable command in each
  executable script body instead of checking only command zero at jump targets.
  It fails with `non_executable_script_command` at the first unclassified
  fallthrough position. The five source-certified global `startbattle`
  boundaries owned by fishing, Bug Contest, Sweet Scent, Headbutt, and Rock
  Smash are included explicitly; an arbitrary global `startbattle` is not.
- Both source `memcall` forms now compile as executable mutations.
  `wCallerContact + PHONE_CONTACT_SCRIPT2_BANK` and `wPhoneScriptBank` consume
  the resolved `wPhoneCallerScript`, push the ordinary next-command return
  frame, and enter the dynamic target on the live map. Missing targets and any
  other pointer expression fail atomically.
- Incoming calls no longer pre-enter the caller, forge return index 3, or queue
  `RingTwice_StartCall` out of band. They enter `Script_ReceivePhoneCall` at
  command zero, so `reanchormap`, ringing, `memcall`, caller execution, return,
  `waitbutton`, hang-up, and delay reset all flow through the compiled
  interpreter in authored order.
- Global phone-script `callasm` commands are no longer exempt from executable
